# NQ GEX + Order Flow Signal Bot — Full Spec (`docs/SPEC.md`)

> The detailed spec. `CLAUDE.md` (repo root) holds the working rules and says which sections to read for each build step. Section numbers (§) are stable: other files refer to them.

---

## 1. Goal

Build a bot that, at every touch of a key level on **NQ futures**, outputs one number:

**P(target is hit before stop)**

It combines:
- **Dealer positioning** from Skylit (gamma per strike on QQQ, NDXP, SPY, SPX)
- **Chart structure** (VWAP, volume profile, opening-range fib, overnight range) computed from futures data
- **Order flow** (delta, CVD, absorption, exhaustion, footprint imbalance, top-of-book refills) from Databento L1 data (trades and top of book)
- **The owner's morning gameplan** as an optional input (improves probability when present, never required)

Live output (from Phase F) = supervised auto-execution — first on a Tradovate demo account, then on the owner's Tradovate prop accounts (MyFundedFutures) through a **TradersPost webhook bridge** — see §10. The bot picks entry, structural stop, structural target, position size and breakeven itself — see §6.
**Scope: NQ only.** GC behaves differently and will get its own model later. Do not add GC/GLD code paths.

### 1.1 Core principles (apply everywhere)

1. **Structure first, trigger second.** Order-flow triggers (absorption, exhaustion, delta divergence, imbalance) are **only evaluated at or near structure** — price within any level zone of the §5.4 registry plus a small `zone_proximity` buffer, or inside a VAH/VAL premium/discount area (§5.3). This applies at **every** structure type (owner): order-flow entries (styles a and b, §5.5) and the absorption stop (§6.2) are available at nodes, highs/lows, VWAP, POC, LVNs, single prints, dark pools, the golden zone, gameplan zones and VAH/VAL areas alike. Away from structure, order flow is ignored: no trigger events are created, no trigger model is called, no alert fires. The trigger model is trained **only** on in-zone events. Order flow answers "is now the moment?" — never "where?".
2. **Confluence = strength.** The strongest structure points are where several structures stack at one price (a GEX node at the weekly POC, at a VWAP band, at a gameplan zone edge). Every zone records **what is stacked in it** (how many levels, which types, node sizes). There are no hand-set weights: the model learns how much each structure and each combination matters (owner). This is the owner's prior: the model must **test and quantify** it (which pairings matter most, and in which gamma regime), not merely assume it.
3. **Real machine learning, not hardcoded thresholds.** Features are defined from the owner's knowledge; their thresholds are tuned on data; the decision (weights, interactions, final probability) is learned by supervised models from outcomes. There are no hand-written "if X then trade" rules in the decision path. Hard rules exist only for risk limits (§6.1, §10.3), the owner's session/setup windows (§1.2), news blackouts (§9) and his structural definitions (e.g. the 25%-of-King node cutoff, the 1:1 RR floor).
4. **No lookahead, walk-forward only** (§7).
5. **Gameplan is optional input**, never a requirement.

### 1.2 Session scope: New York session only
- The bot **only trades 09:30–16:00 ET** (NY regular session). All backtest events, labels, model training, reports, review cards and live alerts are restricted to entries in this window. `session.flat_by` (settings.yaml) = 15:59 (owner), a model constant: that day's session flat-by = min(15:59, `early_close_et` − 1 min); it sets the label timeout, the tradeable filter and `minutes_to_horizon_end`, so changing it goes through Claude Code with a rebuild. An account's effective flat-by = min(its own `flat_by` (§6.7), that day's session flat-by): an earlier own `flat_by` cuts only that account, live and in the prop simulator, and `entry_end` is not a config key: it is computed per account as `flat_by − min_minutes_before_flat`. `min_minutes_before_flat` is fixed at 14 (owner's rule, not tuned): with flat-by 15:59, the last new entry is at 15:45.
- **Exchange holidays and early closes:** `config/calendar.yaml` lists every CME equity-index holiday and early-close day (`{date, early_close_et}`, e.g. the day after Thanksgiving and Christmas Eve; `early_close_et` = the cash-market close, 13:00 ET, not CME's 13:15 futures close, because the option maps stop at 13:00). Holidays are not trading days (no session, no live run, no `ops.job_lock_window` (08:30–16:15) job lock, not counted in any "N sessions" lookback). On an early-close day the session flat-by = `early_close_et` − 1 minute (an account's earlier own `flat_by` still applies) and `entry_end` = its effective flat-by − `min_minutes_before_flat`; the session is flagged `half_day` (a feature). Backtest, labels, both simulators and live use the same per-day close.
- Levels built from data outside the window — overnight high/low, the 18:00 ET VWAP anchor, daily/weekly profiles, prior-day and prior-week levels — are **full levels and confluence** like any other; only the entries themselves are limited to the 09:30–16:00 window.
- **Setup windows (owner's rule; derived from the two keys below, not a config key):**
  | Time (ET) | What can trade |
  |---|---|
  | 09:30–09:50 | Nothing (the opening range isn't established yet, §5.8) |
  | 09:50–10:00 | **Only the opening-range fib golden-zone setup (§5.8)** — the opening is the most volatile part of the day |
  | 10:00–`entry_end` | All setups: the golden zone (it stays drawn all day, §5.8) plus every other level type in §5.4 |
- Only two keys set the windows: the golden zone opens at 09:30 + `ib_fib_start_minute` (20 → 09:50) and all setups open at 09:30 + `ib_lock_minute` (30 → 10:00, when the opening range locks). Both are owner rules, never tuned (`ib_fib_start_minute` and `ib_lock_minute` live in `features.yaml`). The setup windows and the session entry start are derived from them, not separate keys. `in_golden_zone` = the event's zone is the golden-zone container itself; before 10:00 only those events are tradeable. 
- The window limits what can be the **traded setup**, not what is computed. Every level is tracked from the open (and before, as context), so during 09:50–10:00 the other levels still count as **confluence inside the golden zone** (a golden zone sitting on a QQQ node and the ON low scores higher). No separate backtest of those levels before 10:00 is needed for that.
- Implementation is a filter, not a second system: the event builder writes events for every setup at every time (same code) and tags each with `in_golden_zone` and `minutes_since_open`; the setup window decides which events are **tradeable**. All models (lower models and the scorer) train on tradeable events only (tradeable = inside the setup windows and before `entry_end`, outside news blackouts) (so training matches live use). The structure model filters each event on its touch time; the pair scorer, both simulators and live filter each pair on its own `decision_ts` (inside the setup window for its event type, before `entry_end`, outside news blackouts), so a non-golden touch at 09:58 may trade on a decision at or after 10:00. The out-of-window events are kept and reported separately, so the backtest also shows what the rule gives up (e.g. how non-fib setups did 09:30–10:00) — the owner can loosen the windows later if those numbers are good.
- Skylit history is pulled for RTH (09:30–16:00) only, which keeps the backfill cheap (~26 range calls ≈ 650 credits per session). The 09:30–10:00 maps are still needed: nodes are confluence for the golden zone.

---

## 2. Owner context

- Discretionary order-flow + options-flow trader. Trades NQ (and ES as a gate/reference).
- Uses large-magnitude GEX levels as walls (proven edge), plus volume profile and VWAP.
- Reads order flow in **Deepcharts** (dxFeed subscription — platform-locked, cannot be used by this bot).
- Trades **MyFundedFutures** prop accounts (Rapid EOD 25k, Builder 25k and Pro 50k) on **Tradovate**, copies trades with **TradeSyncer**. Automation stays on these Tradovate accounts via a webhook bridge (§10).
- Risk is set as a **fixed dollar amount per trade, per account** (owner defines in `config/accounts.yaml`); the bot sizes contracts to fit its stop.
- Has a morning gameplan made almost every trading day, finished before 09:30 (Markdown, or Word/PDF), built from gexbot + MenthorQ + chart data; the archive comes from the Google Drive folder "Jeron gameplans"; with entry zones E1–E14, ES gates, targets, scenarios.
- The bot's job is to measure and eventually act on this edge, **not** to copy the owner's trades. Owner knowledge decides **what the bot looks at**; market outcomes decide **how much each thing matters**.

---

## 3. Data sources

### 3.1 Skylit (owner already has API access)
- Base URL `https://api.skylit.ai`. Auth: `Authorization: Bearer $SKYLIT_API_KEY`. Every response carries `X-Credits-Remaining`; failed calls (any 4xx/5xx) are refunded; rate limit 120 requests/min per key. **Read the OpenAPI spec (`/v1/openapi.json`) before writing the client and confirm paths and params.** Done at step 7 (code, 2026-10-05): the specs and the stream, rate-limit and dark-pool pages are saved in `docs/skylit/` (`openapi.json`, `flowseeker-openapi.yaml`, `api-streams-and-limits.md`, `flowseeker-dark-pool.md`). The client reads the key's limit from `/v1/account` (free; `limits.requestsPerMinute`, fallback 120), paces itself with `X-RateLimit-Remaining`/`X-RateLimit-Reset`, runs history calls one at a time (`limits.historicalInFlight`), and retries only 429 (after `Retry-After`, else until `X-RateLimit-Reset`), 500/502/503/504 and network errors, with full-jitter backoff, 5 attempts; 400/401/402/403/404 are never retried.
- **Method (decided): Heatseeker heatmap data, not Atlas.** The bot pulls per-strike gamma maps and converts strikes to NQ/ES prices itself (§5.1). The Atlas API only serves price bars, no GEX. Atlas's NQ/ES orbs are the same exposure data with several chains overlaid on one futures chart; the bot recreates that picture, and Atlas is only the visual reference its levels are checked against (step 8b).
- **Symbols (decided, owner 2026-10): `QQQ, NDXP, SPY, SPX`** — 4 (one history-range call takes up to 5; the NDX root is not used: the owner sees the edge in NDXP, the daily 0DTE Nasdaq-100 chain). Nasdaq chains map onto NQ, S&P chains onto ES. `maxExpirations=1` takes each root's *own* nearest expiry, so step 7 confirms SPX's front expiry is that day's 0DTE, as the owner sees in the app (if the API differs, the SPXW root takes the SPX slot). **The API differs (OpenAPI, 2026-10-05):** its `SPX` root holds only the AM-settled monthlies (no 0DTE); the 0DTE S&P board is the `SPXW` root (the app's Trinity view uses it; Tempest also keys the S&P complex on `SPXW`). So the map keeps the name SPX everywhere in the bot and is requested as `SPXW` (`settings.yaml → skylit_symbols`); step 7 still confirms it with `/v1/symbols` and the stored `expirations`. `NDXP` is right as is (`NDX` = monthlies).
- Endpoints:
  | Endpoint | Use | Cost |
  |---|---|---|
  | `GET /v1/historical/range` | Backfill: every snapshot in a window ≤ 15 min, ≤ 5 symbols per call, **only up to 365 days back**; 1-second snapshots where available, else 1-minute (`meta.resolution`). Params: `metric`, `maxExpirations`, `maxStrikes`, `expirations`. `from` and `to` are both inclusive (so the bot's windows end 1 ms before the next starts; the last ends at 16:00:00). Frames reference strike axes (`axes[{id, strikes, expirations}]`, `frames[{asOf (ms), axis, spot, previousClose, values[]}]`); a new axis appears when the strike window moves | 25 credits/call |
  | `GET /v1/stream` (SSE, v2 format, all 4 symbols on one connection) | **Live maps.** Event-driven: one `snapshot` per symbol on connect, then a new `snapshot` on every board change (Skylit's maps are a 1-second series, so at most about one per second per symbol; `asOf` never goes backwards; a board is never re-sent; `lagged: true` means the bot read too slowly and older boards were skipped — logged). Connections end after 1 hour (`reconnect` event): reconnect at once with `Last-Event-ID`. The server keeps no history, so a resume sends only the **latest** board per symbol; boards published during the gap (normally about a second) are not replayed, and the gap is logged. If the connection shows no event of any kind (boards, velocity, credits or the 15-second `: ping`) for `skylit_stale_sec`, or a reconnect fails, entries are blocked and pending entries removed (§10.3) while the engine keeps reconnecting with backoff; after a gap longer than `skylit_stale_sec` or any `lagged: true` event, the engine pulls the gap from `/v1/historical/range` and applies it by the ingest rule, so velocity, lean and rolling features see the same board history as the backtest; entries resume once the gap is filled, after `feed_recovery_sec` of healthy data and a fresh board (§10.3) (a quiet symbol whose board simply hasn't changed is not stale). There is no polling fallback: polled maps would be staler than anything in training. Stream strikes carry `strike`, `value`, `nodeType`, `distancePct` but **no `velocityPct`**; per-node velocity comes as a separate `velocity` event (checked about every 3 s, not replayed on resume). Neither velocity field nor `nodeType` is a model input (absent from history, §5.2); neither is used. The stream (like `/v1/heatmap`) leaves out interior strikes whose |value| < 50 unless `includeEmpty=true`; range has no such parameter, so the step-7 parity check treats a strike missing on one side as equal when the other side's |value| < 50. A `closed` event ends the stream (reconnect only after `credit_check_failed`; any other reason stops and alerts); 60 s with no event at all counts as a dropped connection (the bot's own `skylit_stale_sec` is stricter) | 1 credit/symbol to open + 1/symbol/min connected (charged in advance per minute) |
  | `GET /v1/gex/levels` | Not used: the bot derives nodes from the per-strike maps itself, the same way in history and live | 1 credit |
  | Tempest `GET /v1/vol/history` | The **only** Tempest data used. One row per symbol per session at the 15:59 ET close, about two years back: OHLC, SVX implied vol per tenor (1d, 9d, 30d, 3m, 6m, plus weekend-adjusted), ATM vol, term slope, 30-day risk reversal / butterfly / skew slope, call/put tilt. Every other Tempest module (cones, sigma, surface, intraday tilt, event vol, `/v1/vol/market`, the stream) has no history covering the window, so it is not used | 1 credit per 10 symbol-weekdays in the requested window, rounded up, minimum 1 (holidays not discounted); columnar answer (`dates[]` + `columns{}`). `/v1/vol/symbols` (free) shows each symbol's last stored session (`historyThrough`), which the step-7 timing check polls. **Pre-open copies only (owner 2026-10-09):** the row Tempest publishes about 16:07 ET is a draft that changes overnight (step 7: 121 and 119 of the 10-07 and 10-08 cells differed by the next morning), so a stored row counts only if pulled at or after `preopen_start` (08:30 ET) of a day after its session; a copy pulled earlier is a draft, kept apart in `repull/tempest_draft/` for the timing checks and never read by history, features, the pre-open job or the live bot (the one Tempest loader skips it, even in `vol/`) |
  | Flowseeker `GET /v1/dark-pool/trades` (backfill and live) and `GET /v1/dark-pool/top-prints/{ticker}` (cross-check only). Reference: Skylit docs → API reference → Dark pool (not in `/v1/openapi.json`) | Off-exchange (FINRA TRF) prints, no side. `trades`: `tickers` (≤ 50), `date` or `date_start`/`date_end` (≤ 31 days per call), `time_start`/`time_end`, `min_notional` (default $1M), `limit` ≤ 5,000, `offset` ≤ 50,000, `order`; fields `timestamp` (**trade** time, UTC), `ticker`, `price`, `size`, `notional`, `venue`, `pctAvgVol`; paged by `meta.nextCursor` (time sort, `order=asc`: no gaps or repeats). `top-prints`: `top_n` (≤ 20), `lookback_days` (≤ 180), `as_of_date` (window `[as_of_date − lookback_days, as_of_date]`) | `trades` 5 credits per page; `top-prints` 3 (docs, 2026-10-05; step 7 measures) |
- Response shape:
  ```json
  {"data":{"symbols":[{"symbol":"QQQ","asOf":"...Z","spot":749.12,
     "strikes":[{"strike":750,"value":1894300.4,"nodeType":"king","velocityPct":12.4}]}]},
   "meta":{"metric":"gamma","resolution":"1m","mode":"live","cached":false}}
  ```
  `nodeType` ∈ king, gatekeeper, pika, barney, significant, normal (live endpoints and `/v1/historical` only). **`/v1/historical/range` frames carry only `asOf`, `spot`, `previousClose` and per-strike `values` (no `nodeType`, no `velocityPct`)**, so the bot computes King, % of King, node types and velocity itself from the values, the same way in backtest and live (§5.2). In history and the stream, `spot` is the board's own spot (`previousClose + priceChange`).
- **Expirations (decided): `maxExpirations=1` in every call** (history and stream): the owner reads the front (0DTE) map, and backtest and live must use the same setting or the nodes won't match. `minutes_to_close` is a feature (0DTE gamma concentrates near spot into the close), and so are `minutes_to_horizon_end` and `in_session_release_today` (§5.2 Flags), so the model sees when its trades will be cut short.
- **Strikes (decided): `maxStrikes` = `skylit_max_strikes` (start 200; `"all"` from step 7, below) in every call** (history and stream). The API default is only 92 strikes around spot, and King and node relevance (≥ 25% of King) depend on which strikes come back, so backtest and live must request the same set. Step 7 checks that each sample day's strongest nodes sit well inside that window (set at step 7 from full-chain calls before the sample days are pulled, §12 step 7) and that the stream accepts the parameter. History is pulled with the full `skylit_max_strikes` window (a superset); if the stream turns out to send fewer strikes, replays trim history to the stream's window, so the step-10 backfill never waits on the stream test. **Step-7 finding (2026-10-06): `skylit_max_strikes` = `"all"` (owner), the whole 0DTE chain on every board.** The API's `maxStrikes` is not "the N strikes nearest spot": with `maxStrikes=200` every symbol came back with only 101–184 strikes (median NDXP 160, QQQ 140, SPX 175, SPY 153; full chains 177–646), and the window moved board by board, lopsided around spot (NDXP 2026-03-18 09:33 ET: spot 24,930, window 24,090–26,300), so it cut real nodes off (a 23%-of-King NDXP strike at the lower edge; strikes of 12% dropped) on 2026-03-18 and 2026-03-26, although the full-chain proposal (163, counted as "nearest spot in the full chain") said 200 was enough. A number can't be predicted in the API's units, so the owner chose the whole chain: the same 25 credits per window (checked on the first `all` calls against `X-Credits-Remaining`), about 1.45× the disk per window (≈ 11 MB per JSON answer). The step-7 gate treats the chain's own end as the real end, not a cut. **If `stream-record` shows the live stream can't send the whole chain** (refuses `maxStrikes=all`, or sends fewer strikes than history), history is trimmed to exactly the stream's window, board by board, in both backtest and live, and King and % of King are computed on that same trimmed window, so the two always match (open item in §13, checked at the first live session).
- **Metric:** gamma only. Vanna is not used (owner).
- **Credits:** plan = 100,000/month, resets on the 1st (ET). The engine tracks `X-Credits-Remaining` and the stream's `credits` events; Discord warns below `skylit_credit_warn` (start 10,000), and the pre-open job blocks the day's entries if the remaining credits can't cover the session, one restart rebuild and the evening checkpoint pull (≈ 2,900: ≈ 1,590 for the stream plus ≈ 650 each for a full-session range rebuild and the evening pull). **Live stream:** 4 symbols × 390 RTH minutes + 4 credits per hourly reconnect ≈ **1,590 credits/session (~33k/month)**. History ≈ 650 credits per RTH session (26 range calls × 25), the same for 1–5 symbols.
- **Backfill (decided): 12 months of RTH, ≈ 163k credits — time-critical.** `/v1/historical/range` only reaches **365 days back**, so the window's oldest day drops out of reach every day. The backfill runs right after the step-7 check, **oldest day first**, before the rest of the build (§12 Phase C). Days already out of reach are dropped and listed (month 1 is just shorter, or merged with month 2 under `min_month1_sessions`, §12 step 4b). The plan's 100k credits cover roughly the oldest 150 sessions; the rest come from a top-up pack (owner's price list, 2026-10: 55,000 credits $50, 175,000 $150, 480,000 $400; purchased credits expire 12 months after purchase and are used after the monthly credits; the ~65k shortfall fits one 175k pack or two 55k packs; auto-reload stays off). **Owner 2026-10-05: a pack is bought on SSD day so the whole backfill runs straight through** (no waiting for the 1st-of-month reset): the step-7 `report` prints the shortfall (backfill estimate − current balance, with a margin) and the cheapest pack(s) from this price list that cover it, and the backfill starts only after the owner's OK and the purchase. **The 365-day reach is a moment, not a date** (`from`: "up to 365 days back"): the oldest day's morning windows leave it while its afternoon is still there, so the backfill pulls a window only if its start is still after now − 365 days + `skylit_reach_margin_min` (start 30), checked just before each call; a window that isn't (or that the API refuses with a 400, or a 404 other than no-data, within a day of that line) is **lost**: logged and reported, never cached as empty, never an error, and the step-7 estimate counts only reachable past windows. The 365 days are counted in UTC (DST-safe). A day already partly stored is never called lost by a later re-plan. The Databento window follows the same rule, keeps every day whose Skylit boards are stored and leaves out days the Skylit backfill recorded as wholly lost (§12 step 8). **Run:** `nqbot skylit plan --approve-credits N` freezes the plan with the owner's approval (the whole estimate, once, after the pack is bought), then `nqbot run skylit_backfill --until 07:30` pulls one date per chunk (Skylit windows and that date's dark-pool prints), Tempest last; the balance is printed before it starts; every call is checked against the approval (counted at max(charged, list price)) and the known balance less `skylit_alongside_reserve_credits` (start 1,000, kept for the step-7 checks that run alongside without the lock; owner 2026-10-06) first, so it never pulls past the approved amount, and if either runs out the job stops cleanly at a chunk boundary (status `credits`) and resumes after a top-up or a new plan; the step-7 report suggests approving the estimate plus `skylit_pack_margin`. A dark-pool date older than that line answered with a 400 is recorded as out of reach (newer, it is an error); a dark-pool date starts only if the budget covers twice the most pages any stored date has needed (a date stopped part-way would throw paid pages away), and the step-7 depth-probe answer is reused, not asked again. A window (or dark-pool date) whose call gave up after the client's retries (5xx, network) is tried again after 1, 5 and 15 minutes in the same run. **Failing windows (owner 2026-10-06, presd_fixes D1):** a window or dark-pool date that still fails, or that Skylit refuses away from the 365-day line, or whose paid answer can't be converted or stored, is recorded in `data/jobs/skylit_backfill/failed.json` with its answer kept in `skylit/failed/`, never pulled again in that pass, and the job carries on; `skylit_max_failures_in_row` (start 5) failures in a row stop it cleanly (status `failures`); every run tries each recorded failure again at its start and at the end of the night (owner 2026-10-06; a run with nothing left to pull does only the start pass), oldest first, so nothing leaves the reach while it waits: a refunded failure gets one more call, a paid one is only converted again from its kept answer, for free (one whose answer wasn't kept is never called again); the same holds for dark-pool dates (every page answered is kept, and a failed 31-day call records all its dates with that one answer, so none is paid twice) and for the Tempest call (check-D, owner 2026-10-06); a paid window, dark-pool date or Tempest range whose kept copy is itself incomplete or corrupt (cut off part-way, out of time order or unreadable, not a conversion bug, which is fixed in code and converted again for free) gets **one** paid re-pull while it is still in reach, within `skylit_dropped_retry_credits_per_night` (shared with the dropped-window tries; else it waits for the next night), listed in `coverage.md` with its credits (owner 2026-10-06); only an attempt that was or may have been paid uses it (a refunded one, e.g. an outage, leaves it still to come), it is never re-sent after a dropped connection, a dark-pool date in progress is finished even if that goes a few pages over the cap (the overage is counted against the cap and listed), and a cut-off 31-day answer stores the dates its kept pages already hold whole (pages come in time order: every date before the last print's date) for free and re-pulls only the rest, one date per call; paid pages are also kept and recorded when a walk is stopped part-way (approval, balance, out of credits), and a 31-day answer kept in `darkpool/range/` that fails to split is recorded for all its dates and never ages out (check-D review, owner 2026-10-06); a failure whose window or date has left the 365-day reach is recorded as **lost** and counted with the lost windows; `skylit_max_failures_in_row` timeouts in a row end a retry pass (not the run), and a plain refusal never does; whatever is still missing is listed in `coverage.md`. Only Skylit's answers are skipped this way; any other error stops the job. A `/v1/historical/range` call whose connection dropped after the request was sent (possibly charged) is re-sent only once at once (`LARGE_CALL_DROP_RETRIES`, presd_fixes D7); a connection never made keeps the normal retries. **Owner 2026-10-06: losing history is worse than a few credits:** a window that still dropped gets up to `skylit_dropped_extra_tries` (3) more tries while it is inside the 365-day reach, in the end-of-night retry pass only, in rounds (oldest first, 1, 5 and 15 minutes before each round), each try sent once (25 credits at most), all within `skylit_dropped_retry_credits_per_night` (start 500; it can go if Skylit confirms a dropped call isn't charged); it is given up only once it leaves the reach or uses up its tries, and coverage.md lists every such try with its credits (walk-forward and sealed months as counts, §7 rule 2b). **Strike-window gate (owner 2026-10-06, decision 3):** `nqbot skylit plan` refuses, with no override, until `skylit_max_strikes` is at least the step-7 strike-window proposal and the latest sample-days pull, made with that value, shows no edge violation; the fix is to raise `skylit_max_strikes` and re-check (`step7 sample-days --raised`, its own job `skylit_sample_days_raise`). Zero tolerance stays (owner 2026-10-06): every board on every sample day; no allowed share. The strike-window check and the report name the board(s) that set the proposal and the window every other board fits in, with the cost of each (the same 25 credits per window, since the range price doesn't depend on `maxStrikes`; the data size differs), so the owner decides if one unusual board drives the window bigger. Cached step-7 pulls are reused; a sample day pulled before `skylit_max_strikes` was raised is replaced by its `strike_window_raise` copy (the old files are moved aside, never deleted) or pulled again under that rule-2 exception. **Aged-out history can never be pulled again:** back it up when step 10 finishes (§11 Storage; once, owner 2026-10-06). RTH only (09:30–16:00); the 09:30–10:00 maps are needed as golden-zone confluence.
- **Older history: not planned.** Beyond 365 days only `/v1/historical` (one instant per call, 5 credits, back to 2023-03-28) is available, which is too coarse for a backfill; older months are also less like today's 0DTE-heavy market.
- **Ingest (the backtest never knows more than live — owner):** live applies each new board at max(its arrival time, τ + `map_latency_sec`), τ = floor(`asOf`) + 1 s (owner 2026-10-08, step 8b: Skylit's content can sit up to the end of the stamped second, §5.1), where `map_latency_sec` = the 99.9th-percentile stream delay (measured at step 7; later over the step-7 and live delays together); a board arriving later than that is applied on arrival and logged (rare by construction). Only boards with `asOf` ≥ 09:30:00 ET of the current session are applied — live, in restart rebuilds and in replays alike (history holds none earlier); boards with `asOf` before 09:30 (including one sent on connect before the open) are logged but not applied; connect and resume boards at or after 09:30 are applied, and a map is absent (features null) until its first session board arrives. History is stored as the raw snapshots, and each board is applied at the exact instant τ + `map_latency_sec` (τ = the board's effective time: floor(`asOf`) + 1 s, or floor(`asOf`) + 60 s for the uncleared 1-minute boards below; one rule for every history era and live; live: max(arrival, that instant)) — the same rule, so backtest and live see each board at the same moment (except the logged late ones, which only make live staler). Step 7 checks parity directly: it records 15 minutes of the stream, then, at least one session later (after the next pre-open), pulls the same 15 minutes from `/v1/historical/range` and compares the boards. The delay also shows whether Skylit revises stored boards after the fact (e.g. with the next day's open interest): if any board differs, stop and ask the owner, since the whole backfill would carry information live never has. If history holds boards the stream never sent, the backtest replays only the cadence the stream delivers (the thinning rule is written down at step 7); if the stream sends boards that history lacks, the gap is reported. The roughly one-second gaps at hourly reconnects are not modelled in the backtest (too small to matter); any live gap longer than `skylit_stale_sec` blocks entries like any stale feed (§10.3). Sessions whose history is only 1-minute resolution (`meta.resolution`) are flagged `map_resolution_1m` (a model input, since their velocity and map staleness differ from live's 1-second boards) and reported; their boards get the effective time τ = floor(`asOf`) + 60 s and are applied at τ + `map_latency_sec` (in case a board stamped at the minute's start holds the minute's end state) unless the spot-lag test on a cached 1-minute day from months 1–6, run before step 11, shows `asOf` is the board's true time (no such day: keep +60 s); the flag and the delay apply per 15-minute call window, since a session can mix resolutions. Live logs every board's arrival − `asOf`; at step 25 and at every monthly retrain, if the live 99.9th percentile exceeds `map_latency_sec`, it is raised to the 99.9th percentile of the step-7 and live delays together, events are rebuilt, the months 7–9 walk-forward is re-run and must still meet the step-16 criteria, and the production fit re-runs (as for `order_latency_sec`, step 21) (a 15-minute step-7 sample can understate delays at the open and around news).
- **Dark pools (decided, owner's method):** the only Flowseeker data used. Dark-pool levels = the `dp_top_n` (3) largest individual prints by notional over the trailing `dp_lookback_days` (45) calendar days, per ticker. **QQQ** prints are full levels on NQ (registry §5.4): each print's price ± `dp_band` (start 0.5 QQQ $, like a QQQ node band) is converted with the current R like a strike (§5.1); identity = ticker + print timestamp + price; the level ends when the print leaves the top list or ages past `dp_lookback_days`; it creates events on its own like any other level, and the model learns whether stacking with other levels adds strength. **SPY** prints map onto ES as cross-market features (an ES reaction at a SPY dark pool supports an NQ reaction), like the S&P nodes. One code path: backtest and live compute the top prints from the raw print list (`/v1/dark-pool/trades`), using **prints through the previous session only** (`timestamp` ≤ 16:00 ET of the previous session, or T−2 if step 7 raises `dp_lag_sessions`), in backtest and live alike: large prints can be reported late, so a print traded today might reach live hours after its trade time and the backtest would see it too early. Sessions whose 45-day lookback isn't fully covered by available history (the start of month 1 if prints older than 365 days can't be pulled) use the days available and are flagged `dp_partial` (a feature); `top-prints` is never used to fill the gap. `/v1/dark-pool/top-prints` (`top_n=3`, `lookback_days=45`) only cross-checks the bot's list (step 7 only). Prints are pulled with 31-day `date_start`/`date_end` calls once step 7 has shown, print by print, that such a call returns exactly the prints of one-`date` calls over the same dates; otherwise, and always for dates within a day of the 365-day reach (an out-of-reach answer can only be told apart per date), one `date` per call (owner 2026-10-05; `dp_tickers`, `min_notional` = `dp_min_notional`, the API's $1M default). A range answer is kept whole in `darkpool/range/` first (never paid for twice), then split by ET trade date and cached per date exactly like a one-date answer; a range never spans a date already cached or settled; an empty trading day inside a range is recorded as "no coverage" and never asked again or cached as empty. A full page (`limit` rows) is followed with `meta.nextCursor` (oldest first), and the pull fails loudly on out-of-order pages, a full page without a cursor or a runaway page count (a silently truncated day would change the top-3 list). History pulled months later can contain late or corrected prints that the live pre-open pull didn't have, so the live pre-open job logs its computed top-3 list and step 24 compares it with the backtest's reconstruction for the same days; any mismatch is reported and the cut-off rule tightened. Step 7 reports history depth (a best-effort probe of the first date month 1's lookback needs, run last: a refused or empty answer is recorded, never cached as an empty day, and never stops the check) and credits per call. Options-flow features (sweeps, Flow Score, tide) are out of v1. **Owner 2026-10-06: not in the first model.** Skylit's Flowseeker history starts **2026-04-01** (`dp_history_start`, features.yaml; QQQ and SPY each checked at step 7), so development months 1–6 have no dark-pool data. The backfill still pulls the prints from that date on, but dark-pool levels and dark-pool features are left out of the first model, events and features alike (`dp_in_model: false`), until enough training months have them; they are added and tested at a later retrain (§7.1 Retraining). Dates before `dp_history_start` answer an empty but charged 200 (not the documented free 404), so step 10 never pulls or caches them; sessions whose lookback reaches before it are `dp_partial`, and the step-7 answers for them were moved to `darkpool/before_history/` (never deleted). The step-7 31-day and top-3 re-check runs on walk-forward dates (`step7 darkpool --walk-forward`; its report keeps match / no-match only, no prices); it matched 6/6 and the 31-day call matched (2026-04-15, 05-01, 05-29), once the cross-check compares timestamps to the second (top-prints cuts them) and ignores order within an exact notional tie. **Owner 2026-10-07: in case Skylit's dark-pool history is a rolling window that loses a day each day, the step-10 backfill pulls every dark-pool date from `dp_history_start` first, oldest first, before the range days** (chunks `dp_<date>`; a few minutes). `nqbot step7 dp-start-probe` (5 credits; nothing cached, counts only) checks that `dp_history_start` still has prints for QQQ and SPY, and if not steps forward one trading day at a time to the new start; first run 2026-10-10. If the start moves, it is recorded here and in §13 and `dp_history_start` is updated.
- **Terms:** personal use only — the bot and its alerts/data serve the owner alone and are never shared. Skylit confirmed (2026-10) that trading the owner's own prop accounts counts as personal use.

### 3.2 Databento — Standard plan, $199/month (from step 8; all market data, history and live)
- Dataset `GLBX.MDP3`, package `databento`. `Historical` and `Live` clients share record formats, so backtest and live run the same code. The only market-data source: the backtest uses the same 1-second bars, trades and top-of-book as live from the first backtest on.
- Schemas used in v1: `trades` (exchange aggressor `side`: B = buy, A = sell, N = none — no bid/ask inference needed), `mbp-1` (NQ only, RTH 09:25–16:05 ET only, on early-close days to `early_close_et` + 5 min: used for fills, slippage and top-of-book order-flow features — refills, spread, L1 imbalance), `ohlcv-1s`. No L2/L3 depth (`mbp-10`, `mbo`): L1 only. 1-minute and 5-minute bars are built from trades.
- Standard plan: live CME data; 16+ years of OHLCV including `ohlcv-1s`; last 12 months of L1 history (`trades`, `mbp-1`) and live L1. $125 signup credit; estimate pulls with `metadata.get_cost`. Personal use, up to 2 devices. The included L1 window is the last 12 months *at the time of the pull*, so step 8 pulls the whole window at once. **Warm-up:** `trades` + `ohlcv-1s` (no `mbp-1`) for the `warmup_sessions` sessions right before the first backfill day, where `warmup_sessions` = max(20, the longest session lookback in `features.yaml`: `atr_period`, `rv_lookback_sessions`, `big_trade_lookback_sessions`, `ib_avg_sessions`, `retest_lookback_sessions`) + 1 (owner 2026-10-05: N close-to-close returns need N + 1 closes, so `rv_nq` and the ATR are complete on the first window session); `warmup_sessions` is derived, not a config key; the warm-up `trades` are bought pay-as-you-go after the owner confirms the estimate (`ohlcv-1s` is included in the plan). Window days Skylit could no longer serve are not bought. **Broken downloads (presd_fixes issue 4):** an included request whose stream breaks (network error, Databento 5xx, or a mid-stream failure, which databento 0.87 raises as a plain `BentoError`; never a 4xx) is retried after 30 s, 2 min and 5 min, then the run stops cleanly (status `api_error`) and the day re-runs next time; a broken pay-as-you-go download is never retried: it is recorded in `data/jobs/databento_backfill/payg_failed.jsonl`, the rest of the day carries on, and it runs again only after the owner checks the Databento billing page and approves a re-plan with `nqbot databento plan --retry-failed-payg` (Databento bills a streamed request for the data actually sent and bills every re-stream again, so a retry pays for the delivered part twice; Databento docs, "Streaming vs. batch download", checked 2026-10-05). **Dataset condition (owner 2026-10-09):** every Databento pull records the free `metadata.get_dataset_condition` for the UTC dates its days touch (`raw/databento/condition/conditions.json`, the latest answer and every change) and flags any date that isn't `available` in its log and report; `nqbot databento condition` re-checks every date of the plan (warm-up included), maps each non-available date to the ET sessions it touches and measures their completeness from the stored files (step-8 report `<reports_dir>/step8/step8_report.md`; completeness only, §7 rule 2b); **How such days are used (owner 2026-10-09, §13 Q8-degraded), fixed from completeness only before any outcome on them was looked at and applied by code to every month alike:** a non-available UTC date maps to the ETH and RTH parts of the CME days it overlaps (a weekend date to none); each part is measured per series (NQ and ES `trades`, NQ `mbp-1` in RTH) against the same part on the `databento_damage_baseline_sessions` (20) nearest sessions whose dates are all available; a part is damaged if its empty minutes exceed the normal maximum by more than `databento_damage_excess_minutes` (5) or a gap is longer than both the normal maximum gap and `databento_damage_gap_sec` (120 s); its damaged minutes are the minutes inside such gaps plus its excess empty minutes. RTH with ≤ `databento_damage_session_minutes` (30) damaged minutes (union over the series): only the events (training rows, labels, backtest and simulator trades) whose span from the touch to the end of the label horizon comes within `databento_damage_window_pad_min` (15) of a damaged minute are dropped, and cumulative session features after the damage use the stored data as it is; RTH above 30: the whole session is dropped, and the levels it hands to the next session (prior-day high/low, value area, POC) are left empty there. ETH-only damage keeps the RTH events; above 30 damaged minutes the session's overnight-derived levels and features (ON high/low, overnight profile) are left empty. Applied to the step-8 window (2026-10-09): no session is dropped; 2025-11-28's overnight levels and features are empty. **Upgraded days (owner 2026-10-09, §13 Q8-degraded-2; a CLAUDE.md rule-2 re-pull exception):** a date later upgraded to `available` is flagged (`upgrades.jsonl`) and never re-pulled by itself; it may be re-pulled once, only after the owner's OK for that day, with `metadata.get_cost` printed first (a priced request needs `--approve-usd` like any paid pull); the old files are moved to `repull/degraded/` (kept, never deleted), the affected events are rebuilt (features version, §5.0) and the rule above is re-applied with the new condition; for a sealed or test month the swap is mechanical (completeness only, no outcome looked at). The top-ups and the evening archive (steps 18, 22, 27, 29) use the same check when they are built. **Cost guard (issue 5):** `databento plan` first prices one hour (10:00–11:00 ET) of the last window session's NQ contract in `ohlcv-1s` and `trades`; if either isn't $0 it refuses ("subscription not active, or get_cost doesn't show included data as $0") and writes nothing; it also refuses if `get_cost` prices more than half (`INCLUDED_PRICED_MAX_SHARE`, a fixed method constant) of the requests the 12-month date rule calls included. The only pay-as-you-go items at step 8 are the warm-up and any Skylit-pulled day that has left Databento's included window by the time of the pull. Every order-flow feature is built from `trades` and `mbp-1`, in backtest and live alike.
- **Front month:** at each session start, resolve continuous `NQ.v.0` (volume roll; works for historical and live) to the raw contract (e.g. `NQZ6`), log it, and use that one contract all session for data, features, Skylit mapping and the TradersPost tickers (`NQZ2026`, `MNQZ2026`). Never mix contract months within a session. A calendar holiday with a Globex session (not a session) keeps the previous session's contract (owner 2026-10-05), so the roll, its spread and the shift of every carried level (the holiday's trades included) land on the next session. **Roll days:** on the first session of a new front contract, every level and statistic carried from earlier sessions (PDH/PDL, prior RTH and week highs/lows, the weekly profile, unfilled single prints, retested extremes, the ATR and realized-vol closes, the expected-move prior close) is shifted by the spread between the two contracts at the previous trading day's close (new minus old contract, last 1-second close at or before 17:00 ET; the front contract is resolved per CME trading day starting at 18:00 ET, so the overnight, VWAP and profiles of the roll day are on the new contract) (owner: Deepcharts back-adjusts its history the same way), so the old levels line up with the new contract without a gap; step 8 and the pre-open job pull both contracts' `ohlcv-1s` for the session before each switch to measure it. Step 12 includes a roll-week day.
- **Licensing:** Databento confirmed (2026-10) that the owner is a non-professional individual trader. Keep it that way: personal use only.

### 3.3 ES
- ES is pulled alongside NQ (Databento) for the S&P-chain levels, Trinity alignment, gameplan ES gates and NQ/ES divergence.

### 3.4 Gameplans (optional)
- One file per day in `data/raw/gameplans/`, `YYYY-MM-DD.md` (preferred, exported from Word; text used as-is) or `.pdf` (sent to the API whole); `.docx` (and Google Docs exported as `.docx`) are converted to Markdown first, and step 9 lists any it can't convert. Archived files may have any name (e.g. `09_15 Gameplan.md`, `NFP Gameplan.md`): step 9 reads the date from the file's content, lists file → date for the owner to confirm, and saves a copy as `YYYY-MM-DD.md`. Some gameplans include an "Observed" section (overnight and 08:30 recap) and version labels (e.g. "RTH v2"): only the latest version timestamped before 09:30 is used, and anything describing RTH price action is dropped. Gameplans carry no images (owner, 2026-10-05): image links and embedded pictures are ignored.
- **Parser = Claude API, not regex** (free-form prose and tables). `gameplan/parser.py` sends the text to the Claude API with the §5.6 JSON schema (structured output), validates it (zones ordered, stops beyond zones, targets in the trade direction and in order away from the zone, each scenario set summing to 1), flags uncertain fields, and writes `data/processed/gameplans/YYYY-MM-DD.json`. Cents per file; needs `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`. Model, effort, `max_tokens` and the list prices used for the printed estimate are `gameplan_model` / `gameplan_effort` / `gameplan_max_tokens` / `gameplan_usd_per_mtok` (settings.yaml). Every paid answer is saved by the file's sha256 before any check, so a file is never sent again unless re-parsed on purpose (re-dating with `--date`, conflicts and copies are rebuilt from the saved answer); no fallback model and no automatic retry, so the printed worst case holds. `nqbot gameplan <file>` prints its estimate and proceeds when the worst case is within `gameplan_max_usd_per_file` ($1.00, owner 2026-10-05; above it, `--approve-usd`), `nqbot gameplan-batch` needs `--approve-usd` covering its worst case and writes the step-9 report (file → date list, gameplan days per month with their ledger month, unparsed files, flags). Both commands use their own lock (`processed/gameplans/.gameplan.lock`, cleared with `nqbot unlock --gameplan`), not the SSD job locks, so a morning parse never waits for an overnight job. `--replace` moves the files of the date's previous gameplan to `raw/gameplans/replaced/` (never deleted).
- **The parser records, never chooses (owner 2026-10-07, parser version 2):** what the gameplan says is stored as written; nothing is inferred or guessed. HVL and HVL0 go in separate fields whenever given; the regime line only when the gameplan labels one; a target written as a range is one target with a low and a high (never split); a target written as a name ("home", "the platform") gets a price only if the same gameplan gives that name one, else its price is empty and its words are kept; each invalidation keeps its price, direction, instrument and timeframe as written (`1m_close`, `settled_bar`, `acceptance` with its minutes, `not_stated`, `other`; "settled bar" is never assumed to mean 1 minute); every ES gate is its own item with its words (above = above, over, holding or reclaiming X; below = below, under, losing, rejecting or failing X; any other wording `unclear`, owner 2026-10-07); every probability set is kept, labelled `ny_am`, `session` or `unspecified`, and each must sum to 1 on its own; a value written two ways inside one gameplan takes the version marked final, and with none marked final is left empty and flagged. Single-price zones stay [p, p]. Stops and invalidations are kept exactly as written, even inside the zone (flags only).
- **Parser version 3 (owner 2026-10-07):** every conditional "acceptance through / breakout / breakdown" entry is stored on every gameplan the way 10-06 writes them: its own entry with the zone empty, the NQ trigger level (`trigger.nq_price`) and the acceptance condition kept, and its stop and targets (08-13's breakout BUY over 30,011 and breakdown SELL to 29,635 → 29,585/600 were missed by v2). Anything §5.6 has no field for (VIX and 10Y gates, "take the second test, not the first", no-trade gaps, size) is kept word for word in `other_conditions` on the entry or the gameplan. The model labels each of its own notes info or review by the rule below.
- **Parser version 4 (owner 2026-10-07): the glossary.** `docs/gameplan_glossary.md` (what the gameplan's words mean and where each goes; built from the owner's cheat sheet `docs/gameplan_terminology.md`, which is reference only and never sent) is part of the parser prompt, word for word, for archived and live gameplans alike; where it and the prompt's own rules differ, the glossary wins. Its checksum is pinned next to `PARSER_VERSION`, so editing it means a new parser version and a re-parse. From it: a slash pair ("30,133/30,157") is two targets, nearer first, never a range; "the HVL pair at X" fills both `hvl` and `hvl0`; each key level's Reversion branch is the zone's entry and its Breakout branch a conditional entry; "Stop X by touch" keeps "by touch" in the trigger note; PIVOT, INVALID (map-wide), NO TRADE rows, VIX/10Y gates and one-liners go to `other_conditions`; a scenario card never creates an entry and its conditions stay on it. A model note gets a third kind, `two_versions` (written two ways, none FINAL: the value stays empty). **Review checks from the glossary:** a conditional entry that has a zone; a filled value where a note says two versions existed and none was FINAL; a number too small for NQ in `hvl`/`hvl0`/`regime_line` (a VIX line); the same value in two of `hvl`, `hvl0`, `regime_line` (except `hvl` = `hvl0` when the text says "the HVL pair").
- **Versions after the glossary (owner 2026-10-07):** the archive (30 gameplans, 2026-08-12 … 2026-10-06) is parsed with parser v4 and is re-parsed only once more, before the step-20 gameplan check, with the then-current live parser version (cost printed, owner's OK first; owner 2026-10-07), so archive and live gameplans are read the same way; until then later glossary changes ("HVL/HVL0 X", "HVL & HVL0 X", "HVL = HVL0 X" count as stating both, like "the HVL pair"; break vs retest, below) make v6 the version for live gameplans, and the archive's differences are covered by owner overrides. **Break vs retest (glossary §7):** an entry that trades the break itself (acceptance, breakout, breakdown, lost, reclaimed, initiation) has an empty zone and its level in `trigger.nq_price`; an entry that trades the retest of a written zone after a break (failed reclaim, held retest, audit) keeps that zone, trigger `failed_audit` / `audited_hold`, the break as its precondition in the trigger note (09-10 E5). New check rules reach stored gameplans without a re-parse: `nqbot gameplan-batch` re-runs the checks (`CHECKS_VERSION`) on each stored gameplan from its own saved answer, free.
- **Owner overrides (owner 2026-10-07):** the owner's choices (a value picked or left empty, a note relabelled, a gameplan confirmed) live in `processed/gameplans/overrides/<date>.json`, apart from the parsed file, and are applied on top by the loader (`GameplanStore.load_effective`): the step-9 report and step 11 read the effective gameplan, so a re-parse never loses them. An override finds its entry by the gameplan's own label when it has one (E1, E5b…), otherwise by side plus the level or zone the owner approved (plus the model's name only when that is not unique), because the model names unlabelled entries afresh on each parse; one that matches no single entry becomes a review flag, never dropped. A review flag on a field the owner set is settled ("resolved by owner override", info). `owner_confirmed` marks a gameplan the owner accepted as stored; step 11 uses gameplans with `status: ok` or `owner_confirmed`.
- **Two kinds of flag (owner 2026-10-07):** an **info** flag (`review: false`) says something the gameplan doesn't give (no stop, no targets, no acceptance minutes, a zone or a named target with no NQ price, a scenario without a probability) or a value kept by a rule the owner set (a stop or invalidation inside its zone kept as written; a contradiction with no version marked final left empty). It never needs the owner's confirmation. The model labels each of its own `uncertain` notes the same way (info: it only says something is missing, or it is covered by an owner rule: a date or year, since the owner confirms every date; a stop or invalidation kept as written; the version marked final; a contradiction left empty). Every other flag is a **review** flag. A gameplan is `status: ok` with info flags only and `flagged` with any review flag, so a morning gameplan is never held up before 09:30 by a "no stop" flag. The step-9 report lists review flags in full and counts info flags per gameplan. When the flag rules change (`CHECKS_VERSION`), stored gameplans are rebuilt from their saved answers, free. On the wire the model writes a missing value as `[]` or `""` and one value as `[v]` (`date` is the only nullable field): the API refused the first v2 schema, whose ~15 nullable fields made the compiled grammar too large (2026-10-07; at most 16 union-type fields per request), and the parser converts the answer to the stored §5.6 shape before any check. Because the parser no longer infers a year the gameplan doesn't state, such files need `--date`; a `--date` on a parsed file (the owner confirming a date) is rebuilt from the saved answer for free, as is a gameplan stored by an older parser version once the current version's answer is saved.
- **No hindsight:** archived gameplans must contain only what was written before 09:30; remove any after-the-fact notes, or the backtest overrates the gameplan.
- Daily use: upload on the Control Panel's Gameplan page (§9b) before 09:30; `nqbot gameplan path/to/file` also works. **Until those exist (step 14):** from 2026-10-05 the owner saves each morning's gameplan, as finished before 09:30 and not edited afterwards, to the "Jeron gameplans" Google Drive folder with the date in the file name; step 9 and every top-up (steps 18, 20, 27) copy new files into `data/raw/gameplans/`. Every gameplan is written before 09:30 (owner), so no timing check is made on the files.

### 3.5 Execution bridge: TradersPost (Phase F)
- The bot POSTs JSON to a TradersPost webhook (`https://webhooks.traderspost.io/trading/webhook/<ID>/<PASSWORD>`, one per TradersPost connection, stored in `.env` and referenced per account in `accounts.yaml`); TradersPost places the order on the linked **Tradovate** account. Custom-code senders are officially supported; Tradovate's own API isn't available for prop accounts.
- Signal fields:
  - `ticker`; `action` ∈ buy, sell, exit, **breakeven**. The bot **never sends `cancel`** (TradersPost's `cancel` removes all open orders for the ticker, and a fill racing the cancel could leave a position without its bracket) nor `reverse`, `add`, `resize`. A pending entry is removed with `exit` on its own ticker, which also flattens it if it filled in the meantime. Every `exit` carries the field `"cancel": true` (a field on the exit signal, not the `cancel` action), so it also removes that ticker's open orders whatever the strategy subscription's default is.
  - `orderType` ∈ limit, stop_limit for entries; `stop` only on `breakeven` signals (TradersPost requires it, test 2); a "marketable limit" is a limit priced through the market; plain market entries are never sent (an `exit` closes at market)); `limitPrice`/`stopPrice`; `timeInForce: "day"` on every entry (test 4 records that a resting entry left by a dead bot expires at the CME close, and the time-in-force of the attached stop and target, i.e. whether a bracket left at the broker survives past the session)
  - `quantity` + `quantityType: fixed_quantity` — the bot always sends the size it computed (§6.1). TradersPost honours this only when the strategy subscription has **Allow signal override** checked; step 21 sets it (and no default stop/target, no order-type override, adding off), and the daily checklist confirms the subscription settings are unchanged.
  - `stopLoss`: {`type`: stop, `stopPrice`}; `takeProfit`: {`limitPrice`}
  - `action: "breakeven"` moves the stop to the average entry price (optional `breakevenOffset`) — used for §6.4
  - `extras`: free-form JSON; always carries the `event_id` and, on entries, the unique `order_id` (§10.3)
- Limits: 60 requests/min, 500/hour — far above 1–3 calls per leg (2 legs for a mixed NQ + MNQ trade).
- The Tradovate connection has **no market data**: every entry signal carries absolute `limitPrice`/`stopPrice`, `stopLoss.stopPrice` and `takeProfit.limitPrice` (never relative values); `exit` and `breakeven` carry none. TradersPost also can't show open P&L.
- **Ticker:** the specific contract with a 4-digit year (`MNQZ2026`, `NQZ2026`), never a continuous symbol; TradersPost doesn't handle rolls; on the first session of a new contract, flat-by, `!flat` and kill also send `exit` to the previous contract's tickers, and exits for a carried account go to its carried tickers (§9).
- **Brackets:** Tradovate supports a stop and target attached to the entry. Entry order type follows the entry style (§5.5b); no plain market entries. Exits are absolute structural prices.
- Plan: Starter (~$49/month) covers one live account; each extra live account is +$10/month.
- **Fills:** there are no fill callbacks. The bot infers fills from its own data (a pending entry is assumed filled once price trades through it within the cap; a limit that price only **touched** is "maybe filled" (except on accounts with `sim_fills: true`, where a touched resting limit, entry or target, counts as filled, step 21): the bot treats the account as possibly in a position — no new entries, owner alerted — until it clears). Exits too: a take-profit counts as filled only when price trades through it; a touch-only target is "maybe filled" (no new entries; the bot treats the position as still open). A "maybe filled" state clears by itself once price has traded through that bracket's stop or target (the OCO bracket would have closed any fill) and any resting entry has been removed (`exit`); the owner can clear it sooner with `!resolve`. Every pending-entry removal reason (§5.5b) also sends `exit` for a maybe-filled entry leg (labels count a touch-only limit as unfilled), and once an `exit` sent after that entry's own reply is acknowledged on the leg's ticker, the leg becomes "flat-unconfirmed" (§10.3 Account states; an acknowledgement only proves TradersPost received the exit; the account is flat only when every leg is): the bot books the worse of "not filled" and "filled at the limit, closed at the exit's market price" (flagged for reconciliation), and it is not carried into the next session; the bracket rule above applies only before such an exit. The policy simulator (§7 rule 3b) blocks new entries on that account for the same span. While an account is "maybe filled" or "unknown", every cap uses the worst case (loss caps and lives assume the leg's full stop loss; the profit cap assumes its full target). When the state clears by itself, the bot books the outcome implied by the bracket price that traded, flags the trade for reconciliation, and the owner's next balance confirmation overrides it. After `!resolve … position` the bot tracks the sent bracket and clears the same way. The broker is the source of truth, and Live Status shows "pending", "maybe filled" or "assumed filled". Step 21 checks whether TradersPost has a trade log/API the bot can poll to confirm fills.
- **Nine tests on a Tradovate demo account before any prop account (step 21):**
  1. Stop and target sent with the entry are both placed immediately and cancel each other (OCO) when one fills, and the filled quantity on each leg equals the signal's `quantity`.
  2. `breakeven` works on Tradovate futures, and record the unit of `breakevenOffset` (ticks, points or dollars) so each leg's offset covers its own round-trip costs (each leg's offset = ceil(that leg's `commission_per_*_rt` ÷ its tick value) + `be_offset_ticks`, converted to `breakevenOffset`'s unit; labels use the MNQ value at default costs). If not, breakeven is disabled (`breakeven_enabled: false`, §6.4); it is never emulated with an `exit` or a broker auto-breakeven setting.
  3. `exit` (with `cancel: true`) flattens and cancels the leftover bracket orders, and also removes a resting unfilled entry; run it once with the subscription's order-cancel setting off to prove the field works on its own. Also record what the webhook reply shows when the broker rejects an `exit` (§10.3 Account states). Send `exit` on both tickers while flat and record the reply: a "no position" reply counts as success (flat-by and `!flat` send it to every ticker); for a leg the bot believed open it also triggers fill re-inference: book the worse of "not filled" and the bracket outcome, flagged for reconciliation.
  4. A stop-limit entry with brackets (`timeInForce: "day"`) is accepted; leave one resting and record that it expires at the CME close. It rests until triggered, and gets its brackets on fill; `exit` removes it while unfilled, and after a partial fill flattens the filled part and removes the rest and the bracket. While partially filled, the attached stop and target quantity must equal the filled quantity: let the stop trigger on a partially filled entry and confirm the account ends flat; if not, stop and ask the owner before step 24. If the stop-limit can't rest with brackets: send no resting order; watch price and send one marketable limit with brackets the moment the stop level trades (never both). If this fallback is used, every label and simulator replaces the §5.5b stop-limit rule with: at the first trade at or through `stopPrice` after decision + `order_latency_sec`, a marketable limit (`max_entry_slip_ticks` beyond `stopPrice`) is sent, arrives `order_latency_sec` later and fills by the marketable-limit rule; labels are rebuilt and the step-16 criteria re-checked before step 24.
  5. Where stop and target land: at the exact prices sent, or at the same distance from the *actual* fill (shifted by entry slippage, ≤ `max_entry_slip_ticks`, which sizing already covers). Log the real levels either way. If they shift, fill inference (above) counts a target or stop as filled only once price trades through it ± `max_entry_slip_ticks`; anything in between is "maybe filled".
  6. No doubled positions: send the same entry twice, a second entry while in a position, and an opposite-direction entry while in a position; confirm the second copy, the added entry and the opposite entry are all ignored or rejected (none reverses or adds). Turn on TradersPost's block-adding setting and set the account's max position in Tradovate as a backstop.
  7. Mixed trade: an NQ and an MNQ leg sent together both fill with their own brackets; `breakeven` / `exit` act on both; an `exit` for one unfilled leg's ticker leaves the other leg's position and bracket in place. If not, set `allow_mixed: false`.
  8. `exit` sent at the moment a resting entry fills (race): afterwards the account is flat with no orphan stop or target orders. If orphan orders remain, a second `exit` on that ticker must clear them; otherwise the account goes to "unknown".
  9. **TradeSyncer copying** (a second Tradovate demo account as a TradeSyncer follower of the demo leader): on the follower, (a) the stop and target are present right after the entry fills; (b) an unfilled stop-limit entry rests and is removed by `exit`; (c) `breakeven` moves its stop; (d) a mixed trade copies both legs; (e) `exit` flattens it and removes leftover orders; (f) the size multiplier is 1 (followers are identical accounts); (g) `exit` sent while the leader is already flat leaves a follower's position untouched — record it (the follower check in §10.1 covers it); (h) measure the 95th-percentile leader → follower copy lag (`tradesyncer_copy_lag_sec`). If (a) fails, TradeSyncer can't be used with the bot (each account would need its own TradersPost connection). Repeated on the first prop follower before it copies live trades (step 28).
  Also record what the webhook returns when Tradovate **rejects** an order (send one it must reject), and whether the account's max-position setting is user-editable on the prop accounts (if not, the backstop is TradersPost's strategy-level max quantity and block-adding).
- Measure latency: live logs, per order, the `ts_event` of the record that triggered the decision, the receive time, the send time and the order's working time (TradersPost's log or Tradovate's Orders report). Step 21 measures end-to-end latency = working time − triggering `ts_event` (receive lag + compute + send-to-working) on the demo and sets `order_latency_sec` (§5.5b) to its 95th percentile; the step-24 orders/fills importer re-measures it and computes send-to-fill latency.
- Examples:
  ```json
  {"ticker":"MNQZ2026","action":"sell","orderType":"limit","limitPrice":30706.00,
   "quantity":10,"quantityType":"fixed_quantity",
   "stopLoss":{"type":"stop","stopPrice":30721.00},
   "takeProfit":{"limitPrice":30668.50},
   "extras":{"event_id":"20251119T155200Z-z17","order_id":"20251119T155200Z-z17-d1-mffur1-MNQ"}}
  ```
  (10 MNQ = floor($333 risk ÷ ($30 stop + $1.90 commission + $0.50 exit slippage per micro = $32.40)); a passive limit, so no entry slip; a real trade of this size would be 1 NQ, §6.1.) Stop-limit short (triggers at 30,698, fills no worse than 30,697): 8 MNQ = floor($333 ÷ ((17 pts + 4 ticks) × $2 + $1.90 + $0.50)), sized for the worst fill (§6.1):
  ```json
  {"ticker":"MNQZ2026","action":"sell","orderType":"stop_limit","stopPrice":30698.00,"limitPrice":30697.00,
   "quantity":8,"quantityType":"fixed_quantity",
   "stopLoss":{"type":"stop","stopPrice":30715.00},"takeProfit":{"limitPrice":30650.00},
   "extras":{"event_id":"20251119T155300Z-z17","order_id":"20251119T155300Z-z17-c1-mffur1-MNQ"}}
  ```
  Breakeven `{"ticker":"MNQZ2026","action":"breakeven","orderType":"stop","breakevenOffset":1}` (TradersPost requires a stop order type for breakeven; test 2 confirms) · Flatten, or remove an unfilled entry (time-to-live expired or setup invalidated) `{"ticker":"MNQZ2026","action":"exit","cancel":true}`.

---

## 4. Architecture

```
Skylit (live / history)─┐
Databento (live / hist) ─┼─► Feature engine (incremental) ─► Event detector ─► Models ─► Decision ─► Discord / bridge
Gameplan JSON (optional)─┘                                     (level touch)                              │
                                                                                                    Logger (every event)
```

Five layers:
1. **Structure** — where price might react: every level type in the registry (§5.4), plus gamma regime.
2. **Setup** — is it a good level: node strength, freshness, rug/reverse rug, pika cloud, Trinity alignment, velocity lean, structure confluence, day type.
3. **Gameplan** — inside a zone? scenario agrees? (blank when no gameplan)
4. **Trigger** — when exactly: entry styles (a)–(d) (§5.5): absorption, another order-flow trigger, audit or sweep reclaim at the zone edge, or plain at the touch.
5. **Decision** — the pair scorer's EV (≥ `min_ev_r`) and P (≥ `min_target_prob`), §6.5b → order.

Stacked models (details §7.1):
| Model | Unit | Label | Timescale |
|---|---|---|---|
| Structure | each level-zone touch | zone held (≥ `hold_reaction_pts`) vs broke (the zone crossed, i.e. every active member crossed) | minutes |
| Trigger | order-flow event inside a structure zone or VAH/VAL area | +`trig_fav_ticks` in the trade direction before −`trig_adv_ticks` within `trig_window_sec` | seconds |
| Day type | session, as it unfolds | range vs trend day | session |
| **Pair scorer (the combiner)** | pair = event × entry style (and firing) × stop candidate × target candidate | target first / stop first / timeout (+ breakeven exit when breakeven is on, §6.6) (+ time-to-target) | per trade |

The pair scorer takes the lower models' out-of-fold predictions as inputs and trains on later months than they do. Its output is the probability the bot acts on.

---

## 5. Definitions (seed of the feature spec)

### 5.0 Where the knowledge comes from, and how it gets in

The bot only knows a concept if it is turned into a measurable feature. Knowledge enters through three folders and one spec file:

| Source | Where it goes | Format |
|---|---|---|
| Skylit academy docs (core concepts, Patternpedia, guides, pika clouds, rugs, etc.) | `docs/skylit/` — one file per page/topic, e.g. `core-concepts.md`, `pattern-rug-setup.md` | Markdown or PDF, one file per topic (text only; no screenshots, owner) |
| Owner's own reads and rules (audits, how he weighs SPY/SPX/QQQ, what "strong enough" means) | `docs/owner_notes.md` | Plain notes (owner's answers, 2026-10). No dated trade examples (owner): the step-12 visual check and the step-14 review cards play that role |
| Gameplans | `data/raw/gameplans/` | One file per day, `YYYY-MM-DD.md` (preferred, exported from Word) or `.pdf`, or `.docx` (converted to Markdown, §3.4) |

How Claude Code uses them:
1. Reads every file in `docs/skylit/` and `docs/owner_notes.md` (the Skylit Docs MCP can also be used to look things up).
2. Writes `docs/feature_spec.md`: for each concept → plain-English meaning, the exact measurable definition, tunable parameters with starting values, which doc file it came from, and open questions where docs are vague or conflict.
3. Owner corrects the spec. **When Skylit docs and owner notes conflict, owner notes win** (it's his edge).
4. The approved spec becomes code in `src/nqbot/features/` and `src/nqbot/detectors/`, with parameters in `config/features.yaml`.
5. Detectors are checked against what the owner sees through the step-12 visual check and the step-14 review cards (development months 1–6), not through dated examples; a card where the owner says the detector got it wrong becomes a regression test.
6. New docs later → add the file → Claude Code updates the spec → owner approves → features version bumps → events rebuilt → models retrained.

Docs are used only while building. They are never read at runtime, so they add zero latency.

Every concept below becomes a function with **named, tunable parameters** in `config/features.yaml`. The backtest tunes parameters; never hardcode magic numbers.

### 5.1 Mapping strikes to NQ/ES (precision rules — these levels drive entries, stops and targets)
- **Formula:** `futures_level = strike × R`, `R = F ÷ S` (F = futures price, S = the option underlying's price at the same second). QQQ, NDXP → NQ; SPY, SPX → ES. Bands convert the same way (`band × R`). A ratio, not a points offset, because index futures are priced proportionally to the index (`F = S·e^((r−q)T)`) and ETFs track their index proportionally; an additive offset is off by `distance × basis%` (~1–2 NQ pts for a node 300 pts away).
- **How it runs (identical in backtest and live):** on each Skylit update, the board's own ratio is `R = F ÷ S`, with S = the board's own spot (the `spot` field in history and stream frames) and F = the futures price at that board's content time c (below). The ratio in force for map m is its estimator over those board ratios (owner 2026-10-08, step 8b): QQQ and SPY the **trailing 60-s median** (`ratio_window_sec`), SPX **per board** with the outlier rule below, NDXP **pending** (§13 Q8b-1: its history `spot` is an NQ futures price, so its R comes from basis + carry or QQQ × k, chosen leave-one-day-out across the three recordings (owner 2026-10-08): candidates basis + carry and QQQ × k with N ∈ {5, 10, 20}; hold one day out, pick the candidate with the lowest p95 on the other two, score it on the held-out day, rotate; each candidate's held-out p95 and median are reported, the method and N with the best held-out p95 win (every-board scoring stays the main method, owner 2026-10-11); report only, the same candidates' p95 at relevant-node crossings (F2.4 on the NDXP board in force, the gate's crossing rule) is shown next to it, and the proposed NDXP `stop_buffer_floor` is the larger of the winner's every-board held-out p95 and its crossing p95, so stops are never tighter than crossings show; the owner decides on it (owner 2026-10-11; `nqbot step8b ndxp-select`)). Trailing only, never centred: over the applied boards whose content time lies in (c − window, c], reset at the session's first applied board (after `ratio_open_skip_sec` for SPX/NDXP), at a contract change (only ever between sessions) and after a board gap longer than `ratio_gap_reset_sec` (10 s); after a reset the median uses the boards since it (the first board alone at first, no minimum count). The ETF's last-trade `spot` is noisy by a few cents, which the median removes (findings R2/R8: QQQ level error p95 0.55 NQ, SPY 0.11 ES). Every strike and band is converted once with the R in force, then every NQ/ES tick is compared with those fixed levels until the next update. The bot never needs QQQ/SPY/SPX/NDXP prices every second, only the ratio, which moves very slowly. The backtest refreshes R only when a live update would have arrived.
- **Same second, or the level is wrong:** at NQ ÷ QQQ ≈ 41, a $0.01 error in QQQ moves every QQQ level ≈ 0.4 NQ pts; at ES ÷ SPY ≈ 10, ≈ 0.1 ES pt; for NDXP/SPX one index point ≈ one futures point. F is the last NQ/ES trade at or before the board's **content time** c = floor(`asOf`) + `board_content_offset_ms`[era][map] (the measured median moment the board's spot reflects: before `skylit_stamp_change_date` 2026-03-17 QQQ +740, SPY +840, SPX −80 ms; from it QQQ −60, SPY −60, SPX −80 ms; the live stream the same (parity 2026-10-07: live boards = post-03-17 history) except SPY, set from the 2026-10 recordings (live 10-07 +300/+420 ms); NDXP pending; findings R4) (Databento trades, history and live alike). The board is applied at max(arrival, τ + `map_latency_sec`), τ = floor(`asOf`) + 1 s (+ 60 s for 1-minute boards the spot-lag test hasn't cleared, §3.1): no measured content time passes τ, so the board is never applied before the market data it holds (owner 2026-10-08). The alignment check measures c on every recording against the futures and alerts if a content time falls after floor(`asOf`) + 1 s. Unit-test the timestamp convention (bar start-vs-end labels are a classic off-by-one bug).
- **Sanity check, not smoothing:** R normally drifts by well under 1 bp per minute, so a sudden jump means a bad input (a glitched or stale `spot`, or the futures price taken at the wrong second), not a real change. A new R is rejected if it differs from the median of the last 5 accepted R values by more than `ratio_outlier_bps` (start 3 bps ≈ 9 NQ pts at 30,000); the update's new strikes are still used, converted with the previous R, and the rejection is logged. **No lock-in:** if `ratio_reaccept_n` (start 3) updates in a row are rejected but agree with each other within the threshold, the shift is real: accept it, reset the median history and flag the day for review. Step 8b reports how often rejections happen; if often, find the cause rather than raising the threshold.
- **Same contract:** F always comes from the session's single front-month contract (Databento's `NQ.v.0` / `ES.v.0` resolved at the session start, §3.2); a ratio is never carried across a roll.
- **Opening minutes:** SPX/NDXP values right after 09:30 are computed while many stocks haven't opened, so SPX/NDXP levels don't exist until the first update after `ratio_open_skip_sec` (start 120). QQQ/SPY trade at the open and are unaffected. Nothing trades before 09:50 anyway.
- **Rounding:** keep exact floats for features. Round only prices that go into orders (tick 0.25): stops **away** from entry (sizing uses the rounded stop), targets **toward** entry, limit entries to the passive side.
- **Precision gate (step 8b):** at every Skylit update (where the futures price at that second is known), compare the level from the previous update's R with the level from the current R (= the error of a ratio up to one map interval old). Report median / 95th / 99th percentile / worst case in futures points per chain, by time of day and volatility, next to `mapping_p95_max` (1.0 NQ pt for QQQ, 0.5 NQ pt for NDXP, 0.25 ES pt for SPY/SPX): **a diagnostic, not a gate condition** (owner 2026-10-08, §13 Q8b-4; the step change mixes real R drift with the ETF's last-trade noise). **Pass (owner 2026-10-08)**, scored on strike crossings at event moments (relevant nodes) with R known before the crossing (findings R1): (a) **bias**: each chain's median signed error (futures − strike × R) has a 95% day-block bootstrap interval that contains 0 or lies within one futures tick; (b) **estimator**: the chosen estimator's (F1.7) crossing p95 is within 10% of the best possible R's (a centred median, evaluation only); (c) the **alignment check**, in place of spot lag: on every recording, the fitted content time never falls after floor(`asOf`) + 1 s; **and** the owner's Atlas replay check below (the automated Atlas match is a regression check only, §13 Q8b-5). If not met: find the cause (stale `spot`, wrong second, a slow map cadence) before anything else, then re-measure. **Stop floor per level source (owner 2026-10-08):** a stop placed beyond a mapped level uses at least that level's source's mapping error at event moments, the strike-crossing p95 with the chosen estimator (findings R7: most of it remains with the futures still, so it is real level error, not 1-second timing): `stop_buffer_floor` QQQ 4.0 NQ, NDXP pending its method (≈ 14–15 NQ on the day-ahead test), chart levels 0 (no mapping); SPY/SPX create no NQ stops. Node bands stay as set (NDXP ±20 until the step-13 reaction-distance report, §5.4b).
- **Validation:** (1) unit tests with hand-computed levels, band conversion and rounding directions; (2) bar/snapshot alignment test; (3) **Atlas match (owner's charts):** the owner reads Atlas on **QQQ and SPY** charts, always the front (0DTE) expiration, matching `maxExpirations=1`, and knows NQ and ES reach the same levels at the same time (owner, 2026-10). So for minutes the owner picks, Claude Code lists the QQQ/SPY strikes the bot treats as relevant nodes (strike and % of King; node types are added at step 12, once step 11 builds the classifier) and the moments QQQ/SPY traded at each strike next to the NQ/ES price at the bot's mapped level then (owner 2026-10-08, §13 Q8b-5: the futures-vs-mapped difference is information only, a regression check with `atlas_match_tol` frozen at the crossing p95, QQQ 4.0 NQ / SPY 0.78 ES, alerting if fewer than `atlas_regression_min_share` (90%) of the crossings pooled over all sample days fall within it; per-day shares are information only; baseline 2026-10-11 with `map_latency_sec` 0.59 and §5.2's relevance rule with the 20% hold (owner 2026-10-11): QQQ 94.6% of 519, SPY 94.6% of 574 (2026-10-09 with a plain ≥ 25% per board: 94.2% of 500 / 95.2% of 495); the tables mark nodes listed only because of the 20% hold ("held"), and a crossing counts only while its node is still relevant on the board in force then, as in the gate); the owner checks in Atlas replay that the same strikes show as nodes of similar size, and that replay check is the Atlas gate condition.
