A strike Atlas shows that the bot misses (or the reverse) means the node-strength cutoff or `skylit_max_strikes` window differs — find the cause.

### 5.2 Skylit structure
- **The four maps:** SPY, SPX, QQQ and **NDXP** (the daily 0DTE Nasdaq-100 chain).
- **Node types (computed by the bot):** history has no `nodeType` (§3.1), so the bot classifies every strike itself from the per-strike values, the same way in backtest and live: King = the largest |value| on the map (within the `skylit_max_strikes` window); Gatekeeper and significant nodes are relevant nodes (≥ 25% of King, owner 2026-10-05): every relevant node between price and the King is a Gatekeeper, every other relevant node is significant, and anything smaller is normal; pika (positive) and barney (negative) are the node's colour, a separate feature (`docs/feature_spec.md` F2.5). Skylit's own live `nodeType` is not used. Step 7 pulls 40 `/v1/historical` instants (which do carry `nodeType`); once step 11 builds the classifier it measures how often the bot's types agree with Skylit's, and step 12 shows the report to the owner; disagreements go to the owner, who decides whether the definition needs changing.
- **Node strength:** absolute value as a **% of that map's King** (the measure Atlas shows), plus the bot's node type. Sizes are only ever compared within a map — raw gamma values of SPX vs SPY, or NDXP vs QQQ, are on different scales.
- **Relevant nodes (owner's rule):** a node is structure only if it is **≥ 25% of its map's King** (`min_node_pct_of_king: 0.25`). Smaller nodes create no zones or events. The backtest reports how nodes just under the cutoff behaved, so the owner can check the 25%.
- **King (owner):** not required for any setup, but the strongest node on its map and the most likely to act as a floor or ceiling. Node type, % of King and a King-present flag are model features; the model learns how much more a King is worth (no hand-set weight).
- **Combining maps:** all Nasdaq levels sit on one NQ level map, all S&P levels on one ES level map, each node keeping its source map as a feature. QQQ and NDXP nodes within `confluence_tol` merge into one NQ zone and count as confluence (§5.4). S&P nodes are read on ES at the same moment (alignment, Trinity, obstacles) and never create an NQ event by themselves. QQQ vs NDXP and SPY vs SPX count separately, as the owner reads them.
- **Node identity:** a node is tracked by (map, strike), so its history survives R updates and King changes. It stops being structure only below `node_drop_pct` of King (start 0.20; it becomes structure at 0.25), so a node hovering near the cutoff doesn't flicker; % of King is against that same board's King, and the state starts fresh at each session's first applied board (owner 2026-10-11; feature_spec F2.4). Every check and feature uses this one rule (step 8b's gate, Atlas tables and regression alert, and step 11; owner 2026-10-11). Freshness: §5.4.
- **Velocity:** computed by the bot from consecutive snapshots, the same way in backtest and live: `(value_t − value_{t−k}) / |value_{t−k}|`, where value_{t−k} is the latest applied board with τ ≤ τ(current board) − `velocity_lookback_sec` (start 60), so history and stream give the same number. Skylit's live `velocityPct` (unknown formula, absent in history) is display-only, never a model input.
- **Velocity lean:** per map, Σ(velocity × % of King) over relevant nodes below spot − the same sum above spot, averaged over the last `lean_window` minutes; accumulation in one direction tends to pull price that way.
- **Positive / negative nodes (Skylit docs):** positive value = yellow ("pika" colour, dealers long gamma, slows price); negative = purple ("barney", dealers short gamma, speeds price). Magnitude matters more than colour; sign is a separate feature (`docs/feature_spec.md` F2.2).
- **Rolling ceilings/floors (Skylit docs + owner, 2026-10-05; `docs/feature_spec.md` F2.11):** the ceiling is the largest-magnitude node above price (either sign). A **ceiling roll-down** = a closer node, still above price, becomes the largest, whether the old one faded, the new one grew or both (e.g. QQQ 760 and 759, with 759 becoming the largest above price); a change counts once it has held for `node_switch_min_sec`. A **floor roll-up** is the mirror image. Price simply moving through the old ceiling is not a roll. A roll stays **active until it is rolled back** (owner 2026-10-05: a ceiling roll-down is bearish in general and a floor roll-up bullish; it ends when the ceiling or floor rolls back to its original position, and is paused while price is through the current ceiling or floor (beyond its zone) until price comes back fully below the ceiling or above the floor; a new roll-down of the ceiling that formed above price while paused replaces it with a new, active roll; never a timer). The compression of node mass above/below spot (since the open and since the roll began) is a feature too (owner: shrinking upside or downside targets matter). Features: roll active, roll paused, net strikes rolled since the anchor, rolls, roll-backs (full and partial) and pauses today, the shift in pts, the old node's shrink and the new node's growth, minutes since the roll began.
- **Gamma regime (Skylit docs, F2.12):** per map, net gamma within ±`regime_band`% of spot normalised by that map's King, plus the positive share of |gamma| there (the docs' yellow-vs-purple predominance). Maps are never summed; each is a separate feature (+ gameplan HVL, a gameplan-specific input gated by §5.6). Long gamma = slower, levels hold, ranging; short gamma = overshoots, trending.
- **Rug / reverse rug, pika cloud (Skylit docs + owner, 2026-10-05; F2.13–F2.14):** rug = a relevant positive node (≥ 25% of King, so it can cause the bounce) with a negative node of any size at most `rug_gap_strikes` (1) strike step below it (closer counts too; more negative strikes directly below count as well), spot below the positive node; reverse rug = the mirror (a negative node at most 1 strike step above a relevant positive node, spot above the positive node). The earlier placeholder's "no significant floor within `floor_search`" clause is dropped. Pika cloud = adjacent positive strikes, each ≥ `pika_cloud_min_pct` (0.10) of the King, at least `pika_cloud_min_nodes` (2) long (context, not a level).
- **Map-shape concepts from the Skylit docs (owner-approved 2026-10-04; model inputs only, never trade rules):** exact definitions in `docs/feature_spec.md`.
  - **Beach ball** (overshoot): a node crossed and then reclaimed (Crossed vs flipped, below). Features: overshoot depth, seconds beyond, count today (F2.7).
  - **Nodes as magnets:** pull = % of King ÷ distance (in expected moves, floored at `magnet_min_dist_em`), summed above and below price per map (F2.10).
  - **Air pockets (owner, 2026-10-05):** runs of adjacent low-magnitude strikes (each < `air_pocket_max_pct`, owner: 2% of that map's King) that price moves through quickly, faster through negative ones; whether price sits in one, the pocket ahead of and behind the trade, and its negative share (F2.15).
  - **King dominance and migration:** King ÷ second-largest node, King distance, King strike changes today (a change counts after `node_switch_min_sec`), direction and stair-step count (F2.16).
  - **Rainbow road:** counts of relevant positive and negative nodes, sign mix, King dominance and a rainbow flag (`rainbow_min_nodes_per_sign`, `rainbow_max_dominance`) (F2.17).
  - **Whipsaw map:** per-map bias (up / down / pinned from velocity lean with `bias_deadband`), Trinity bias agreement and conflict, and a whipsaw flag (conflict while a floor and a ceiling exist) (F2.18).
- The exact measurable definitions of every concept in this section live in `docs/feature_spec.md` (step 5; owner approves at step 6).
- **Crossed vs flipped (owner):** a node is **crossed** — the setup is invalidated — the moment price trades beyond its band. That is not yet a GEX flip. It **flips** (ceiling → floor, or floor → ceiling) only when a **5-minute bar settles beyond the band** (open and close both past it by ≥ `flip_margin`, per level type, start 0, tuned at step 13 on months 1–4 in three groups and written per type, owner 2026-10-05, `docs/feature_spec.md` F16.4 — owner: learn the buffer around each type of level; clock-aligned 5-minute bars, `flip_bar_minutes: 5`). After a cross the level creates no events until it either flips (events resume in the opposite role) or is **reclaimed**: price comes back through the band to the original side and leaves it by `rearm_dist`; events then resume in the original role (`reclaimed` is a feature). Re-entering the band from the crossed side creates no event. Flips can repeat both ways (owner): e.g. a ceiling breaks above its band and is pushed back down — it can still hold later as a ceiling; if a 5-minute bar settles above the band it becomes a floor; if price later pushes back through and a 5-minute bar settles below, it flips back to a ceiling. Each node carries `flip_count`, its current role, minutes since the last flip and cross, and its freshness per role, and the model learns how these combine.
- **Trinity alignment** (owner's core read): SPY, SPX and the Nasdaq side (QQQ or NDXP) each have a relevant (≥ 25% of King) ceiling — or floor — whose band contains its own instrument's price ± `zone_proximity` (NQ) or `es_zone_proximity` (ES; default = the NQ value × ES ÷ NQ price at the session open) at time t, in the same role (no outcome is used). **A King is not required.** S&P bands are checked against ES ticks, Nasdaq bands against NQ ticks. The Nasdaq side counts as aligned if QQQ **or** NDXP is. Features: Trinity aligned yes/no, aligned count 0–3 (SPY, SPX, Nasdaq side), and separately how many of the four maps have a relevant node there (0–4), smallest and average node size (% of King), whether a King/Gatekeeper is among them, rug or pika structure present, per-map lean.
- **Nasdaq first, S&P second (owner):** QQQ and NDXP nodes are tied directly to NQ and matter most (the model learns how much more). SPY/SPX nodes **aligned** with the NQ trade (same role at the same moment on ES) strongly improve it. SPY/SPX nodes **in the trade's path** on ES — especially a Gatekeeper or King between the ES price and the ES equivalent of the target — can stall the bounce off a QQQ/NDXP node. Features: S&P nodes in the path (count, largest % of King, Gatekeeper/King present, distance in ATR); they also count as obstacles for target selection (§6.3).
- **Daily vol context (Tempest, as of the previous close):** for QQQ and SPY (and NDXP/SPX if Tempest covers them), the `/v1/vol/history` fields of §3.1 — SVX 1d/9d/30d/3m/6m, ATM vol, term slope, 30-day risk reversal, butterfly and skew slope, call/put tilt — plus realized vs implied, which the bot computes from NQ's own realized vol over `rv_lookback_sessions` (start 20) and SVX 30d, and the daily VIX close if `/v1/vol/history` serves VIX (otherwise VIX is not used). Inputs to the structure, day-type and pair-scorer models (not the trigger model, which works in seconds). Nothing intraday from Tempest is used, because no history exists for it. Backtest and live use the row of session T − `tempest_lag_sessions` (start 1, the previous close); step 7 and step 22 record when the previous session's row (and the VIX value) first becomes available and whether historical rows change on later pulls; if it isn't reliably there by 09:00 ET, `tempest_lag_sessions` becomes 2 for backtest and live alike (decided at step 7 before any events are built; if in doubt, 2). **Owner 2026-10-09:** it also needs the pre-open copy to be final: the step-7 finality check (`step7 tempest-finality`, Mon 2026-10-12 before 09:30) re-pulls the 10-08 and 10-09 rows and compares them with their first pre-open copies; unchanged = 1, anything changed = 2. Only pre-open copies are ever used (§3.1 Tempest). **Decided 2026-10-12: `tempest_lag_sessions` = 1** (both rows unchanged, 0 cells; §13). Step 22 re-checks it live: it records when each previous-session row first appears and re-pulls the pre-open copies on later days; a row missing at 09:00 or a pre-open copy that changes raises it to 2 for backtest and live alike (the step-21 execution-change re-run follows).
- **Flags and context:** (hedge nodes: dropped at step 5, since `docs/skylit/` doesn't define them), OPEX week; calendar (days to monthly expiry, quarter-end, CPI/FOMC/NFP/PPI/retail-sales days from `config/calendar.yaml`); `minutes_to_close`; `minutes_to_horizon_end` (minutes from `decision_ts` to the earlier of `session.flat_by` and the next news-blackout start, §9) and `in_session_release_today` (a `blackout: true` event whose window overlaps 09:30–`entry_end`), both from the calendar as known at `preopen_start` and both pair-scorer inputs. Features only; the model decides their weight.

### 5.3 Chart structure (computed from trades)
- **VWAP + σ bands**: anchors = Globex open 18:00 ET (primary) and 09:30 ET (secondary). Bands at 1σ, 2σ.
- **Volume profile**: volume per tick price.
  - Sessions: **daily from 18:00 ET**, **weekly from Sunday 18:00 ET**. No prior-day or prior-week profiles (owner doesn't use them).
  - POC = max volume price (a level; ties: nearest the range midpoint, then nearest the volume median, owner 2026-10-05). Value area = expand from POC one tick at a time (owner 2026-10-05) until 70% of volume → VAH, VAL (context, not levels: see below). Exact rules: `docs/feature_spec.md` F3.2–F3.3.
  - **LVN** (low-volume node, a level): a local minimum of the smoothed developing profile (Gaussian smoothing, σ = `lvn_smooth_ticks` ÷ 2, start 8 ticks; owner 2026-10-05, F3.4) whose volume is at most `lvn_max_pct` (start 0.3) of the smaller of the two peaks on either side (peak = the highest smoothed volume between the LVN and the next LVN or the profile edge). Daily and weekly profiles, recomputed every `profile_update_sec` (start 60) from data ≤ t; identity = profile + price rounded to `lvn_merge_pts` (start 5 NQ pts); band = half-width from the chart-level table (§5.4b); so an LVN keeps its history while the profile develops; it drops out when it no longer qualifies. HVNs are not used (the POC covers them).
  - **Single prints** (a level, owner): TPO over the daily session (from 18:00 ET) in 30-minute periods at `tpo_tick_pts` (start 1 NQ pt) resolution; a single print = a run of ≥ `sp_min_ticks` (start 8) consecutive prices traded in exactly one **completed** period so far (no lookahead). The unfilled part of the run, ± a learned half-width (start 0; set by the step-13 reaction report like other chart levels, owner), is the level's band (a run wider than `max_zone_width` is split). Because the run shrinks as price trades into it, an event uses the band **frozen at the touch** for its audit, cross and label; the live band only changes later touches. It stays a level — across later sessions too — until price trades through the whole run (`filled`); unfilled single prints from earlier sessions carry over, with their age in sessions as a feature.
  - Built from Databento trades (volume at each traded price). The step-12 check compares VAH/VAL/POC against Deepcharts.
- **VAH / VAL = premium and discount (owner):** the **VAH zone** runs from the VAH line up to the profile's high, the **VAL zone** from the VAL line down to the profile's low, for the developing daily and weekly profiles, recomputed every `profile_update_sec` from data ≤ t. They are **not levels**: a touch creates no event, they don't break, and they are not stop or target candidates. In the VAH zone price is at a premium (shorts have more power), in the VAL zone at a discount (longs have more power), mainly on ranging days. Every event records, per profile: `in_vah_zone` / `in_val_zone`, depth into the zone (0 = at the line, 1 = at the profile extreme), distance to the VAH/VAL line (pts, ATR) and `va_direction_fit` (short in VAH, long in VAL); with day type as an input the model learns the ranging-day effect. They are also **structure areas for order flow** (§1.1 principle 1): an order-flow trigger inside them can start an **area event** (§5.4) even with no level there. The backtest tests and validates all of this like any other structure.
- **IB (NY opening range)**: see §5.8 — adaptive 20–30 minute range from 09:30 ET, not a fixed 60-minute IB. Used two ways: the **fib golden zone** drawn on it (§5.8), and its **high/low as levels** once the range locks (`ib_lock_minute` = 30 → 10:00, fixed).
- **IB high/low (owner):** mainly a break-and-retest level. After price breaks below the IB low, the low can act as a **ceiling**; after a break above the IB high, the high can act as a **floor**. Both roles are tested — the range edge before a break and the flipped role after it. Same states as other highs/lows: price past the level and closing back inside within the sweep window = sweep (the level held); past it without a reclaim = crossed (setup invalid); a **5-minute bar settling beyond it = flipped** (owner): from then on a broken low acts as a ceiling and a broken high as a floor.
- **Overnight (ON) high/low** (18:00–09:30): its own structure level.
- **Prior-period extremes**: previous day high/low (PDH/PDL, 18:00–17:00 ET session), previous RTH high/low, previous week high/low. Tested-to-the-tick levels from earlier sessions (e.g. a prior overnight low that held) are tracked as `retested_extreme` levels (tested = within `retest_tol_ticks`, start 4, in the last `retest_lookback_sessions`, start 10; held = the structure label held).
- **Bounce, sweep or break (owner) — highs and lows only** (the sweep-eligible types in the §5.4 registry; **not** VWAP, POC, LVNs, single prints, dark pools or GEX nodes): price can bounce at the level, or **sweep** it — trade a little past, take the liquidity, and come back. A sweep = price goes beyond the zone by at most `sweep_max_depth` (start 15 NQ pts) and is reclaimed — a 1-minute close not beyond the band (inside it or on the range side) — within `sweep_reclaim_bars` 1-minute bars (start 3); both are tuned by the data (§7.1), and the sweep's depth, time beyond and order flow are model features, so the model learns how sweeps behave (owner). For these levels, trading past the zone is therefore not yet a break: it becomes a break only if the sweep window passes without a reclaim, or at once if price goes beyond the zone by more than `sweep_max_depth` (then the crossed/flipped logic of §5.2 applies). A swept-and-reclaimed level is an entry setup back into the range (entry style (c), §5.5), with the sweep extreme as a stop candidate (§6.2). Features: `swept` yes/no, sweep depth (pts, ATR), seconds beyond, and later the order flow during the sweep. The report compares bounce vs sweep entries per level type.
- **Day type inputs**: IB width vs its `ib_avg_sessions`-session (start 20) average, VWAP crosses so far, rotation count (a rotation = a move of ≥ `rotation_min_pts`, start 0.25 × ATR, away from a swing extreme before a new extreme; counted since 09:30), regime, vol.

### 5.4 Structure confluence
- **Level registry (canonical: every other section refers to this list):**
  | Type | Levels | Sweep-eligible (§5.3) |
  |---|---|---|
  | GEX nodes | relevant QQQ and NDXP nodes on NQ. SPY/SPX nodes are **features only** (alignment, Trinity, obstacles, §5.2): they never create an NQ event on their own | no |
  | Opening range | golden zone (§5.8); IB high/low from range lock (§5.3) | IB high/low only |
  | Session highs/lows | ON high/low, PDH/PDL, prior RTH high/low, prior week high/low, retested extremes (§5.3) | yes |
  | Volume profile | daily and weekly POC and LVNs; single prints (today's and unfilled earlier ones). VAH/VAL are context and order-flow areas, not levels (§5.3) | no |
  | VWAP | 18:00 and 09:30 anchors, 1σ/2σ bands | no |
  | Dark pools | QQQ top prints (§3.1) on NQ: full levels that create events on their own. SPY top prints on ES are cross-market features only. **Not in the first model** (owner 2026-10-06: Skylit's history starts 2026-04-01, `dp_in_model: false`, §3.1 Dark pools) | no |
  | Gameplan | zone edges, when a gameplan exists (§5.6) | no |
- **Chart levels** = every registry level except GEX nodes, the golden zone and gameplan zones; where a rule names highs/lows separately (sweep logic, §5.3), "chart levels" there means the others.
- All registry levels except the containers (golden zone, gameplan zones, below) merge into **one zone** per the Merge rule below → one event, not several.
- **Zone object (one definition for backtest and live):**
  - **Merge:** two levels merge when their bands overlap or their centres are within `confluence_tol`. No chaining past `max_zone_width` (start 60 NQ pts; a single QQQ node band is already ~40 wide): a level that would stretch a zone further starts its own zone. Deterministic order: existing zones keep their still-valid members; the remaining levels are processed in ascending price, each joining the nearest qualifying zone if the merged width stays ≤ `max_zone_width` (ties: larger % of King, then the older zone), else starting a new zone; bands that touch count as overlapping. On every recompute a member stays only while it still meets the merge rule with another kept member and the zone stays ≤ `max_zone_width` (the member farthest from the founding anchors is released first); released members and zones that now overlap are reprocessed in the same pass (the older `zone_id` survives); all updates sharing one timestamp are applied first and zones recomputed once, so arrival order can't change the result.
  - **Edges:** the union of the members' bands. **Invalidation is per member (owner):** when price trades past a GEX node's ±band, that node is invalid (the overshoot went too far) and drops out of the zone's active members; a 5-minute bar settling past its band turns it into a GEX flip, and the node can still hold on a later test. The zone carries on with its remaining members (e.g. a PDH with its sweep logic). The zone is crossed only when every current member is crossed by its own rule; a zone that loses all its members without any crossing before held or broke ends as a timeout for the structure label (unit-tested).
  - **Identity:** `zone_id` is assigned when a zone is created, from its founding members' anchors (node = map + strike, chart level = type + session), stored as a short stable id (e.g. `z17`) with a lookup table to the anchors, and kept while any member remains (members joining or leaving don't change it), so the bot knows a zone is the *same* level after it moves (needed for freshness and re-arm counts). **Every map update is used immediately:** levels and zones are recomputed on each Skylit update (each tick for VWAP, and every `profile_update_sec` for POC, LVNs and VAH/VAL), and all new decisions and the structure label use the updated band. A pending entry is removed (`exit`) if its zone vanishes or moves more than `entry_reprice_tol` (start 2 NQ pts) away from the entry price, so an order never rests at a price that is no longer at the level. The zone itself stays (same `zone_id`) and keeps trading at its new price: the next touch is a new event. Moves that large come mostly from VWAP, a jumping developing POC or a member dropping out of a merged zone; a node's own move from an R update is far smaller. An open trade keeps the stop and target already resting at the broker (v1 only moves the stop for breakeven), so trade labels use the bracket fixed at entry.
  - **Crossed / flipped for every level:** GEX nodes use §5.2; highs/lows (including retested extremes) and IB high/low use §5.3 (sweeps first); single prints cross like other chart levels (against the band frozen at the touch, §5.3) and end when filled. **Every other level** (VWAP and bands, POC, LVNs, and dark-pool levels while they are in the top list; they end when they leave it, §3.1) uses the same §5.2 state machine with one difference (owner): it is **crossed only when a 1-minute candle settles beyond its band** (open and close both past it); trading past the band without settling is not a break, and an audit or touch in progress stays valid. Flipped when a 5-minute bar settles beyond it, reclaimed as in §5.2.
  - **Containers:** the golden zone and gameplan zones are never merged with other levels; levels inside them are counted as confluence features. Golden zone (owner): the current setup is invalidated when a 1-minute candle settles beyond the 0.786 edge (open and close both past it). There is no limit on tests: price can come back later and hold, and that return is a new event — it re-arms once price is back on the original side of the 0.786 edge and `rearm_dist` away from the zone.
  - **Touch and re-arm:** a touch = price enters the zone ± `zone_proximity` (the single touch rule; freshness, events and labels all use it). The next touch of the same zone counts only after price has left the zone ± (`zone_proximity` + `rearm_dist`) (`rearm_dist` start 10 NQ pts). While a break is unresolved — a high/low inside its sweep window and within `sweep_max_depth`, a chart level beyond its band with no settled 1-minute candle yet, the golden zone beyond 0.786 with no settled candle — price on the far side neither re-arms the zone nor changes its approach side, and coming back inside is the same event (a sweep reclaim or audit), not a new touch. A re-arm on the far side happens only after the level is crossed and flips; after a reclaim it re-arms on the original side (§5.2).
  - **IDs:** `event_id = <UTC timestamp>-<zone_id>`. Each order sent gets a unique `order_id = <event_id>-<entry style><n>-<account>-<leg>` (n = 1, 2… per style per event, since order-flow styles can fire again, §5.7; leg = `NQ` / `MNQ`; e.g. `…-b2-mffur1-MNQ`), so a re-fired style, a second entry style or a second TradersPost account trading the same event never collides (§10.3).
  - **Direction:** approach from below → short (the zone is a ceiling); from above → long (floor). The approach side is the side price was on at the last re-arm. Flipped levels use their new role. Golden zone: the direction from the fib orientation (§5.8); the drawing and its direction stay the same all day after the range locks (owner). Gameplan zones: the approach rule; agreement with the gameplan's `side` is a feature. A zone moving onto price is not a touch; it arms once price is `rearm_dist` away.
  - **Area events (VAH/VAL, §5.3):** when an order-flow trigger (entry style a or b) fires inside a VAH zone (short only) or a VAL zone (long only) while no level zone is within `zone_proximity` of price, it creates an area event (`event_type: area`; one area event per trigger even when price is inside both the daily and the weekly area — both profiles' features are recorded; `zone_id` = the areas containing price + session; it is its own touch group). Only entry styles (a) and (b) apply, and only stop candidates 1, 3 and 5 (§6.2). "Level zone" here means an active (not crossed) zone. There is no touch and no structure label: the structure model's prediction is null for these events, and the trigger model and pair scorer judge them. Firings in the same area before re-arm are further firings (n = 2, 3…) of the existing area event, subject to `trig_min_gap_sec`; the event ends when price leaves the area or re-arm is met. The next area event in the same area needs `area_rearm_sec` (start 300) or a move of `rearm_dist` away from the last trigger price. A trigger near a level belongs to that level's event instead (and carries the VAH/VAL context features). Area events exist from step 19 (they need order flow). A pending area-event entry is removed (`exit`) on `entry_ttl_sec` or when price leaves the area.
  - **Overlapping events:** levels inside a container keep their own zones and events. Events created by the same touch share a `touch_group_id`; training weights a touch group once (pair-row weight = 1 ÷ (events in its touch group × filled pair rows of its event); trigger rows 1 ÷ firings in the event). The simulator and live take at most one of them: the **first** event whose pair passes the thresholds trades, later events in the group are skipped while that entry is pending or filled (if it is removed cleanly and unfilled, the others become eligible again, as for entry styles, §7.1), and highest EV decides only between events in the same second (no hindsight).
  - **No hand-made setup labels (owner):** a zone is described by its full makeup — which level types, how many, how tightly they stack, how big and fresh the nodes are, and how far it sits from the next zone — and the models learn how each of these affects price (§5.4 Confluence). The only flag with a hard rule attached is `in_golden_zone`, because of the 09:50–10:00 window (§1.2). Entry style is learned the same way (§7.1).
- Cross-market features: ES at its own stacked level at the same moment (SPY dark pool, ES ON/PDH/PDL, SPX/SPY nodes), **NQ/ES divergence** at the touch (one makes a new extreme, the other doesn't — SMT), plus relative delta (NQ vs ES delta, each ÷ its own volume, over `rel_delta_sec`). ("Whether ES is reacting there" was dropped, owner 2026-10-05: Trinity covers it; ES is never traded.) Every NQ-point parameter applied on ES (re-arm, sweep depth, flip margin, merge tolerance, zone width, half-widths, proximity) is the NQ value × ES ÷ NQ price at the session open (owner 2026-10-05).
- **Freshness (owner, all level types):** the more times a level has been tested and held, the less likely it is to hold the next time; fresh levels are strongest. Every level carries `tests_so_far` and `holds_so_far` (today, and since creation for multi-day levels such as PDH/PDL, prior week high/low, weekly POC and LVNs, unfilled single prints and dark pools), plus minutes since the last test. Only tests whose outcome was already resolved at time t count as held; open ones are pending. The model learns the decay; the report shows hold rate by test number per level type.
- **Day memory**: how many levels held vs broke so far today (counting only touches whose outcome was already resolved at time t), and yesterday's day type.
- **Distances are vol-scaled** (ATR = `atr_period`-session (start 14) RTH daily ATR from completed sessions): every distance feature is stored in points **and** in ATR units **and in expected moves**: the bot builds the day's expected move itself: NQ points = NQ RTH close × SVX1d(QQQ) ÷ 100 ÷ √252, both from session T − `tempest_lag_sessions`, the close taken from today's front contract (ES: ES close × SVX1d(SPY)), identical in backtest and live, so a 30-point miss means the same thing on a quiet day and a wild one.
- Features: count of structures stacked, which types (one-hot), daily vs weekly profile, each member's node strength and freshness, and **proximity**: zone width, spread between the members, each member type's distance from the zone centre, distance from price to the zone edge, and distance (pts and ATR) to the next zone above and below.
- Expected (tested, not assumed): stacked levels hold more often, especially fresh nodes in long gamma; crowded levels may break harder in short gamma. Confluence zones are expected to be stronger targets too (§6.3).
- **Confluence (learned, no hand-set weights — owner):** each zone carries the type, number and proximity features above, plus the largest node's % of King and King/Gatekeeper present. The structure model and the pair scorer learn what each is worth and how they combine (e.g. a King plus the weekly POC stacked tightly vs loosely), from how price actually reacted. 
- Report every run: hit rate by stack count and by pairing type, split by gamma regime — this is the direct test of "more confluence = stronger level".

### 5.4b Zone width, activation and invalidation

Every level is drawn as a **zone** (a band), not a line. Price "at structure" (core principle 1) means inside the zone ± `zone_proximity`.

**Starting values and caps (`config/features.yaml`):**
| Item | Value / question | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| Skylit node band (decided) | **QQQ and SPY: ±0.5** of the strike (underlying $); **SPX: ±5**; **NDXP: ±20** (starting value) | Converted to futures with R on every update: QQQ ±0.5 ≈ ±20 NQ pts, NDXP ±20 ≈ ±20 NQ pts, SPY ±0.5 = SPX ±5 ≈ ±5 ES pts. Every band is the same ~0.07% of price, so a QQQ node and an NDXP node at one price get the same width (SPY/SPX already match). Already allows for overshoots and near-misses. `node_band: {QQQ: 0.5, SPY: 0.5, SPX: 5, NDXP: 20}`. QQQ/SPY/SPX are fixed; the backtest reports NDXP reaction distances (testing ±10/15/20) and the owner confirms |
| Beyond the band (decided) | **Crossed** (setup invalidated): GEX nodes the moment price trades past the band; chart levels when a 1-minute candle settles beyond it; highs/lows by the sweep rule (§5.4 Crossed / flipped). **Flip** = a settled 5-minute bar beyond the band, for all (§5.2) | Cross = label "broke" for the structure model. How flipped levels then behave is measured, not assumed |
| Half-width for each chart level type | Every registry type (§5.4) except GEX nodes, dark pools (`dp_band` is fixed like a QQQ node band; the step-13 reaction report shows dark-pool reaction distances for the owner to confirm), the golden zone and gameplan zones; single prints get a learned half-width around their unfilled run | Owner lets the data decide: Claude Code proposes a start value and a cap for each at step 5 (owner approves at step 6), and the step-13 reaction-distance report on months 1–4 sets the width inside the cap (NQ; ES cross-market levels default to the NQ value × ES ÷ NQ price). The owner may still write his own in the owner notes |
| Gameplan zones | Already have explicit edges | Use as given |
| `zone_proximity` | How far outside a zone price can be and still count as "approaching" | Claude Code proposes the start value at step 5; owner approves at step 6 |

**Learned from data (inside the caps approved at step 6):**
- Skylit node bands are set by the owner (above): QQQ/SPY/SPX fixed, NDXP ±20 confirmed after the reaction-distance report. For **chart levels** (the same types), every approach records the **closest distance reached** and whether a reaction followed (reversal ≥ `react_pts` within `react_minutes`); the distribution of reaction distances per level type gives the empirical "active band", and widths are tuned to that, inside the cap approved at step 6. Rule: an approach = price comes within the type's cap + `zone_proximity` of the level line after re-arm (price beyond cap + `zone_proximity` + `rearm_dist` from the line); reaction distance = max(0, the deepest point past the line) before a reversal of ≥ `react_pts` within `react_minutes`, taken only over approaches followed by such a reaction (highs/lows: penetration beyond the sweep window excluded; single prints: measured from the near edge of the unfilled run); half-width = the `react_width_q` quantile (start value proposed at step 5) per level type, clipped to the cap. Regime splits are report-only unless pre-declared at step 6.
- `distance_to_level_center` (in points and as a fraction of zone width) is also a model feature, so the structure model learns how much a near-miss vs a dead-center touch matters.
- Flip behavior is measured the same way: after a flip (§5.2), record whether the level actually acted in its new role.

**Taps between map updates (resolution):** a new Skylit map only changes **where** the levels are. Between updates each level's band is fixed, and price is checked against it on **every trade**, so a tap that happens and reverses within seconds is still caught:
- Live and backtest alike: every Databento trade (tick) is checked against every band, in exchange sequence.
- Paths are stored as 1-second bars (§6.5), and every re-simulation (breakeven, stops, targets, policy and prop simulators) runs from them. Where two of entry, stop, target or breakeven trigger fall inside the same 1-second bar, the simulator re-reads that second's trades from the cached Databento files, in exchange sequence, to order them. Only when a single trade print meets two conditions at once is it scored the **pessimistic** way and flagged `ambiguous_tick`: entry and stop together → filled and stopped out; entry and target → filled, target not yet reached; stop and target → stopped. ("Not filled" is used only when the other reading would be a win.)

### 5.5 Entries (owner's definitions)
- **Failed audit** (short): price pushes up into a zone from below and is pushed back down out of it. It needs at least one trade inside the zone's band since the last re-arm, and completes at the first 1-minute close outside the band on the approach side after that trade (that bar is the trigger bar). A **sweep reclaim** (the style (c) trigger) = the first 1-minute close on the range side of the level's band within `sweep_reclaim_bars` (a close inside the band keeps the level reclaimed, §5.3, but doesn't trigger). At a high/low level, a sweep through the level and back (§5.3) counts too.
- **Audited hold** (long, the inverse): price tests a zone from above and is pushed back up out of it.
- Both (owner): price may go **as deep into the zone and stay as long as it wants** — no depth or time limit. The only thing that kills the audit is the zone's own invalidation (§5.4 Crossed / flipped: for chart levels a 1-minute candle settling on the other side; for GEX nodes trading past the band; for highs/lows a break after the sweep window); the audit must complete before price re-arms (`rearm_dist`), but a re-arm during the bar whose close completes the audit does not cancel it (re-arm takes effect only after that bar closes). The depth reached and time spent inside are recorded as features.
- **Settled bar** (owner's definition) = a closed candle whose open is beyond the band as of the bar's open and whose close is beyond the band as of its close (bands can move during the bar). Used for the 1-minute chart-level cross (§5.4), the golden-zone 0.786 invalidation (§5.8), the owner's breakeven rule (§6.4) and every 5-minute flip (§5.2).
- Four entry styles compared on the same setups, listed in the owner's order of precision: (a) at the level on **absorption** and (b) mid-bar order-flow trigger (**exhaustion**, delta divergence, imbalance) — most precise; (c) audit-based at the zone edge (failed audit / audited hold / sweep reclaim) — decent precision and confirmation; (d) plain entry at the structure on the touch, no confirmation — least precise, kept as the baseline. The backtest tries all of them and the results decide (owner); the ranking only breaks near-ties (EVs within `style_tie_ev_r`, §7.1). (a) and (b) need the order-flow features built in step 17, so Phase D tests (c) and (d); step 19 compares all four on the same setups.

### 5.5b Entry order types (how each entry style is executed)
Fill rate vs price quality is a real tradeoff: limits get the best price but miss fast moves (and tend to fill on the trades that keep going against you), market orders always fill but slip, stop orders fill only when price moves your way.

| Entry style | Default order | Why |
|---|---|---|
| (a) At the level on absorption | **Marketable limit** (fills at once, slippage capped), or a **stop-limit** 1 tick beyond the current price in the trade direction, limit `max_entry_slip_ticks` beyond the trigger | Absorption reverses fast, so the entry must actually fill (owner); a passive limit risks missing the move and is tested only as a comparison |
| (b) Mid-bar order-flow trigger (exhaustion, etc.) | **Marketable limit** (limit `max_entry_slip_ticks` through the current price) | Momentum has turned; fills like a market order but slippage is capped |
| (d) Plain entry at the structure | **Limit** at the zone edge on the touch, short time-to-live | Baseline: shows what confirmation adds |
| (c) Failed audit / audited hold / sweep reclaim | **Stop-limit** 1 tick beyond the trigger bar's extreme in the trade direction, limit `max_entry_slip_ticks` beyond that | Owner's idea: only fills if price confirms the move, still precise; the limit cap stops runaway fills in fast markets |

- Every pending entry has a **time-to-live** (`entry_ttl_sec`, tuned) and is removed (with `exit`, §3.5) on expiry, when its zone is crossed (§5.4 Crossed / flipped; golden zone: a 1-minute candle settling past 0.786; gameplan zone: traded past its far edge, its `invalidation` being a feature only, §5.6), or when its zone vanishes or moves away after a map update (§5.4). Pending entries are also removed at the start of a news blackout (§9), on a data outage (any §10.3 feed or Skylit staleness or lag, `live_gap`, a clock-offset block or SSD loss, §9), on `!pause`, and for area events when price leaves the area (§5.4). At `entry_end` any pending entry is removed, and after the trade's last leg ends and after every time-to-live expiry with no leg filled, `exit` also goes to both tickers (§10.3). These are the only removal reasons, and the policy simulator (§7 rule 3b) applies the same ones. At flat-by, `!flat` and `!kill`, the `exit` sent to every ticker also removes every pending entry leg.
- These are defaults; the **backtest decides**. For every event it simulates all applicable order types (limit, marketable limit, stop-limit — the ones §3.5 can send) and reports per entry style: fill rate, average slippage, how the **missed** trades would have done (adverse selection), and expected R **including missed trades**. The best order type per entry style (a per-regime split only if pre-declared at step 6) is chosen from that, not assumed — except that style (a) may only use marketable limit or stop-limit; its passive limit is report-only.
- **Targets and stops in every label and simulator:** a target (limit exit) counts as hit only when price trades at least 1 tick through it; a stop fills on touch at the stop ± `exit_slippage_ticks`. This applies to the structure label's reaction level, pair labels, the policy simulator and the prop simulator; labels and the scorer always use these trade-through rules; the per-account sweeps, lives and profile cutoffs and the prop-simulator reports re-simulate each account with its own fill rule from the stored paths (`sim_fills: true` = the step-21 touch fill), and the gate-23 replay compares against the simulator run with the replay account's `sim_fills`.
- **Order latency (backtest = live):** every order, every `exit` and every `breakeven` takes effect `order_latency_sec` after the decision (settings.yaml; start 1.0 s, a conservative placeholder used for every label and simulator run until step 21 measures the real 95th-percentile end-to-end latency, §3.5; if the measured value is larger, labels are rebuilt; step 25 re-checks it against the imported fills). A pending entry can still fill until its `exit` takes effect.
- Backtest fill rules: a passive limit fills only if price trades **through** the limit after it arrives (no queue model). A stop-limit is working from decision + `order_latency_sec`; it triggers on the first trade at or through `stopPrice` after that (or on arrival if price is already through) and then fills like a marketable limit at the triggering trade's time, never worse than `limitPrice`; any unfilled rest becomes a resting limit at `limitPrice` that fills only on a trade through it before removal (step 21 test 4 confirms Tradovate's behaviour when the stop is already through). A marketable limit buy (sell) fills at the best ask (bid) from `mbp-1` at its arrival time (decision + `order_latency_sec`); if the order's size (the §6.1 mixed position at `default_risk_usd` and default costs, in NQ-equivalent contracts: minis + micros ÷ 10, rounded up) exceeds the displayed size there, the rest fills at the limit cap (the pessimistic choice: there is no depth data); anything beyond the cap is unfilled and handled like a live partial fill. Same-timestamp ties use the pessimistic reading in §5.4b (`ambiguous_tick`). Timeout-exit slippage is measured from Databento trades and top of book, and stop exits in the policy and prop simulators fill at the stop ± `exit_slippage_ticks`, the same value sizing uses (a sensitivity run also reports the measured slippage on stops).

### 5.6 Gameplan JSON schema
```json
{"date":"2026-09-29","hvl":[30590,30590],"hvl0":[30600,30600],"regime_line":[30575,30615],
 "zones":[{"id":"E1","side":"sell","zone":[30640,30722],
   "trigger":{"type":"failed_audit","nq_price":null,"note":null,
              "es_gates":[{"direction":"below","price":[7766,7766],"as_written":"ES < 7,766"}]},
   "stop":30734,
   "invalidation":[{"instrument":"NQ","direction":"above","price":30722,"timeframe":"1m_close",
                    "acceptance_minutes":null,"as_written":"1m close above 30,722"}],
   "targets":[{"low":30615,"high":30615,"as_written":"30,615"},
              {"low":30575,"high":30590,"as_written":"the regime line 30,575-590"}],
   "other_conditions":["only with the VIX under 16.50"]},
  {"id":"E1 accepted","side":"buy","zone":null,
   "trigger":{"type":"acceptance","nq_price":[30722,30722],"es_gates":[],
              "note":"15+ min above 30,722"},
   "stop":30700,"invalidation":[],
   "targets":[{"low":30800,"high":30800,"as_written":"30,800"}],"other_conditions":[]}],
 "scenario_sets":[{"frame":"ny_am","label_as_written":"NY AM 09:30-12:00","scenarios":[
   {"id":"S-A","prob":0.5,"bias":"down","desc":"lid refuses, rotation to regime line"},
   {"id":"S-B","prob":0.5,"bias":"up","desc":"lid breaks"}]}],
 "other_conditions":["take the second test, not the first","no trade 30,615-30,640"]}
```
Parser version 3 (owner 2026-10-07; rules in §3.4). **Conditional entries:** an "acceptance through / breakout / breakdown" trade is its own zone item with `zone` null, `trigger.type` acceptance/breakout/breakdown, its NQ level in `trigger.nq_price` ([low, high]), the acceptance condition word for word in `trigger.note`, and its stop and targets; the checks test its stop and targets against `trigger.nq_price`. **`other_conditions`** (a list of strings on each entry and on the gameplan) keeps word for word what this schema has no field for (VIX and 10Y gates, "take the second test, not the first", no-trade gaps, size). **No feature uses `other_conditions` yet** (F8 doesn't read it); it is kept so a later feature can, without a re-parse. F8 uses a conditional entry (zone null) as a single-price zone at `trigger.nq_price` (owner 2026-10-07, §13 step 9 v3). Parser version 4 adds the glossary to the prompt (§3.4) without changing these fields; the effective gameplan (owner overrides applied, §3.4) also carries `owner_overrides` (the overrides applied) and `owner_confirmed`. From version 2: every level is [low, high] (one price → [p, p]); `hvl0` is the HVL0 / HVL 0DTE level; `targets`, `invalidation` and `es_gates` keep their words in `as_written`; a target with no price in the gameplan has `low`/`high` null; `timeframe` ∈ `1m_close`, `settled_bar`, `acceptance` (+ `acceptance_minutes`), `not_stated`, `other`; `direction` ∈ above, below, unclear; `bias` ∈ up, down, neutral, mixed; `frame` ∈ `ny_am`, `session`, `unspecified`. The stored file also carries `status` (`ok` = no review flag, `flagged`) and `flags` (each with `field`, `reason`, `origin` validate/model and `review` true/false, §3.4); only info flags never need the owner. How the features read it (owner 2026-10-07, §13 step 9; exact definitions in feature_spec F8): "settled bar" = the §5.5 settled bar (1-minute, or the timeframe the gameplan names); the scenario set used is the one matching the event's time (`ny_am` from 09:30 to `gameplan_ny_am_end`, `session` otherwise; a lone set at any time); ES gate wording "holding" / "reclaiming" X = above X, "losing" / "rejecting" / "failing" X = below X, anything else unclear (empty feature, never a guess); HVL and HVL0 are two separate features, empty when not given; a target range is a band like any other level (its near edge counts as reached, order by the middle); a gameplan zone's invalidation is a feature only ("invalidation as written has been met", per timeframe), never a trading rule (CLAUDE.md rule 6): the zone itself is crossed when price trades past its far edge.
Features: in_zone, zone_side, scenario_agrees (a scenario's `bias` in the set used matches the trade direction), es_gate_ok, hvl, hvl0, gameplan_invalidated, and the gameplan member-type flag. Missing gameplan → features null (LightGBM handles missing).

**How gameplans are used (28 archived gameplans, 2026-08-12 to 2026-10-02: 26 in Aug–Sep 2026 and 2 in October 2026; with step 10 starting in October or early November they are all in the sealed or top-up months):** If the ledger written at step 4b puts any archived gameplan date in months 1–9 (a later SSD, or a second month-1 merge), that file is held back (no events, candidates or features) until step 20, so months 1–9 stay gameplan-free; Claude Code tells the owner when the ledger is written.
- **From the start:** gameplan zones create events, their `stop` and T1/T2 become stop/target candidates, (a gameplan's own blackout notes are not used: the owner's news rule, §9, replaces them). The models judge these by their makeup like any other zone (what's stacked there, node sizes, freshness, distances), so a gameplan zone that sits on strong structure scores well even before the gameplan itself has been learned; the gameplan boosts setups that already exist on the chart and maps.
- **The gameplan-specific inputs** above (all of them, including the member-type flag and side agreement) enter a model only when its training months hold ≥ `gameplan_min_days` (start 20) gameplan days **and** the gameplan check has passed once: an automated comparison, on gameplan days in already-scored months, of gameplan-zone hold rate and realized R against zones with the same stack count and member types that are not gameplan zones; pass = gameplan zones are not worse (point estimate), reported with intervals. With the archive in the sealed months, the first check runs at step 20 right after the sealed score, before the production fit. Every monthly retrain then re-runs the gameplan ablation (same method as §7 rule 5, on scored months with gameplan days) on scored months and drops the inputs if they hurt.
- The owner keeps uploading a gameplan every day so the data grows.

With all archived gameplans in sealed months, gameplan stop and T1/T2 candidates never appear in training rows before the production fit; in any fold whose scorer has no gameplan rows in training, they are encoded as type `other_price` (distance features only) and a gameplan container's makeup is computed like the golden-zone container. The sealed score is reported with and without gameplan-zone events, and the rule-2b pass rule is applied to the without-gameplan set, run as its own simulation (removing gameplan trades from a list would lose trades they had blocked; months 7–9 have none), and there is no gameplan report before step 20 (months 1–9 have no gameplan days). There is no separate gameplan model: "the gameplan was right" means its zones held rather than getting run through (owner), which is exactly the structure label for gameplan zones. The report shows gameplan-zone hold rates, and the §5.6 gameplan check and the monthly gameplan ablation show what the gameplan adds.

### 5.7 Order flow (Databento)

**Discovery, not a checklist (owner):** every indicator below is computed at every structure touch and handed to the trigger model and the entry-style comparison. None is ranked or preferred because the owner uses it: the backtest finds which indicators, thresholds and combinations actually work as entry triggers, including patterns the owner has never looked for. The trigger report shows each indicator's lift (alone and in combination), on development months 1–6; individual indicators are not pruned by hand (LightGBM's regularisation handles weak ones, §7.2 item 2), and the step-20 gate decides only whether order flow as a whole stays.

**Scope rule:** every detector in this section runs only while price is inside a structure zone (± `zone_proximity`) or a VAH/VAL area (§5.3), at every structure type. Outside zones these features are not computed into events. Each trigger event records which zone it belongs to and what is stacked there.

**When a trigger fires (one definition for backtest and live):** while price is in scope, every detector is evaluated once per second on data ≤ t. **Detectors that fire:** absorption (style a); exhaustion, delta divergence, footprint imbalance (style b). Big trades, delta, CVD and the top-of-book readings are inputs only. A detector **fires** when its own thresholds (named parameters below, start values proposed at step 5) are crossed. Each firing writes one trigger row (the trigger model's unit), at most once per `trig_min_gap_sec` (start 5) per detector per event. Style (a)'s `decision_ts` is each absorption firing; style (b)'s is each firing of any other detector. Both can fire again within an event (a later firing is a new candidate), and the trigger model and pair scorer judge every one; which firing "worked" is never used to pick it. The detector set and thresholds are frozen on months 1–4 at step 19 (§7.1).
- **Big trades** (like Deepcharts' big-trades view; the owner doesn't rely on them because CVD and the footprint show the same thing, so they are an input only, never a trigger): the fills of one aggressive order (same `ts_event`, side and price, plus prints within `big_trade_agg_ms`, start 50 ms) are combined into one trade; a big trade = combined size ≥ `big_trade_min_contracts` (start: the 99th percentile of combined NQ trade size over the prior `big_trade_lookback_sessions` (start 20) sessions, so it adapts to volume). Features: big-trade count, size and side near the level, where they hit relative to the zone, and price progress after them. Big trades with no price progress are one absorption pattern among several (see Absorption below).
- **Delta** per bar = buy-aggressor volume − sell-aggressor volume; **CVD** = running delta; CVD slope.
- **Footprint**: per-price bid/ask volume over a rolling `imb_window_sec`; diagonal imbalance ratio ≥ `imb_ratio`; it fires on a stack of ≥ `imb_stack_min` imbalances in the trade direction (owner 2026-10-05: buyer or seller dominance; window, ratio and stack are tuned, the grid includes a single imbalance).
- **Absorption** (owner: lots of aggressive buyers at the top, or sellers at the bottom, that fail to move price, and then price starts to push away from them; seen on the footprint or as CVD divergence): aggressive volume ≥ `abs_volume` within the last `abs_seconds`, traded within `abs_ticks` of the visit's price extreme so far, wherever that is in the zone (owner 2026-10-05: order flow counts anywhere in the zone; area events: the area's price extreme so far), with no new extreme in that time, plus best bid/ask refill count ≥ `abs_refills` (from `mbp-1`, event-level). It **fires** (style a) only once price has moved `abs_push_ticks` away from the absorption range in the trade direction (owner: he wants to see price start to push away; the grid includes 0 so the data can show whether waiting helps; there is no time limit on the push, owner 2026-10-05: the range stays valid until the event ends or its zone is crossed, and the time to the push is an input). Feature: whether exhaustion or CVD divergence fired in the same zone visit (owner: absorbed **and** exhausted buyers is a great signal; the model learns how much).
- **Exhaustion**: aggressive volume decaying into the extreme (volume in the last `exh_lookback_sec` ≤ `exh_decay_ratio` × the window before it, with a new extreme made), thin volume at the high/low (unfinished auction) is an input only, not a firing condition (owner 2026-10-05).
- **Delta divergence** (the owner's "CVD divergence"): price makes a new extreme by ≥ `div_min_pts` within `div_lookback_sec` while CVD does not. It fires style (b); when it coincides with an absorption range it also feeds the absorbed + exhausted combo feature (it never fires style (a) by itself). This is how the owner's "absorption seen as CVD divergence" is covered: divergence alone is a style (b) trigger; with an absorption range in the same visit it feeds the combo feature and makes stop candidate 5 available.
- **Top of book (mbp-1, L1)**: best bid/ask size and its changes near the level, L1 imbalance (bid size vs ask size), spread, refill and pull counts at the best bid/ask.
- **v1 model inputs:** `trades` and `mbp-1` (L1) only — available over the whole window, in history and live.

### 5.8 Opening-range (IB) Fibonacci golden zone setup (owner's setup)
The owner catches many trades by drawing a Fibonacci retracement over the NY opening range and entering in the **golden zone (0.5–0.786)** on an order-flow trigger.

**Opening range, adaptive (not a hard 30-minute rule):**
- From 09:30 ET the bot tracks the running high/low. From **minute 20** onward the range counts as "established" and the fib is drawn on it, so a retrace into the golden zone between 09:50 and 10:00 is caught (owner's example: price hit the golden zone and bounced ~200 points at 09:55). Any of the fib levels inside the zone can play out (owner); which one was reached is a feature.
- If price makes a new extreme after minute 20, the fib is redrawn on the larger range. The range **locks at 10:00** (`ib_lock_minute: 30`, minutes after 09:30; owner's rule, not tuned).
- **Trade window (owner's rule, §1.2): from 09:50 to `entry_end` (15:45)** (`ib_fib_start_minute: 20`). It is the only setup allowed before 10:00 (09:30 + `ib_lock_minute`). After the range locks (`ib_lock_minute`) the fib **stays drawn on the IB all day**: price often comes back to the golden zone later, even after it already hit and played out earlier. Every return is a new event.
- **Repeat-touch features:** touch number of the golden zone today (1st, 2nd, …), whether the earlier touch(es) worked and how far they ran (**only outcomes already resolved at time t**; unresolved ones are marked pending), minutes since the last touch, minutes since the range locked, and whether price has left the IB since. The model learns whether a later re-test is as good as the first touch, instead of assuming it.
- **Skylit nodes inside the golden zone (owner: the setup works noticeably better with them, especially when QQQ, SPY and SPX all have nodes there):** features = which of the four maps have a relevant node (≥ 25% of King) whose band overlaps the golden zone (Nasdaq maps on NQ, S&P maps via ES), how many (0–4), node sizes and types, Trinity alignment (§5.2) inside the zone. The backtest reports golden-zone results split by how many maps have a node there, as a direct test of this.
- **Sample size:** a few golden-zone events per day (first touch plus later re-tests), so several hundred over the 12 months — the same 12 months as every other setup (owner: older data reflects different market conditions). If any golden-zone bucket ends up under ~100 events, the report says so and the model leans on simpler, pooled features for it.

**Fib orientation (owner's rule):**
- The fib is drawn on the leg in the direction of the most recent swing: if the low came first and price has run up to the high, anchor low → high (golden zone below current price = long pullback zone); if the high came first and price has sold off to the low, anchor high → low (golden zone above = short pullback zone).
- This is the owner's rule; no orientation is learned from outcomes. The step-12 visual check plots it on the sample days for the owner to confirm.

**How it enters the system:**
- The golden zone (0.5–0.786 of the established range) is a **structure zone** like any other and goes into the confluence engine: a golden zone that also holds Skylit nodes (best when QQQ, SPY and SPX all have one there), the VWAP, the ON low, a dark-pool level or a gameplan zone scores higher.
- Features: `in_golden_zone`, which fib level was reached (0.5 / 0.618 / 0.705 / 0.786), minutes since 09:30 when the range was established, range size in points and vs its `ib_avg_sessions`-session average (§5.3), whether the range broke the ON high/low, confluence inside the zone, regime, order-flow trigger readings.
- **Entry:** all four entry styles (§5.5), as at every other zone: (c) audit/sweep reclaim and (d) plain from Phase D, (a)/(b) order-flow triggers (§5.7) from step 19.
- **Stops:** candidates beyond the zone — past 0.786 + `stop_buffer` (§6.2 candidate 2, far edge) and past the range extreme (the 1.0 level) + `stop_buffer` (§6.2 candidate 6, golden zone only), plus the usual §6.2 candidates. Learned like §6.2.
- **Targets:** candidates include the range extreme (0 level), fib extensions (−0.272, −0.618), and all usual structures (VWAP, POC, LVNs, single prints, nodes, ON/PD levels). Chosen by the pair scorer (§6.3).
- Data: the fast 09:50–10:00 moves are why everything runs on 1-second bars and trades; order-flow detectors are built at step 17 and join the events at step 19.

---

## 6. Trade management: sizing, stops, targets, breakeven

Every trade has different proportions, so stop and target come from **structure**, and size comes from **fixed dollar risk**.

### 6.1 Position sizing (hard rule, not learned)
- All per-account inputs live in `config/accounts.yaml` (full field list in §6.7).
- NQ = $20/pt ($5/tick); MNQ = $2/pt ($0.50/tick). 10 MNQ = 1 NQ in risk.
- `risk_per_micro = (stop_pts + entry_slip_ticks × 0.25) × 2 + commission + exit_slippage_ticks × 0.50` — sized for the **worst accepted** entry fill, so a slipped fill still can't lose more than the risk amount. `stop_pts` is measured from the order's reference price: the limit price (passive limit), the trigger (stop-limit) or the current price at send (marketable limit). Every marketable or stop-limit order's limit sits exactly `max_entry_slip_ticks` beyond that reference. `entry_slip_ticks` = 0 for passive limit entries and `max_entry_slip_ticks` (start 4) for marketable-limit and stop-limit entries; `exit_slippage_ticks` start 1, set at step 13 (before labels are built) to the measured 95th-percentile stop slippage on development months 1–4 (method in step 13), and re-checked at step 25 against the step-24 imported fills (TradersPost log, or the owner's Orders/Fills upload). A few dollars of extra stop slippage on a third loss is ignored on purpose: firms usually allow slightly more than the stated drawdown (owner), so risk stays at drawdown ÷ 3 = 3 lives. So `risk_usd_per_trade` is **all-in**: a full loss, costs included, is at most that amount. `risk_per_mini` is the same formula with $20/pt, $5/tick exit slippage and `commission_per_mini_rt`.
- `micros = floor(risk / risk_per_micro)`, where `risk` is the capped amount from the order of operations below (profile multiplier, drawdown ÷ 3, remaining daily allowance). If `micros < 1` → **skip the trade** (stop too wide for the account). **Mixing NQ and MNQ (owner: allowed, `allow_mixed: true`):** pick minis `n` and micros `m` so the all-in risk lands as close as possible to the allowed risk without exceeding it, with the total size inside the account's limit (`n + m / micro_mini_ratio ≤` max, or `n + m ≤ max_contracts_total` on an account whose firm counts every contract the same (§6.7), and each instrument's own max where the firm sets one). Use minis for the bulk and micros only for the remainder (`m < micro_mini_ratio`), since 10 micros cost ~4× the commission of 1 mini ($19.00 vs $4.68 at MFFU rates). With `allow_mixed: false`, use the single instrument that lands closest. Commissions come from the account's `commission_per_*_rt`.
- **Max position cap (hard rule):** size is capped at the account's current limit: cap = min(the account's `max_minis` / `max_micros` / `max_contracts_total`, the active `scaling_tiers` row's limits for its current profit). Under a `max_contracts_total` limit a micro uses a whole slot, so when that limit binds the search keeps minis (e.g. 5 slots, a stop that fits 4.6 minis' risk: 4 minis + 1 micro, not 4 minis + 6 micros). The bot **sizes down to the cap** rather than sending an order the broker would reject; a capped trade simply risks less than the full amount. An order that would exceed the cap is never sent (checked again right before every webhook).
- **Per-trade risk cap = a static 1/3 of the account's total drawdown.** Actual risk = `min(risk_usd_per_trade × profile multiplier (§6.8; Custom: custom_risk_usd), drawdown_usd / max_risk_fraction_divisor)` with divisor 3 by default. Example: $1,000 drawdown → ~$333 per trade, **every trade, regardless of earlier losses**. The cap does not shrink after a loss: three full losses use up the drawdown (a dollar or so is left), and any later loss fails the account. This is deliberate (owner's choice): the most the owner can lose is the cost of the prop account, so the bot should press the edge rather than shrink after losses. The backtest's prop simulator uses this exact rule. The last trade is **not** sized down to fit what's left (owner): after two full losses (~$334 left) the next trade still risks the full $333.
- **Daily loss cap (when the account has one: `bot_daily_loss_cap_usd` if set, else the firm's DLL if `dll_enabled`):** risk is also capped at what's left of today's allowance, so a full loss lands at the cap, not past it (§6.7 item 1).
- **Daily profit cap:** size is also limited so the trade's target, if hit at the best accepted fill (no entry slip), doesn't push the day past the account's daily cap (see §6.7). Example: cap $450 (Rapid EOD eval), risk $333, target 3R → the trade is sized so a win nets ≤ $450 after commissions. If even 1 micro would overshoot the remaining cap, skip the trade.
- Order of operations: for each eligible pair, risk = min(fixed risk × profile multiplier, static 1/3 of total drawdown, remaining daily allowance if the account has a daily cap) → contracts from stop distance → cap at max position size → reduce if the target would exceed the remaining daily profit cap → drop the pair if < 1 micro; then rank the remaining pairs by EV (by expected dollars whenever any sizing cap binds — max position or scaling tier, remaining daily loss allowance, daily profit cap — §6.3) and send the best.
- Because dollar risk is fixed, every trade is measured in **R** (1R = the dollars risked). The model optimizes expected R, not points. **Account R:** in every simulator result, money score, gate and report, R = realized net dollars ÷ the account's nominal risk (`risk_usd_per_trade` × profile multiplier, capped at drawdown ÷ 3; `default_risk_usd` in default-cost runs), never ÷ the trade's own capped risk, so a trade the caps shrank counts for what it really made. Labels keep per-trade R for the scorer.

### 6.2 Stops (structural)
- Candidate stops, all beyond something that could support the trade:
  1. beyond the **swing extreme** of the approach — the highest high (shorts) / lowest low (longs) from the event's last re-arm to `decision_ts` (area events: over the last `swing_lookback_sec`, start 300) — + `stop_buffer`
  2. beyond the **far edge of the entry zone** + `stop_buffer`
  3. beyond the **next structure** behind the entry (another node, POC, LVN, single print, band) + `stop_buffer`
  4. the gameplan's `stop`, when the trade comes out of a gameplan zone
  5. beyond the **absorption range** (owner): when absorption (§5.7) was detected within `abs_stop_window_sec` (start 120) at or before `decision_ts`, inside the event's zone ± `zone_proximity` or its VAH/VAL area, the stop goes beyond the highest price (shorts) / lowest price (longs) at which the absorbed aggressive volume traded, + `abs_stop_buffer` (owner: about 5 NQ pts, the start value; frozen with the other execution parameters on months 1–4, §7.1). Available for **every** entry style and structure type, not only absorption entries. Built at step 19 (it needs the step-17 detectors); events are relabelled with it then.
  6. golden zone only: beyond the opening range's extreme (the fib 1.0 level, §5.8) + `stop_buffer`.
- **No stop guardrails (owner):** `min_stop_pts` and `max_stop_pts` are null; the only limit is that at least 1 micro fits the risk (§6.1). Claude Code proposes the starting `stop_buffer`; a stop beyond a mapped level uses at least `stop_buffer_floor` of the level's source (§5.1; candidates 2 and 3: the zone member that sets the far edge, or the structure the stop sits behind; candidates 1 and 6 sit beyond prices, not mapped levels, and have no floor). The model chooses the stop for each trade.
- Learned: the buffer (from stored path data, §6.5 — e.g. how often stopped trades overshot the stop by only 1–3 points and then hit target), and which stop type works best per regime/level type.

### 6.3 Targets (structural, min 1:1, no max)
- **Exact prices (one rule for backtest and live):** a target candidate's limit price = the near edge (the edge price reaches first) of the target zone's band as of `decision_ts`, plus the zone's centre as a second candidate so the model learns which works; stop candidates 2, 3 and 6 sit `stop_buffer` beyond the far edge of their band as of `decision_ts` (§6.2). Prices are then rounded as in §5.1.
- Candidate targets = every registry level (§5.4) and confluence zone in the trade's direction, plus fib extensions for golden-zone trades (§5.8) and the gameplan's T1/T2 when the trade comes out of a gameplan zone. The same structures used for entries are used for exits.
- In selection, discard candidates below **1:1 net RR** at the account's own costs (owner rule); labels are built down to `pair_build_min_rr` (0.8) at default costs so every account's true 1:1 candidates exist (§7.1, step 13). **RR is net of costs:** net RR = reward_usd ÷ risk_usd, with risk_usd = `risk_per_micro` (§6.1) and reward_usd = (|target − reference price| − entry_slip_ticks × 0.25) × $2 − `commission_per_micro_rt` (a target is a limit, so it has no exit slippage; minis likewise at $20/pt), so a tight stop isn't flattered by ignoring costs. The same net RR is used in EV and the 1:1 floor.
- **The farthest target is not assumed to be best.** The pair scorer (§7.1) learns, from every past trade path, which structures price actually reaches before the stop and which ones it stalls at. Distance only wins when the odds of getting there hold up.
- **The pair scorer** predicts, for each candidate, the probabilities of three outcomes — target first, stop first, timeout (neither by `session.flat_by` or the news flatten, §9, closed at market; the prop simulator re-cuts per account) — plus `E[R | timeout]` (a regression on the realized R of past timeouts). Pick the one with the highest expected R: `EV = P_target × RR − P_stop × 1 + P_timeout × E[R | timeout]`. Timeouts are not counted as wins or full losses; in the simple 2-outcome case this is `P × RR − (1 − P)`. A far target with high P wins; a far target with low P loses to a closer one.
- **Per account:** probabilities are account-independent (horizon `session.flat_by`, cut short by a news flatten, §9). Each account applies its own costs, 1:1 floor, λ, thresholds, caps and lives and picks its own pair (so one setup can trade on one account and be skipped on another), subject to the cross-account direction lock (§10.3); the entry notice lists each account's pair.
- **Stop and target are chosen as a pair**, not separately: a wider stop changes which targets are reachable and the RR of each. The model scores every pair (entry style × stop candidate × target candidate, §7.1 dataset) and, at each entry style's `decision_ts`, picks the best stop × target pair; across styles, §7.1 "Which entry style trades" applies (the first eligible style trades; EV only compares styles eligible at the same moment).
- **Prop-account aware objective.** Plain expected R favors rare big winners, which means long losing streaks that can breach a prop drawdown. The selection objective is `expected R − λ × risk penalty`, where the penalty is the pair's variance of R (from P_target, P_stop, P_timeout and E[R | timeout]). With breakeven on, EV and the variance use the four outcomes of §6.6 (`R_be` includes `exit_slippage_ticks`). `λ` is **not tuned**: it is 0 by default (pure expected R; the `max_three_loss_rate` limit, §6.5b, already keeps losing streaks in check), and the owner can set it per account (`lambda_risk_penalty`) — consistent with his view that losing an account is an accepted, bounded cost.
- **Time matters** only through P_timeout and E[R | timeout] (a target that usually takes hours carries more timeout risk). Time-to-target is predicted and reported, not added to EV.
- **Probability floor:** a pair is only eligible if P ≥ `min_target_prob` (tunable), so a huge-RR, low-odds target can't win on paper alone.
- **Winner's curse (must handle):** picking the best of many stop × target pairs systematically picks pairs whose P was *over*-estimated, so the chosen trade's stated EV is too optimistic. Order: (1) at each decision point (event × enabled entry style × firing n) pick the pair with the highest raw EV among that point's pairs passing the 1:1 floor at default costs — the **decision-point candidate**; (2) fit calibration on the filled decision-point candidates in the fold's tuning months (§7 rule 2), weighted 1 ÷ (decision points in the event × events in its touch group) (the same rows and weights serve ECE, quantile cutoffs and live checks; row minimums count the sum of weights); (3) apply EV and probability thresholds to the calibrated values; (4) live ranks each account's sized pairs by **raw** EV at the account's costs (raw expected dollars when a cap binds) and applies the calibrator only to the chosen pair before the thresholds, since the calibrator was fitted on chosen pairs. The policy and prop simulators re-run this per-account selection over all pairs at each decision point, in §6.1's order of operations, rather than re-sizing a fixed trade list; gate 16 also checks ECE on the traded pairs. Every reported result uses the **realized** R of those selected trades, never the model's predicted EV.
- **When any sizing cap binds** (§6.1: max position or scaling tier, remaining daily loss allowance, daily profit cap), pairs are re-ranked by **expected dollars after cap-limited sizing**, not by R: a nearer target at full size can beat a far target that had to be cut to a few micros.
- **Proof:** compare target-selection policies on walk-forward test months 7–9 (chosen once there, §7 rule 2 Choices vs scores; later runs only report) — nearest structure, farthest structure, max expected R, and risk-adjusted max expected R (identical to max expected R while λ = 0, so skipped then) — each run as the full per-fold pipeline (style, breakeven, calibration, threshold sweep on the tuning months) with only the target choice restricted by its rule — on expected R, win rate, max drawdown, longest losing streak, and simulated prop-account pass rate. Keep whichever wins by the §6.5b objective. The fixed 1:2.5 policy (the target at 2.5 × the chosen structural stop's distance, the stop picked by EV) is run the same way but only as the gate-16/20 baseline, never selectable; its target rows train the scorer but are never a candidate for any other policy.
- Learned per candidate: distance in ATR/expected-move units, target type, confluence at the target, **velocity of the target node** (a growing node pulls harder — owner's read: accumulation in a direction strengthens targets there), number of structures **in between** (obstacles, including SPY/SPX nodes in the path on ES, §5.2), regime, day type.
- v1 bootstrap before the pair scorer is trained: stop candidate 2 (far edge + `stop_buffer`; candidate 1 for area events, which have no far edge) with the nearest structural target ≥ 1:1, plus a fixed 1:2.5 baseline for comparison.

### 6.4 Breakeven (optional)
- If the execution bridge can't move the stop on Tradovate, breakeven is simply turned off (`breakeven_enabled: false`); trades run to stop or target. The backtest reports results with and without it so the owner knows what is lost.
- Move stop to entry (+ fees, `be_offset_ticks`) once the fold's chosen `be_trigger` rule fires.
- Owner's rule (owner: his view and starting point, not a must-use; the re-simulation below decides by the money score, §6.5b): when the target is **more than one structure away**, move to breakeven once a **1-minute bar settles past the first structure** between entry and target (open and close both beyond its band); when the target is the first structure, no breakeven. "Structures" here = active NQ registry zones (§5.4) strictly between entry and target as of `decision_ts`, excluding containers, VAH/VAL areas and ES-only levels; the first one's band is **frozen at its `decision_ts` value** for that trade, in backtest and live alike, and the labeler stores, per pair, the first 1-minute settle time past that frozen band. The `breakeven` signal is sent at that bar's close and takes effect `order_latency_sec` later.
- Learned: the trigger is chosen by **re-simulating every stored trade path** under the four pre-declared rules (none, +0.5R, +1R, first structure; `be_trigger`, owner-approved 2026-10-05, `docs/feature_spec.md` F16.2) and picking what scores best on the §6.5b objective on each fold's tuning months (§7 rule 2); a per-regime split counts as separate grid entries and must be within the ≤ 4 rules approved at step 6. BE often raises win rate but cuts winners that retest entry — the stored paths show the true tradeoff.

### 6.5 Path data stored for every candidate trade (taken or not)
So any stop/target/BE rule can be re-simulated later without rebuilding events:
- Full price path after entry: read from the shared per-session 1-second bars (Databento `ohlcv-1s`, never copied per candidate) from entry to 16:00 ET; the timeout label uses `session.flat_by` (anything still open then is closed at market, exactly as live), and the prop simulator re-cuts at each account's own `flat_by`. Events don't belong to an account: the prop simulator also re-applies the 1:1 floor with each account's commissions.
- **MAE** (max adverse excursion) and **MFE** (max favorable excursion), and the time each happened.
- **If stopped:** how far price went beyond the stop (overshoot), and whether the target was reached afterward (stopped-then-target) and how long after.
- **If target hit:** how far price extended past the target (extension), and whether it returned to entry afterward.
- Which structures were reached in order, with timestamps.
- Reports every run: stopped-then-target rate and overshoot distribution by stop type (tight stops show up here), extension past target by target type (targets left too close), BE simulation results.

### 6.5b Execution threshold (when the bot takes a trade)
- The threshold is on **expected R**, not raw probability, because every trade has a different RR. Breakeven probability for a trade = `1 / (1 + RR)` (1:1 → 50%, 1:2 → 33%, 1:3 → 25%). A trade is taken only if EV (the §6.3 formula, net RR, including the timeout outcome) `≥ min_ev_r` **and** `P ≥ min_target_prob`.
- **Pre-declared grids:** every per-fold choice uses a grid fixed in `settings.yaml` before step 15 (`threshold_grid`: ≤ 5 × 5 values of `min_ev_r` × `min_target_prob`; ≤ 4 breakeven rules; `last_life_grid` [0.10, 0.20, 0.33] (fixed, owner); and, fixed, `profile_ev_quantiles` {conservative: 0.67, protect: 0.90}). The menus (which values are on each grid) are proposed by Claude Code at step 5 and approved by the owner at step 6, before any result is seen; the data then picks from them.
