With few tuning days the top average is partly luck, so every per-fold choice picks the setting with the highest **money score** = mean total R per month − one standard error (day-block bootstrap), subject to the `max_three_loss_rate` limit: it rewards making the most money and discounts settings whose result rests on a few lucky days. The number of settings tried is printed with each fold's result.
- **How the values are chosen (never guessed):** sweep `min_ev_r` and `min_target_prob` on the out-of-fold tuning months of §7 rule 2 (months 5…m−1 for test month m); for each setting record trades/month, expected R per trade, total R per month, max drawdown, longest losing streak, and prop-simulator pass/breach rate per account. Pick the setting that maximizes the owner's objective (scored by the money score above, which applies to every per-fold choice of §6.4, §6.5b and §6.8) — **total R per month**, subject to winning often enough that three straight full losses stay uncommon: the bootstrapped share of 3-trade runs that are all full losses ≤ `max_three_loss_rate` (full loss = a stop outcome with account R ≤ −0.9; runs = overlapping consecutive 3-trade windows in the account's chronological trade sequence, across days, averaged over day-block bootstrap paths; account-independent choices and gates 16/20 (and the step-21/25/27 re-checks) use the `gate_account` block in settings.yaml — `default_risk_usd`, default commissions, `session.flat_by`, 2 minis / 20 micros, `default_max_three_loss_rate` (0.15), no daily caps, no lives, no `stop_at_target`, no drawdown or inactivity rules (per-account and preset results are reports); if no setting meets the limit, the fold takes no trades and the report says so) (per account, start 0.15, roughly a 47%+ win rate) — then confirm once on the sealed months at step 20. Both the win probability and R per trade count: this limit (and λ, if the owner sets one, §6.3) stops the bot from chasing rare far targets. Breach rates are reported, not used as a limit (owner: hitting the DLL or drawdown is acceptable as long as each trade risks the right amount). **Money first (owner):** what he wants is the most money made, which also passes accounts fastest; trade count matters only through that (more +EV trades raise total R per month, so the money score already favours them). Report trades/day and the share of days with no trade; if even the loosest +EV setting gives < 1/day, the report says so, with which setups would add volume.
- Requires **calibrated** probabilities (§7): a stated 0.65 must win ~65% of the time, or the threshold is meaningless. Live calibration is monitored and the threshold re-checked at each retrain.
- **"Lives" mode (owner's idea):** thresholds depend on how many full losses the account can still take: `lives = floor((drawdown_remaining − lives_dust_usd) / risk_per_trade) + 1`, with `risk_per_trade` = the all-in risk after the profile multiplier and `lives_dust_usd` (start 25) so a few leftover dollars don't count as a life — **drawdown only** ($1,000 at $333 → 3; after two full losses ~$334 left → 1, the last life). Lives ≤ 1 (including 0, when only dust is left) is the last life. The daily loss limit is not counted as lives (on a $600 DLL with $333 risk, any single loss would put the bot on its last life and it would barely trade); it is handled by the daily cap (§6.7). **It only changes anything on the last life** (owner's rule): while 2 or more lives remain, the normal thresholds apply. With **1 life left — one full loss from failing the account** — only the best setups are taken: the cutoff is the (1 − `last_life_top_pct`) quantile of calibrated EV among the decision-point candidates that pass the account's Normal thresholds in the fold's tuning months (if fewer than 30 rows lie above a cutoff, the next looser option is used — 0.33, then the Normal thresholds — and the report says so) (`last_life_top_pct: auto` by default: in per-fold step (4) of §7 rule 2 the prop simulator compares 0.10 / 0.20 / 0.33 on the fold's tuning months and keeps the one with the highest money score computed in the prop simulator in account dollars (account fees are not modelled, §6.7; after a breach the simulator starts a new evaluation); expected profit per evaluation started is reported; a number the owner types overrides it). It is written to `profiles.yaml` as the `last_life` thresholds, and live applies those. Risk per trade stays static.
- The prop simulator compares "lives mode on vs off" per account: pass rate, average days to pass, expected profit per evaluation started. Tradeoff to watch: on the last life the bot may wait days for a qualifying setup (the simulator counts a 7-day gap as an inactivity close, §6.7), and very high P setups usually carry lower RR. The on/off comparison is report-only; the owner's `lives_mode` flag decides.
- Per account in `config/accounts.yaml`: `lives_mode`, `last_life_top_pct`, and `min_ev_r` / `min_target_prob` for the Custom profile. The other profiles read their thresholds from `models/profiles.yaml` (§6.8).

### 6.6 Labels and costs
- **Breakeven-aware labels:** pairs get 4-outcome labels for **every** candidate breakeven rule of §6.4 (a cheap re-simulation from the stored paths), and each fold trains on the labels of the rule its own tuning months picked (§7 rule 2), so no fold uses a rule chosen with later data. The four outcomes are target / stop / breakeven exit / timeout, where `R_be` = the MNQ breakeven offset at default costs (§3.5 test 2: ceil(commission ÷ tick value) + `be_offset_ticks`) net of costs and `exit_slippage_ticks` (a breakeven exit is a stop fill); the pair scorer is retrained and EV = P_target·RR − P_stop + P_be·R_be + P_timeout·E[R | timeout]. A no-breakeven scorer is kept for accounts with `breakeven_enabled: false`; per-fold step (4) calibrates and sweeps it too and writes its profiles, so it is validated by the same folds and the sealed score. If step 21's test 2 fails, months 7–9 are re-run with breakeven set to none and must meet the step-16 criteria, and the production fit re-runs before step 24. Live checks compare hit rates under the same exit rule as the prediction.
- Costs in every label: commission + slippage per the order-type fill rules (§5.5b). Unfilled entries are recorded as "no trade" (with what would have happened), never as wins. The pair scorer trains and calibrates **only on filled rows** (its probabilities are conditional on a fill); unfilled rows feed only the fill-rate and adverse-selection report (§5.5b) and are never labelled with their hypothetical outcome.
- Labels stored per pair (event_id, entry style, firing n, stop candidate, target candidate) for every order type (the frozen order type per style, §7 rule 1, trains the scorer; the others feed only the §5.5b order-type report), plus the path data above. After each freeze (step 13 for (c)/(d), step 19 for (a)/(b)) the non-frozen order types are built only for months 1–4, where that report runs, and pairs whose `decision_ts` is outside the setup windows or in a blackout only for months 1–6 (the out-of-window report, rule 2b), which keeps every later rebuild about a third the size. Time limit = `session.flat_by`, or the moment the next news flatten takes effect if earlier (§9; that exit is a timeout at market) (no max-hold exit: v1 trades end only at stop, target, flat-by, the news flatten or an owner exit, §10.1); costs use the default commissions in `settings.yaml`, expressed per micro (`risk_per_micro`, §6.1, at the default micro commission — the costliest mix), and the fill simulation uses the size the default risk (`default_risk_usd`, start 333) gives; the prop simulator and live re-cost each trade with the account's actual NQ/MNQ mix and commissions.
- Keep the fixed 1:2.5 label (a target at 2.5 × each structural stop's distance) as a baseline to prove structural targets beat it.

### 6.7 Per-account prop rules (each account is different)
Every account carries its own rules. The owner edits them in the **Control Panel UI** (§9b) — no YAML editing needed — and the UI saves them to `config/accounts.yaml`. Both the live risk module and the backtest's **prop-account simulator** enforce them:
```yaml
- name: mffur1                 # lowercase letters and digits only (the UI checks); this example = the MFFU Rapid EOD 25k evaluation preset (below)
  active: true                 # false once passed, failed or replaced: only active accounts trade and get lives and caps
  firm: MyFundedFutures
  phase: evaluation            # evaluation | funded
  demo: false                  # true = Tradovate demo account (step 21–25 validation)
  min_trading_days: 4          # firm rules for the prop simulator's pass test (null = none); Rapid EOD eval: 4 days
  max_inactive_days: 7          # calendar days with no trade before the firm closes the account (MFFU: 7, every account)
  payout_min_days: null        # funded: trading days needed in a payout cycle (Builder: 2, which its 50% consistency rule forces anyway)
  traderspost_webhook_env: TP_WEBHOOK_MFFUR1   # name of the .env variable holding this account's webhook URL
  # --- profit cap: the owner picks a mode and types numbers ---
  profit_cap_mode: consistency # consistency | none (Rapid EOD eval: 30% consistency → $450/day)
  profit_target_usd: 1500      # the eval's profit target (consistency mode: also the base of the cap)
  stop_at_target: true         # evaluation: no new entries once balance ≥ start + profit_target_usd and consistency and min_trading_days are met (the eval is passed)
  consistency_pct: 0.30        # consistency mode: best day ≤ this share of the target
  payout_target_usd: null      # funded: profit above payout_baseline_usd at which the owner requests a payout (default payout_min_usd)
  funded_buffer_usd: null      # funded: profit above start balance to clear before any payout (Rapid EOD / Builder: 1100)
  payout_min_usd: null         # funded: smallest payout (Rapid EOD 500, Builder 250)
  payout_profit_since_last_usd: null # funded: net profit needed since the last payout before the next (Rapid EOD 250)
  payout_max_usd: null         # funded: largest payout per cycle (Rapid EOD: null = no cap; Builder 1000)
  payouts_taken: 0             # funded: set by the "Payout taken" button (count and last payout date)
  after_first_payout: null     # funded: cap fields that replace the current ones after the first payout (Builder: {profit_cap_mode: consistency, profit_target_usd: 1000, consistency_pct: 0.50} → $500/day)
  payout_wait_calendar_days: null # funded: calendar days before each payout may be requested, counted from the first funded trade for the first payout and from the previous payout after that; runs alongside the buffer, both must be met (Pro: 14; Rapid EOD / Builder: null)
  payout_clock_hold: "off"     # quoted: YAML reads a bare off as false; funded, with payout_wait_calendar_days: what the bot does once the payout is reachable but the clock is still running: off (trade normally; default) | pause (no new entries until the clock runs out) | protect (trade on the Protect profile until then); the share and cap stops of the payout cap group apply whatever this says
  profit_split_pct: null       # funded: the trader's share of each payout, for reports only (Pro: 0.80); null = reports show the gross payout only
  payout_cap_group: null       # funded: accounts with the same group share one cumulative payout cap (Pro: mffu_pro); null = no shared cap
  payout_cap_group_usd: null   # cumulative gross payouts allowed across every account of the group, all funded runs, active or not (Pro: 100000)
  copied_accounts: 0           # how many accounts copy this one through TradeSyncer (0 = none); the owner changes it any time, e.g. to 0 when he cuts a copy connection; used for the group payout cap
  auto_protect_within_usd: null # funded: switch to Protect this close to the payout target (§6.8)
  # --- loss side ---
  drawdown_usd: 1000
  drawdown_type: eod_trailing  # static | eod_trailing (owner never trades intraday-trailing accounts)
  drawdown_lock_usd: null      # eod_trailing: the floor value at which the floor stops rising; null = never locks (every MFFU evaluation, this example included: the floor trails continuously; MFFU Rapid EOD and Builder funded: start + $100 = 25100, reached when the $1,100 buffer is; Pro funded: 50100)
  drawdown_lock_trigger: floor # eod_trailing: floor = the floor stops rising when it reaches drawdown_lock_usd (Rapid EOD and Builder funded); first_payout = the floor trails with no cap until the first payout, then is fixed at drawdown_lock_usd for good (Pro funded: 50100)
  start_balance_usd: 25000     # account size at the start
  # daily state (balance, floor) is NOT stored here: it lives in <paths.live_dir>/account_state.json (below)
  dll_enabled: false           # false = this account has no daily loss limit (Rapid EOD has none; Builder: true, 600)
  firm_daily_loss_limit_usd: null  # used only when dll_enabled (Builder: 600)
  bot_daily_loss_cap_usd: null # null = use the firm's DLL (no daily cap if dll_enabled is false); set it to stop earlier, or to add a cap on a no-DLL account
  max_losses_per_day: null     # optional, e.g. 2
  max_trades_per_day: null     # optional hard cap
  max_three_loss_rate: 0.15    # §6.5b: share of 3-trade runs that are all full losses must stay at or below this
  # --- sizing ---
  max_risk_fraction_divisor: 3 # risk ≤ 1/3 of TOTAL drawdown (static, never shrinks)
  risk_usd_per_trade: 333
  custom_risk_usd: null        # Custom profile only (≤ drawdown ÷ 3); other profiles use risk_usd_per_trade × multiplier
  max_minis: 2                 # firm's max position in NQ (owner's MFFU 25k accounts: 2 minis)
  max_micros: 20               # firm's max position in MNQ (2 minis = 20 micros)
  micro_mini_ratio: 10         # how the firm counts micros vs minis (usually 10:1)
  max_contracts_total: null    # set when the firm counts every contract the same, mini or micro: minis + micros ≤ this, replacing the micro_mini_ratio rule (Pro 50k funded: 5, so 5 minis, 4 + 1 micro, 3 + 2, … 0 + 5); null = the micro_mini_ratio rule
  scaling_tiers: null          # optional: firm scaling plan, a list of {min_profit_usd, max_minis, max_micros, max_contracts_total (optional)}
  allow_micros: true
  allow_mixed: true           # one trade may be an NQ leg + an MNQ leg (§6.1, §10.3)
  commission_per_mini_rt: 4.68    # round-trip $ per NQ, all fees included (MFFU)
  commission_per_micro_rt: 1.90   # round-trip $ per MNQ, all fees included (MFFU)
  flat_by: "15:59"             # owner: trade 09:30–16:00, flat just before 16:00 (firms liquidate ~16:00–16:15)
  # --- risk profile (§6.8): quick dial the owner changes in the UI ---
  risk_profile: normal         # aggressive | normal | conservative | protect | custom
  # --- thresholds: min_ev_r / min_target_prob apply only with risk_profile: custom; other profiles read models/profiles.yaml ---
  min_ev_r: 0.20
  min_target_prob: 0.40
  lives_mode: true
  last_life_top_pct: auto      # last life: only the top X% by calibrated EV; auto = the prop simulator's pick of 0.10/0.20/0.33, or type a number
  lambda_risk_penalty: 0      # §6.3; not tuned; 0 = pure expected R, a positive number penalises high-variance pairs
  breakeven_enabled: true
  sim_fills: true              # Tradovate sim account: a resting limit fills on a touch (step 21); false for a real-money account
```

both clocks run at the same time), **minimum payout $1,000**, **$100,000 cumulative payout cap across all Pro accounts combined** (not per account, not per cycle; the payout that reaches it takes the account back to $52,100 with the floor static at $50,100 and nothing left to withdraw, owner 2026-10-09): `start_balance_usd: 50000`, `profit_cap_mode: none`, `dll_enabled: false`, `drawdown_usd: 2000`, `drawdown_type: eod_trailing`, `drawdown_lock_trigger: first_payout`, `drawdown_lock_usd: 50100`, `max_minis: 5`, `max_micros: 5`, `max_contracts_total: 5`, `funded_buffer_usd: 2100`, `payout_min_usd: 1000`, `payout_max_usd: null`, `payout_profit_since_last_usd: null`, `payout_min_days: null`, `payout_wait_calendar_days: 14`, `payout_clock_hold: "off"`, `profit_split_pct: 0.80`, `payout_cap_group: mffu_pro`, `payout_cap_group_usd: 100000`, `copied_accounts`: the owner's number, `after_first_payout: null` (the floor lock is `drawdown_lock_trigger`'s job), `risk_usd_per_trade: 666`, `max_inactive_days: 7`;   **Pro 50k, which rules the simulator already models and which are new (2026-10-08):** already modelled by existing fields: start balance, profit target, EOD-trailing drawdown, no DLL, minimum trading days, the consistency cap (same rule type as Rapid EOD's 30%: cap = `consistency_pct` × `profit_target_usd`), per-stage max minis / micros for the evaluation (each preset stage fills its own), no scaling (`scaling_tiers: null`), `stop_at_target`, the buffer (`funded_buffer_usd` → `payout_baseline_usd`), the minimum payout, no per-payout maximum, inactivity, the Tier-1 news rule (§9) and fees (not modelled for any account). **New rule types** (fields above; each defaults to null / `floor` / `off` / 0, so the Rapid EOD and Builder presets don't use them):
  1. **Payout clock alongside the buffer** (`payout_wait_calendar_days`): each payout also needs that many calendar days (weekends and holidays included), counted from the account's first funded trade for the first payout and from the previous payout for every later one (the clock restarts at each payout; owner 2026-10-09); the simulator counts from the first trade of each funded run and then from each simulated payout, live from the first funded fill the bot records and then from each recorded payout date (the owner can correct either date on the Accounts page). It and the buffer run at the same time: a payout waits for whichever is met last. **Hold while the clock runs** (`payout_clock_hold`, default `off`): once balance − `payout_baseline_usd` ≥ max(`payout_min_usd`, `payout_target_usd`) but the clock has not run out, `pause` blocks new entries (a Discord note gives the date trading resumes) and `protect` trades on the Protect profile until then; the owner switches it on when he feels the account holds enough, and manual Pause / Resume still works. The share stop and the cap stop (new rule type 3) apply whatever the hold is set to; `off` never overrides them (owner 2026-10-09). The prop simulator reports off, pause and protect side by side.
  2. **Drawdown that trails until the first payout, then locks** (`drawdown_lock_trigger: first_payout`): before the first payout, floor = `eod_high_water − drawdown_usd` with no cap (it can rise above `drawdown_lock_usd`); from the first payout on, floor = `drawdown_lock_usd` for good, even when the trailing floor was higher (owner 2026-10-09). Live floor check (§6.7 Account state): before the first payout the typed floor must lie in [start − `drawdown_usd`, balance); after it, it must equal `drawdown_lock_usd`. Lives and breaches use this floor; the per-trade risk cap stays `drawdown_usd` ÷ 3.
  3. **Cumulative payout cap shared by a group of accounts** (`payout_cap_group`, `payout_cap_group_usd`, `copied_accounts`): gross payouts (before the profit split) recorded on every account of the group, past and present, active or not, plus TradeSyncer followers, count toward one total. **Copied accounts:** each account carries `copied_accounts`, the number of accounts copying it through TradeSyncer, which the owner sets and changes on the Accounts page (e.g. to 0 when he cuts a copy connection); followers are not in `accounts.yaml`, so this number is how the bot counts them. **Trading to the cap (owner: always trade up to it):** the bot keeps trading every account of the group until the cap is used. Each account's share = what is left of the cap ÷ the number of group accounts trading (each active account counts 1 + its `copied_accounts`), never less than `payout_min_usd`. Once an account's withdrawable profit (balance − `payout_baseline_usd`) covers its share, it takes no new entries and a Discord note says so; this share stop applies whatever `payout_clock_hold` says. **Near the cap** (owner 2026-10-09): a payout request still needs `payout_min_usd` of withdrawable profit, but the amount paid is min(requested, what is left of the cap); e.g. with $99,500 already paid out, the account must build $1,000 above $52,100 to request a payout and is paid $500. **The payout that reaches the cap** must be taken; it takes the account back to `payout_baseline_usd` ($52,100 on the Pro 50k) with the floor static at `drawdown_lock_usd` and nothing left to withdraw (profit beyond the cap is not paid), so recording it (Payout taken / `!payout`) sets the confirmed balance to `payout_baseline_usd` instead of subtracting the amount. A payout is never recorded above what is left of the cap. **Cap stop (final):** once the cap is used, entries stop on every account of the group. What is left of the cap is recalculated from the recorded payouts plus the current balances and `copied_accounts` every time the owner edits a balance or a copy count (e.g. he stopped copy trading but forgot to update the count), so such an edit can reopen trading. After the $100,000 payout MFFU moves the account to a live account, which is out of scope for now (owner 2026-10-09; §13 Q-Pro-6): there is no next $100,000 count, and working toward another payout applies only before the cap. Live: **Payout taken** / `!payout` asks how many accounts the payout was taken on (default 1 + `copied_accounts`, editable) and records amount × count toward the group; Live Status and the Accounts page show the group's total and what is left, and a Discord note goes out at 75% and 90% of the cap. Simulator: payouts are min(withdrawable, what is left of the cap); a funded run whose group reaches the cap is reported as `payout_cap_reached` with the days it took; since copy trading multiplies every payout, the report shows payouts and days to the cap for 1, 2 and 3 Pro accounts in total, leader included (MFFU's limit of 3 sim-funded at once, below), and for the owner's `copied_accounts`; each simulated payout follows the same share rule, so amount × count never exceeds what is left of the cap, and the simulator ends the funded run once the cap is reached.
  4. **Contract limit counted per contract** (`max_contracts_total`, owner 2026-10-08): the funded stage allows 5 contracts in total, a micro counting as a full contract, unlike the 10:1 mini-equivalent rule (`micro_mini_ratio`) of the evaluation and the 25k accounts. Sizing (§6.1) searches minis + micros ≤ the total; the Accounts page offers it for any account or stage (e.g. a later Pro 100k funded preset), and a scaling plan row may carry its own total.
  5. **Profit split** (`profit_split_pct`): reports only. The simulator and Live Status show both the gross payout and the trader's share; nothing in trading or sizing reads it.
  All of the owner's accounts are MyFundedFutures, up to 3 sim-funded at once (MFFU limit; the UI warns at a 4th, counting each account's `copied_accounts`). **What the owner does on the firm's site, and records in the UI:** activating a funded account after a pass (then he switches `phase` in the UI) and requesting each payout (then he clicks "Payout taken" and types the amount, or sends `!payout`, which records `payouts_taken`, the date and amount in `accounts.yaml`, subtracts the amount from the confirmed balance (the payout that reaches a payout cap group's cap sets it to `payout_baseline_usd` instead, new rule type 3) without touching today's P&L tallies, and is refused while the account has an open, pending or maybe-filled leg; the bot applies `after_first_payout`, with that stage's `profiles.yaml` entry, once `payouts_taken` ≥ 1, and measures profit since the last payout from the first confirmed balance after it). `payout_baseline_usd` = start + `funded_buffer_usd`, fixed from the day the funded phase starts (payout distance is simply negative until the buffer is cleared), and `payout_target_usd` defaults to `payout_min_usd`. The bot never requests a payout or activates an account. `stop_at_target` applies only while `phase: evaluation`. **Prop simulator stages:** an evaluation that passes or breaches starts a new evaluation the next session (account fees are the owner's and are not modelled); a funded account walks buffer → payouts, taking min(balance − baseline, `payout_max_usd`, what is left of the `payout_cap_group_usd`) at session end whenever balance − baseline ≥ max(`payout_min_usd`, `payout_target_usd`), the profit since the last payout is ≥ `payout_profit_since_last_usd`, the cycle has ≥ `payout_min_days` trading days, at least `payout_wait_calendar_days` calendar days have passed since the run's first funded trade (first payout) or the previous payout (later payouts) and the cap rules hold, then applies `after_first_payout` and, with `drawdown_lock_trigger: first_payout`, fixes the floor at `drawdown_lock_usd`. A funded account that breaches ends that funded run (payouts already taken are kept) and the stage restarts from its preset start balance the next session; payouts and breaches per funded run are reported. **Inactivity in the simulator:** an account with no trade for `max_inactive_days` calendar days (weekends and holidays included) is closed by the firm: the prop simulator counts it as a failure (`inactive_close`), reported separately from drawdown breaches, and lives mode, profile and `last_life_top_pct` comparisons include it (the bot never places a trade just to stay active). **Inactivity alert:** after `inactivity_alert_days` (start 5) calendar days without a trade on an account with `max_inactive_days`, a daily Discord alert gives the days left. **Stops that outlast the inactivity limit** (owner 2026-10-09): when a `payout_clock_hold: pause`, a share stop or a cap stop (new rule types 1 and 3) would keep an account from trading past `max_inactive_days`, the owner gets a warning only: a Discord warning live, and the simulator flags it (reported apart from `inactive_close`). The bot never trades just to stay active.
  **MFFU Tier-1 news rule** (no positions or orders 2 minutes either side of FOMC, FOMC minutes, the Employment Report and CPI on funded accounts): covered on every account by §9 News blackouts.
  **Larger accounts later (owner):** the owner may move to MFFU 50k or 100k accounts of the same types (Pro 50k is a preset since 2026-10-08). They need **no retraining**: the models work in R, and only the account's rules change. He enters each new account on the Accounts page (start balance, drawdown and lock, target, consistency, DLL, buffer and payouts, max minis / micros and how the firm counts them (10:1 mini-equivalent or a total contract count), risk per trade); the presets are only starting points and every field stays editable. A new account whose rules match no existing account or preset stage is stale and refuses entries until its overnight threshold sweep has run (§6.8), so it can trade from the morning after it is entered.
- **Daily profit cap, by mode** (`profit_cap_mode` = how the bot limits how much it makes in one day; the UI labels it "Daily profit limit"):
  - `consistency` (owner: MFFU Rapid EOD evaluation 30% × $1,500 = **$450/day**; Builder funded 50% × $2,100 = **$1,050/day** until the first payout, then 50% × $1,000 = **$500/day**; Pro 50k evaluation 50% × $3,000 = **$1,500/day**): cap = `consistency_pct × profit_target_usd`, fixed for the current phase or payout cycle. MFFU measures consistency on net daily P&L, so the cap applies after commissions and fees, and gross can run slightly above it. This caps the **day**: a single trade can make at most the cap, and once the day reaches it the bot stops.
  - `none`: no profit cap.
- **Daily loss limit (DLL):** a cap on how much the account may lose in one day, separately from max drawdown. The owner's only DLL is MFFU Builder's $600, which is **soft**: a breach ends the day, it doesn't fail the account; Rapid EOD has none (`dll_enabled: false`). With `dll_enabled: true` the bot:
  1. **Size to what's left:** `risk = min(normal risk, remaining_allowance)`, costs included, where `remaining_allowance = daily cap + today's net P&L` and the daily cap is `bot_daily_loss_cap_usd`, or the firm's DLL when that is null. Example: DLL $600 (Builder), risk $333 → after a $333 loss the next trade risks ~$267; if it also loses, the day is over. A winning trade earlier in the day adds room, but risk never exceeds the normal amount. If not even 1 micro fits the stop, skip.
  2. **Not counted as lives:** lives mode (§6.5b) counts drawdown only.
  3. **Daily stop:** no new entries once the remaining allowance is used up (or after `max_losses_per_day` losses); the allowance resets each session date (trading ends at 15:59).
- **No DLL (`dll_enabled: false`):** trades are never sized down for the day and there is no daily stop, unless the owner sets `bot_daily_loss_cap_usd` (then it works exactly like a DLL, items 1 and 3) or `max_losses_per_day`.
- **Account state and the drawdown floor:** live, the owner types the firm's balance and current drawdown floor (liquidation level, shown on the MFFU dashboard) on Live Status each morning, or from his phone with `!balance <account> <balance> <floor>` (§9c), confirming the account has no open position or working order; **entries stay blocked until he does**. He may enter them again on any account at any time the account is flat (e.g. after a trade of his own; owner 2026-10-09); the new values replace the bot's working balance and floor from then on. An entry is refused and asked again if the floor is below start − `drawdown_usd`, above `drawdown_lock_usd` (when the account has a lock, except with `drawdown_lock_trigger: first_payout`, which uses the range in new rule type 2 above; with no lock, as on every MFFU evaluation, there is no upper limit but the balance) or ≥ the balance, or the balance differs from the bot's estimate by more than that account's largest possible day (a typo check; typing the same values again confirms them, for a day with the owner's own trades). These daily values (`current_balance_usd`, `current_floor_usd`, P&L adjustments) are stored in `<paths.live_dir>/account_state.json`, not in the committed `accounts.yaml`; the prop simulator keeps its own `eod_high_water`. The bot shows its own estimate for comparison but never accepts it by itself. Intraday, the working balance = the confirmed morning balance + today's estimated net P&L (a maybe-filled or unknown leg counted at its full stop loss); lives, `stop_at_target`, auto-protect, the scaling tier and the breach check use it, and the next owner confirmation replaces it. `drawdown_remaining = balance − floor` (used by lives); profit = `balance − start_balance` (scaling tiers; the owner confirms the active tier from the firm dashboard); payout distance and auto-protect use `balance − payout_baseline_usd`. The owner doesn't trade the leader manually during the session, or, if he does, enters a P&L adjustment on Live Status or, once the account is flat, its new balance and floor. The prop simulator computes the floor itself: `start_balance − drawdown_usd` (static) or `min(eod_high_water − drawdown_usd, drawdown_lock_usd)` (eod_trailing, updated each session end; when `drawdown_lock_usd` is null, floor = `eod_high_water − drawdown_usd`; with `drawdown_lock_trigger: first_payout`, `eod_high_water − drawdown_usd` until the first payout, then `drawdown_lock_usd`). A breach (simulator and live) = equity ≤ floor, counting open P&L.
- **Phase-aware:** when an account passes, the owner flips `phase` and its cap mode in the UI. With `stop_at_target: true`, an evaluation stops taking entries once it reaches its target and the bot's own tally also meets the consistency rule and `min_trading_days` (more trading only adds risk); if not, it keeps trading under the daily cap until they are met.
- **Backtest:** the prop simulator replays each account's own per-account selections (§6.3) through its rules (pass = profit target reached, consistency held, ≥ `min_trading_days`, no breach) and reports pass rate, days to pass, breach rate, and payout-eligibility. It also **bootstraps** with whole days/weeks as blocks (losing streaks cluster in bad regimes, so shuffling single trades would understate them): resamples whole days of decision points (with all their pairs) and re-runs the per-account selection and state machine on each path (`bootstrap_paths`, start 500), since which trade an account takes depends on its lives, caps and phase; gate-account bootstraps (no state) resample daily trade results. One historical path is a single draw. The thresholds (§6.5b) are tuned by the money score (§6.5b) subject to the bootstrapped three-loss rate ≤ `max_three_loss_rate`; breach probability is reported only.

### 6.8 Risk profiles (owner's quick dial)
Each account has a **risk profile** the owner can switch any time in the Control Panel (applies from the next trade). A profile moves two things together: **how much is risked** and **how picky the bot is**.

| Profile | Risk per trade | Selectivity | Typical use |
|---|---|---|---|
| Aggressive | multiplier 1.0 (full risk, capped at drawdown ÷ 3) | base thresholds, slightly looser | fresh evaluation, wants to pass fast |
| **Normal** (default) | multiplier 1.0 | backtest-chosen thresholds (§6.5b) | everyday |
| Conservative | multiplier 0.5 | stricter: only roughly the top third of setups by calibrated EV | after a drawdown, or a choppy week |
| Protect | multiplier 0.33 | strictest: only roughly the top 10% of setups by calibrated EV | funded account close to a payout |
| Custom | owner types the risk $ directly (`custom_risk_usd`, ≤ drawdown ÷ 3) | owner sets `min_ev_r`, `min_target_prob` directly | anything else |

- The exact thresholds for each profile come from the **threshold sweep** (§6.5b), not guesses — Normal: the §6.5b objective; Aggressive: among `threshold_grid` points with both thresholds at or below Normal's, the one with the most trades, kept only if its paired total-R-per-month difference against Normal on the tuning months is > 0 within `max_three_loss_rate` (else Aggressive = Normal); Conservative and Protect: the `profile_ev_quantiles` (67% Conservative, 90% Protect) quantile of calibrated EV among the decision-point candidates that pass the account's Normal thresholds in the fold's tuning months (same method as the last-life cutoff, §6.5b). The sweep runs per account (with that account's rules and `max_three_loss_rate`) and writes `models/profiles.yaml`, keyed account (or preset stage) → stage (current / after_first_payout) → profile → {`min_ev_r`, `min_target_prob`, EV cutoff, last-life cutoff}, each per-account sweep covering both stages; an account added later, or one whose sweep-relevant fields (rules, caps, commissions, `max_three_loss_rate`, `last_life_top_pct`, λ, `risk_usd_per_trade`, flat-by, `breakeven_enabled`, `sim_fills`, `lives_mode`) are edited, is marked stale. A stale **new** account refuses entries until its sweep has run, unless its sweep-relevant fields (above) equal an existing account's: then it copies that account's `profiles.yaml` entry and is not stale (a replacement leader can trade the next morning); an edited account keeps its previous thresholds until the sweep finishes. Edits that reduce exposure — lower risk, lower max size or caps, a DLL turned on or lowered, an earlier flat-by — apply from the next trade with the previous thresholds; editing `copied_accounts`, `payout_clock_hold` or `profit_split_pct` is not sweep-relevant, needs no threshold re-run and applies from the next trade (owner 2026-10-09); only threshold changes and edits that raise risk wait for the sweep and take effect at the next session after it. The sweep runs overnight through `nqbot run sweep --account <account>` ("Re-run sweep" button queues it; `nqbot setup-tasks` also registers `nqbot run queue --until 07:30` at `archive_time` + 30 min daily to run queued sweeps, and saving an account that becomes stale outside 08:30–16:15 on a trading day starts the queue at once; the step-15/19 sweeps and the production fit also write `profiles.yaml` entries for every §6.7 preset stage, so a new account matching a preset stage copies that entry and is not stale; Custom-profile edits never need one), and the UI shows what they mean, from out-of-fold months only (months 5–6 before step 20; months 7–9 plus the sealed set after it; the page shows the period) — e.g. "Protect: ~2 trades/week, 71% win rate, avg 1.4R, chance of a losing day 18%."
- **Auto-protect (optional, per funded account):** `auto_protect_within_usd` — when the account is within that many dollars of its payout target, the bot switches to Protect by itself and posts a Discord note; it switches back after the payout is requested (owner clicks "Payout taken" or sends `!payout`).
- Risk never goes **above** drawdown ÷ 3 in any profile; the static 1/3 rule (§6.1) is the ceiling.
- Lives mode (§6.5b) still applies on top: on the last life, the stricter of the two rules wins.
- The prop simulator reports each profile's pass rate / payout rate per account, so the owner can see the tradeoff before choosing.

---

## 7. Training and backtest rules (non-negotiable)

1. **No lookahead.** Every feature at time t uses only data with timestamp ≤ t. A touch at second t is tested against the levels from the latest snapshot live would have had by then (applied at τ + `map_latency_sec`, §3.1 Ingest; the unit test uses τ), and bar-based features use only bars closed by t; a unit test shifts the data by one second and one bar and asserts the features at t don't change. Anything chosen from outcomes (chart-level zone widths, the NDXP band, detector grids, and the execution parameters `entry_ttl_sec`, `stop_buffer` (a stop beyond a mapped level uses at least `stop_buffer_floor`, §5.1), `abs_stop_buffer`, `abs_stop_window_sec`, and the order type per entry style) is frozen from development months 1–4 (reaction-distance report run at the start of step 13; owner confirms the NDXP band and `dp_band` there) and never refit. Profiles "so far", not full-session. Dark-pool prints count only from the next session's pre-open (prints with `timestamp` ≤ the previous session's 16:00 ET, or earlier under `dp_lag_sessions`, §3.1). Unit-test this. Every order, `exit` and `breakeven` takes effect `order_latency_sec` after the decision (§5.5b).
2. **Walk-forward**: train on older months, test on the next unseen month, roll forward. Never tune on test months. **Schedule (the 9 unsealed months: development months 1–6 + walk-forward test months 7–9, ledger in rule 2b):** the lower models' (structure, trigger, day type) predictions for months 1–4 are **cross-fitted**: each month 1–4 is predicted by lower models trained on the other months 1–4 only (day-blocked, purged); these predictions only ever train the pair scorer, never score anything. From month 5 on, month k is predicted by lower models trained on months 1…k−1 only (expanding window). The pair scorer that predicts month k (5 ≤ k ≤ m−1) trains on months 1…k−1; test month m reuses the scorer that predicted month m−1 (trained on months 1…m−2); calibration / thresholds / breakeven are tuned on the out-of-fold predictions for months 5…m−1 (the **tuning months** of fold m), and month m is scored. *Folds for test months 7, 8, 9:* scorer on 1–5 / 1–6 / 1–7, tuning on 5–6 / 5–7 / 5–8.
   - **Sealed set:** scored by fold 10: lower models through month 9, scorer on 1–8, tuning on 5–9, applied unchanged to every sealed and top-up month. That leaves about 3 walk-forward test months (~60+ trades at ≥ 1/day) plus the sealed set (3 months + the top-up months); confidence intervals will be wide and are always reported.
   - **Per fold, in order** (every choice except step (1)'s searches is made on the tuning months 5…m−1, by the §6.5b objective, re-simulated from the stored paths, §6.5): (1) `recency_halflife_months` (it weights the pair scorer's training rows only) and LightGBM's small parameter grid (§7.1), searched **once per scorer** (for the scorer trained on months 1…k−1) inside its own training months — the LightGBM grid by day-blocked cross-validated log-loss, and `recency_halflife_months` by forward validation (train on months 1…k−2, score month k−1 by log-loss), since random-day CV can't reward weighting recent months, then cached and reused by every later fold; then the enabled entry styles, chosen from the pre-declared `style_set_grid` (≤ 6 sets, approved at step 6) by re-simulating the trades that the no-breakeven (3-outcome) scorers for k = 5…m−1 (each trained on months 1…k−1, cached across folds) select with each set (a selection filter, no retraining). Trades are selected with `settings.yaml → tuning_start` (start `min_ev_r` 0.20, `min_target_prob` 0.40, applied to raw scores at default costs, λ = 0); (2) choose the breakeven rule by re-simulating the trades those same no-breakeven scorers select at the start thresholds; (3) relabel with this fold's rule and train the 4-outcome scorer for every k in 5…m−1 (each on months 1…k−1, so every tuning month has an out-of-fold prediction under the fold's rule; cached and reused whenever an earlier fold chose the same rule); the k = m−1 scorer scores month m; (4) recalibrate on those out-of-fold predictions, then sweep thresholds and set the last-life cutoff (`last_life_top_pct: auto` picks 10/20/33% here) and the risk-profile EV cutoffs (§6.5b, §6.8).
   - **Choices vs scores:** everything above is chosen inside each fold; test month m is only scored. The only decisions made on the months 7–9 scores are a few whole-system yes/no choices — ablation cuts (rule 5, §7.2 item 2), the target-selection policy (§6.3 Proof) and the step-20 order-flow decision — and the sealed set checks them. The gameplan check (§5.6) runs on all scored months after the sealed score; a production fit that adds gameplan inputs is validated only live (steps 24–25).
   - **Training order inside every fold:** day-type model first; the structure model's day-type input is the day-type model's own out-of-fold prediction (cross-fitted for months 1–4, nested: when month h ∈ 1–4 is held out, the day-type inputs of its training rows come from day-type models trained without both h and the row's own month; expanding after); then the structure and trigger models; then the pair scorer.
   - **Fold j** scores month j (for a sealed set, every month of that set, unchanged) = lower models through month j−1, pair scorer on months 1…j−2, tuning months 5…j−1; folds 7–9 are the walk-forward test folds, fold 10 scores the sealed set, and the production fit is fold N+1 (it first trains the expanding-window scorers for months 10…N that its tuning months need).
   - **Production fit** (runs after the step-20 sealed score passes, and again after a new sealed set or a round-2 re-check passes, after the step-21 and step-25 re-checks, after a `map_latency_sec` raise (§3.1) and at each §7.1 Retraining refit — not after step 16): fold N+1 over all N scored months, sealed and top-up months included — out-of-fold lower-model predictions through month N train and tune the scorer, while the deployed lower models are trained on months 1…N; pair scorer on months 1…N−1, per-fold steps (1)–(4) on the tuning months 5…N — and write `models/` and `models/profiles.yaml`. This fit is not re-scored; steps 24–25 validate it live.
   - **Purge and embargo:** drop events whose outcome window overlaps the train/test boundary and leave a gap of one session, so overlapping trades can't leak information across the split.

2b. **Locked final test set and the month ledger.** The ledger is written into `settings.yaml` (`months`) on the day the SSD is set up, just before step 7 (§12 step 4b), and every step uses it:
   - **Development months 1–6:** the only months for hand-made work — detector or definition fixes, sample days, plots, review cards, regression tests — and for every report that can lead to a hand-made rule or feature change (SHAP and importance, win rates by regime/day type/confluence, hold rates, zone-width reports (no gameplan reports before step 20: months 1–9 have no gameplan days; from step 20 on scored months, §5.6), flip and near-cutoff node reports): tables built from labels use months 1–6; model explanations use the fold-7 models (lower models through month 6, pair scorer through month 5) on months 1–6; review cards show out-of-fold scores only (months 5–6 from the expanding-window scorers; months 1–4 show the cross-fitted lower-model scores and no pair score). Outcome-based choices that are frozen (rule 1, §7.1 detector tuning) use months 1–4.
   - **Walk-forward test months 7–9** (after a re-seal or a round-2 fix: every scored month from 7 up to the month before the new sealed set or re-check month, excluding live months used to justify round-2 fixes; gates and the pass interval then use all of them): scored only by the automated walk-forward (rule 2), never looked at by hand; reports on them show only gate metrics and walk-forward scores. A test month may be trained or tuned on (as a later fold's tuning month) only after it has been scored. All gate numbers before step 20 come from these months.
   - **Sealed months 10–12, plus the top-up months** (every complete month after the window, pulled at step 18 and again at step 20 for months completed since): kept out of **all** development work, reports, charts and summaries until step 20 (exceptions, without outcomes: pull and data-coverage reports — days served, `meta.resolution`, credits, costs, verification, no prices or outcomes; the step-9 gameplan parsing and validation; the step-7 and step-22 live-feed measurements — stream cadence and latency, history parity, Tempest availability time; the step-7 `top-prints` cross-check uses development-month dates), then scored **once**, together, by fold 10 of the model that will go live (rule 2). **Pass** = the sealed set's mean R per trade after costs is > 0 and not below the lower bound (10th percentile) of the months 7–9 day-block bootstrap interval. The production fit (rule 2) then adds them to training, so every later model is judged on months it has never seen (step 27, §7.1 Retraining).
   - **Re-seal (if the sealed score fails at step 20 and they are used to justify a fix):** they become walk-forward test months (still never used for hand-made work), and the new sealed set is the first ≥ `min_new_sealed_months` (start 2) complete months after the fix is frozen, pulled when they exist and scored by fold j, where j is the first new sealed month.
   - **Months completed after step 20** are not in the production fit; they are pulled at step 27 (as in step 18). Live months used to justify round-2 fixes are training data only, never walk-forward test months.
   - **Live months** (step 24 onward) are new unseen months. Review round 2 (step 24) shows cards from them but only produces proposals. Fixes are applied after the step-25 gate is scored, those live months then count as development months (hand-made work allowed), and the fixed model must pass the re-check in step 27.
   - *Example — ledger written 1–20 October 2026:* months 1–6 = Oct 2025–Mar 2026 (October 2025 starts at the first day still inside Skylit's 365-day reach), 7–9 = Apr–Jun 2026, sealed 10–12 = Jul–Sep 2026, top-up = Oct 2026 onward (complete months only).
   - The sealed score is the only truly honest score; if it disagrees with the walk-forward results, the walk-forward overfit.
3. Models: start with win-rate tables, then **LightGBM**. Calibrate probabilities on the fold's tuning months (rule 2): isotonic when the calibration rows' weights (§6.3) sum to ≥ ~1,000 (filled decision-point candidates, §6.3), otherwise sigmoid (Platt) — isotonic overfits small samples. The 3- or 4-outcome pair scorer is calibrated per outcome (one-vs-rest, same method and rows), then the calibrated probabilities are renormalised to sum to 1 per pair; reliability is reported per outcome; `E[R | timeout]` is not calibrated. Training rows are weighted so **each touch group counts once** (weights in §5.4 Overlapping events; an event with 40 pairs must not outweigh one with 4), and folds are split by whole days so rows from the same session never sit on both sides.

3b. **Every reported number comes from a sequential policy simulator** that replays the days in order with the exact live rules: one trade per account (NQ and/or MNQ legs), pending-entry time-to-live, setup windows, blackouts, daily profit/loss caps, lives mode, flat-by, slippage and commissions. Scoring every event independently would overstate trades per month and profit (overlapping setups can't all be taken live).
4. Report per run (`reports/`): **total R per month (account R, §6.1) and the money score** (primary), expected R per trade after costs (secondary), win rate vs the breakeven win rate for each trade's RR, calibration plot, trade count, max drawdown in R and in dollars at the configured risk, results by regime and day type, feature importance. Breakdowns (regime, day type, confluence, stack count, entry style, stop/target type) and feature importance / SHAP cover development months 1–6 only (rule 2b); on months 7–9 only the gate metrics, the rule-5 ablation and the §6.3 policy comparison.
5. **Ablation**: remove one pre-declared **group** at a time — trigger model, Trinity, confluence features, day-type input, area events, stop candidate 5, map shape (owner 2026-10-05: the six Skylit concepts beach ball, magnets, air pockets, King dominance/migration, rainbow road and whipsaw; memberships of every group in `docs/feature_spec.md` F16.8) — each arm running the full per-fold pipeline (reusing the full system's per-scorer LightGBM grid point and half-life), and cut by backward elimination: remove the group with the lowest point estimate of the total-R-per-month difference (with − without, paired day-block bootstrap on months 7–9) if it is ≤ 0, re-run, repeat; then the all-cuts system is compared with the full system (paired), and lower bounds are reported. Cuts of structure groups (Trinity, confluence, day-type, map shape) apply to the fallback system too. The ablation runs once, at step 19, over all seven groups (not at step 15). The list is fixed before step 15 (groups added at step 19 included) and the number of comparisons is printed with the result; the sealed set then checks the cuts. Individual features are never kept or dropped by their months 7–9 score (§7.2 item 2). Gameplan inputs are not in this ablation (months 7–9 have no gameplan days): the §5.6 gameplan check and the monthly-retrain ablation handle them.
6. Minimum sample: don't trust a setup bucket with < ~100 events; report confidence intervals.

### 7.1 How the training works

**The dataset.** The backtest replays history tick by tick (Databento trades) from the first backtest on. `data/processed/events.parquet` has one row per touch (§5.4 touch rule), with features as of the touch — what the structure model uses. Values measured after the touch (`swept`, sweep depth and time beyond, audit depth and time inside, the fib level reached) are pair columns computed as of each pair's `decision_ts` (null for the structure model and for style (d)) and never stored in `events.parquet`. `data/processed/pairs.parquet` has one row per (event_id, entry style, firing n, order type, stop candidate, target candidate); a **pair** = (event_id, entry style, firing n, stop candidate, target candidate) executed with the frozen order type for its style, and only those rows train the scorer. Each entry style has its own `decision_ts` (one per firing for styles (a) and (b), §5.7) (when its absorption fires (a), its order-flow trigger fires (b), the audit or sweep reclaims (c), or at the touch (d)); pair features are computed as of `decision_ts`, and labels start at the fill after it, exactly as live. Columns = inputs + what happened afterward (labels). Pair rows run to millions per year, so event features are stored once in `events.parquet` and joined at training time; `pairs.parquet` holds only pair columns. Targets per event are capped at `max_target_candidates` (start 10 nearest distinct zones with net RR ≥ `pair_build_min_rr` at default costs) plus the 1:2.5 baseline, and stop candidates within `stop_merge_ticks` (start 2) of each other are merged. This table is the only thing the models learn from.

**Algorithm (decided).** Start with simple win-rate tables by stack count, member type and entry style (readable, sanity check). The production model is **LightGBM** with conservative settings (shallow trees, strong regularization, a small parameter grid searched inside the training folds only). A **logistic regression baseline** is trained alongside on the same features — about ten lines of code — purely as a cheat detector: if LightGBM can't beat it on unseen months (total R per month, paired day-block bootstrap), the extra complexity is fitting noise. Report-only: the owner is alerted, and it never switches the model class. No CatBoost, no large hyperparameter searches. Good with mixed inputs, missing values (no gameplan that day), and interactions (regime × confluence). Fast to train on a laptop; < 1 ms to predict.

**Each model, separately:**

| Model | Rows | Inputs | Label | What it learns |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Structure | every level-zone touch | zone makeup (stack count, member-type flags, each member's node size and `tests_so_far` / `holds_so_far`, and the proximity features of §5.4: zone width, member spread, each type's distance from the centre, distance to the next zone above/below), velocity lean, daily vol context (§5.2), regime, rug/pika/Trinity flags, day-type P(trend) as of the latest snapshot ≤ t, distance to VWAP/bands (ATR-scaled), daily vs weekly profile, day memory, time of day, calendar, gameplan features (§5.6; only once the gameplan check enables them, else omitted) | **held** = after the touch, price moves ≥ `hold_reaction_pts` in the hold direction, measured from the touch's extreme (the deepest price reached into or past the zone) with a target-style trade-through (§5.5b), before the zone is crossed; **broke** = the zone is crossed, i.e. every active member crossed by its own rule (§5.4: GEX nodes trade past the band; chart levels a settled 1-minute candle beyond the band, single prints against the band frozen at the touch; highs/lows after the sweep window — a sweep that reclaims is not a break, and is labelled held only if the hold reaction follows before a cross or `hold_window_min`; golden zone a settled 1-minute candle past 0.786, §5.8; gameplan zones their `invalidation`, e.g. a 1-minute candle settling past `settled_1m_above`, else trading past the far edge); neither within `hold_window_min` = timeout | which Skylit + chart structures actually hold, and under which conditions |
| Trigger (Databento) | order-flow events inside a structure zone or VAH/VAL area, at every structure type (absorption, exhaustion, delta divergence, imbalance) | big trades (count, size, side, price progress after them), delta, CVD slope, aggressive volume vs no-progress, refill counts (mbp-1), top-of-book features (mbp-1, §5.7), imbalance ratios, seconds since touch | +`trig_fav_ticks` in the trade direction before −`trig_adv_ticks` within `trig_window_sec` (start 120 s; a label parameter, fixed), measured from the best ask (longs) / bid (shorts) at `decision_ts` + `order_latency_sec`, the window starting then | which order-flow signals mean "now," and how much they add on top of structure |
| Day type | one row per session per `daytype_snapshot_min` (start 15) from 10:00, inputs as of that time (no full-day inputs) | IB width, VWAP and POC crosses so far, time above/below VWAP, new highs/lows made so far, rotations, regime, daily vol context (§5.2), realized range so far vs the expected move | range vs trend day (owner): **range** = price keeps cutting through the midpoints (VWAP, POC); **trend** = price stays on one side of them, bouncing off them, while making consistent new highs (up) or new lows (down). EOD label (RTH only; a cross = a 1-minute close on the other side): trend if crosses of the 09:30-anchored VWAP ≤ `trend_max_vwap_crosses` (start 2) and crosses of the developing daily POC ≤ `trend_max_poc_crosses` (start 2) and the close is in the outer `trend_close_pct` (start 20%) of the RTH range in the direction of the new highs/lows; else range. The owner may refine this at step 6. Known only at EOD; predicted from partial-day inputs. Few labels (one per session), so: rows are weighted so each session counts once, and it is a regularised logistic regression on at most `daytype_max_features` (start 8) inputs chosen at step 6 (fold 7 has too few sessions for anything richer); a LightGBM day-type model is added later as a §7.1 Retraining code change (the candidate must beat the live model). The report prints its out-of-fold log-loss vs the base rate | when levels get pushed through (heavy ranging/trending days) |
| **Pair scorer (combiner)** | pair (event × entry style × firing × stop candidate × target candidate, §6.2, §6.3, dataset above) | the models above (out-of-fold; trigger inputs = the trigger probability of this pair's own firing for styles a/b, plus, for every style, the maximum and the latest trigger probability of this event's firings with ts ≤ `decision_ts`, null if none; same-second firings of different style-(b) detectors form one decision point whose trigger input is their maximum), zone makeup (same features as the structure model), entry style, stop type/distance, target type/distance (ATR and expected moves), confluence at target, daily vol context (§5.2), target-node velocity, obstacles in between, time of day, gameplan features (§5.6; only once the gameplan check enables them, else omitted) | **target first / stop first / timeout** (+ breakeven exit when breakeven is on, §6.6), plus `E[R \| timeout]` and time-to-target | the final probability per candidate pair; which stop/target pairs work where |

There is **no separate target or stop model**: the pair scorer *is* the target/stop decision, because the probability of reaching a target only makes sense for a given stop. Stop behaviour (overshoot past stop, stopped-then-target) is reported from the stored paths (§6.5) and used to tune `stop_buffer`, not modelled separately. Breakeven is chosen by re-simulating stored paths (§6.4). Final trade = entry + the pair with the highest risk-adjusted expected R (min 1:1, P ≥ floor), sized by §6.1.

**Stacking without cheating.** The pair scorer must never see a lower model's prediction for data that model trained on. The schedule (cross-fitting for months 1–4, expanding window after) is in §7 rule 2. The three lower models stay separate because each learns its own label from far more rows than there are trades.

**Tuning detector parameters.** Structure parameters (e.g. `zone_proximity`, `confluence_tol`, `sweep_max_depth`) at step 13, after the reaction report, and order-flow parameters (`abs_*`, `imb_ratio`, …) at step 19: for each grid value, rebuild events on months 1–4 and pick by day-blocked cross-validated **total net R per month** on months 1–4, using the bootstrap policy (stop candidate 2, or candidate 1 for area events; nearest target ≥ 1:1, §6.3) on events whose cross-validated predicted hold probability (trigger probability for order-flow parameters) is above the fold median; report the trade count. Entry-trigger parameters (`abs_*` except `abs_stop_*`, `imb_*`, `exh_*`, `div_*` at step 19; `sweep_reclaim_bars` at step 13 on style (c) trades) are scored on their style's trades only; `sweep_max_depth` and `flip_margin` (searched in three groups, F16.4) are structure parameters scored on all events at step 13; `abs_stop_buffer` and `abs_stop_window_sec` are frozen at step 19 on all entry styles' trades with stop candidate 5 in place of candidate 2 (candidate 1 for area events). Log-loss is used only to compare model settings on a fixed event set with fixed labels. Label parameters (`hold_*`, `trig_*`) stay at the owner-approved start values. Write the winners to `features.yaml` marked `frozen_from: months 1-4`. Test months are never used. **Execution parameters** (`entry_ttl_sec`, `stop_buffer`, `abs_stop_buffer`, `abs_stop_window_sec`, the order type per entry style) are frozen the same way at step 13 (order-flow styles: step 19), with the same single-style policies (step 13), re-simulated from the stored paths, so the per-fold pipeline never has to retrain for them.

**Which entry style trades.** `enabled_entry_styles` (settings.yaml) is chosen inside each fold from the styles built so far (Phase D: (c) and (d); step 19: all four; §7 rule 2), and the production fit writes the final set; if step 20 fails, it is limited to (c) and (d). The model learns which entry style works best (the pair scorer has entry style as an input). Owner's ranking, used only to break near-ties: order flow (a, b) > audit/sweep at the zone edge (c) > plain entry at the structure (d). When several enabled styles are eligible at the same moment, the highest EV wins; the ranking (a = b > c > d) decides only when their EVs are within `style_tie_ev_r` (start 0.05R). If a lower-ranked style is eligible first, it trades (no waiting for a higher-ranked one). While a sent entry is pending or "maybe filled" the other styles for that event wait; after any fill they are skipped for the rest of the event; if it is removed cleanly and unfilled, they become eligible again. The rule applies per account, and the simulator does the same.

**Calibration.** Raw model scores are mapped (method per §7 rule 3, fitted on the filled decision-point candidates in the fold's tuning months, §6.3, §7 rule 2) so that "0.70" really means ~70% of such trades hit target.

**Checking what it learned.** Every training run writes a report: feature importance and SHAP plots (which inputs drive predictions and in which direction), win rates by regime/day type/confluence level, calibration plot. (Months 1–6 only; months 7–9 show only gate metrics, rule 2b.) Owner reads these: if the model learned something that contradicts experience, either a feature is mis-defined (fix it) or it's a real finding (keep it).

**Retraining.** Live engine logs every event in the same format. New months' rows are rebuilt from the pulled history with the backtest code, as at step 18; live logs are used only for live-vs-backtest checks, drift monitoring and fill/slippage measurement. Each monthly retrain also re-runs the gameplan ablation (§5.6) and drops the gameplan inputs if they hurt (part of the declared retrain procedure, not a "change" below). Every `retrain_every_months` (start 1; owner: every 1–3 months is fine; data top-ups still run monthly so Skylit history never ages out), for the newest complete month M: if no code or feature changed, the live model is simply refit through M (production-fit offsets). If anything changed (new feature, fix, new data source), the months that motivated the change (review cards, drift alerts) are not used to judge it: the candidate and the unchanged model are both re-run with the same fold offsets and scored on the next complete month(s) after the change is frozen (paired, like step 27's fold j); the candidate must have the higher money score and meet the step-16 criteria, and any engine or feature code change re-passes the gate-23 replay; only then is it refit through M and put live.

### 7.2 Further accuracy practices
(Labels with full paths, realistic fills, costs, expected-R thresholds, the sequential simulator and bootstrapped prop simulation are covered above.)
1. **Recency weighting:** weight recent months more (`recency_halflife_months`, tuned) because markets drift.
2. **Prune:** no per-feature pruning search; LightGBM's regularisation handles weak features, and only the pre-declared groups of §7 rule 5 are cut.
3. **Live drift monitor:** track live calibration monthly; alert when a stated 0.7 bucket wins far from 70%. Retraining follows §7.1 Retraining.
4. **Review rounds** (§8) after every retrain that changes features or code.

Every extra tweak evaluated on the same months is a chance to fit noise; the locked test set (§7 rule 2b) is the defence.

---

## 8. Review loop: owner judges scenarios

Purpose: make sure the detectors see what the owner sees, and capture judgment he didn't think to write down.

**What the owner sees (one card per setup, from development months 1–6 only, §7 rule 2b; round 2 uses the live demo trades):**
- NQ chart around the moment with VWAP + bands, daily/weekly VAH/VAL/POC, IB high/low and fib, ON levels, mapped Skylit nodes, gameplan zone if any
- Skylit maps (QQQ, NDXP, SPY, SPX) at that minute
- Detector readings: node type/freshness, rug/pika/Trinity, regime, confluence, day-type inputs so far (P(trend) from step 15; never the end-of-day label), order-flow trigger (once step 17 has built them)
- The proposed trade: entry, stop, target, entry style
- The outcome stays **hidden** until he answers, so he judges the setup, not the result

**What the owner answers:**
- **Take / Skip**
- **Why** (one or two sentences, in his own words)
- Optional: how he'd enter (order-flow trigger / audit / plain at the level), where he'd put stop and target, confidence 1–5

Saved to `data/reviews/*.parquet`. Cards are a spread of examples for the owner to judge, so the detectors can be checked against what he sees: high, low and borderline scorers once a model exists; in step 14, before any model exists, a stratified sample across stack counts, level types, entry styles and times of day (entry style (c) if an audit or sweep occurred, else (d); stop = candidate 2, far edge + `stop_buffer`; target = the v1 bootstrap, §6.3), plus valid setups that lost (losing is normal when targets are far) and a few setups the owner would never take (to test the filters).

**How answers become changes:**
| Owner's answer | Becomes | Who decides |
|---|---|---|
| "That's not a real rug, the purple node is too small" | Fix to a detector definition/threshold | Claude Code proposes, owner approves (hardcoded rule) |
| A reason that repeats and isn't captured ("already ran 80 pts into it", "lunch chop") | New feature | Owner names it; the model learns its weight from outcomes |
| Outcomes revealed after answering | Comparison of owner's calls vs model's odds | Shows where discretion beats the model and vice versa |

Claude Code reads all reasons after each round, groups repeats, and proposes concrete changes ("6 of 9 skips mention lunch hours → add time-of-day feature"). Approved changes → spec updated → events rebuilt → retrain.

Rounds: 50–100 cards in step 14 (backtest setups), a second round in step 24 (live demo trades). The outcome model is **never** trained to copy owner labels; it trains on what price actually did.

---

## 9. Runtime (live)

- The engine (one asyncio process, plus the guard, §9 Supervision) during session hours: Skylit stream (`/v1/stream`) + Databento Live → incremental feature updates (microseconds) → model called only when price reaches a qualifying zone → decision.
- **Data is always tick-level.** Every NQ/ES trade is processed all session (cheap); 1-minute bars are built from those ticks for the bar-based rules (audits on 1-minute bars, GEX flips on 5-minute bars). What switches on near a level is not finer data but the **work**: order-flow detectors (§5.7) and the models run only while price is inside a zone ± `zone_proximity` or a VAH/VAL area (§5.3).
- Latency budget: data in ~tens of ms; model < 1 ms; Skylit map as soon as the stream pushes it; Discord push 1–3 s.
- **Pre-open warm-up and restarts:** live needs state from before the session (the 18:00 ET VWAP and daily profile, ON high/low, PDH/PDL, the weekly profile, unfilled single prints, multi-day freshness counts, ATR, realized vol, the big-trade threshold, the opening-range averages, the trailing `dp_lookback_days` of dark-pool prints, Tempest's previous close). It starts from the latest evening checkpoint (`state_<date>.parquet`, §11) plus any sessions since. A pre-open job (a Windows Task Scheduler task registered by `nqbot setup-tasks` at `preopen_start` (08:30 ET; re-registered if the Windows time zone isn't America/New_York) with "Run with highest privileges", "Wake the computer to run this task" and "Run task as soon as possible after a scheduled start is missed", and both AC-power conditions turned off (`archive_live` is registered the same way); it exits at once on non-trading days from `calendar.yaml`; done by 09:00 ET, and the checklist turns red if not) first kills any `nqbot run` still running, runs `archive_live` for any day not yet archived (allowed inside the job lock when the pre-open job calls it), starts the Windows Time service and forces a time resync, refreshes the news calendar (§9 News blackouts), pulls the overnight and any missing sessions' Databento trades, Tempest `/v1/vol/history` and the dark-pool prints, and rebuilds that state with the backtest code. The Tempest row is pulled here, at the pre-open, and only that copy is used; no evening job stores a Tempest row pulled after the close (a draft, §3.1 Tempest; owner 2026-10-09). In session, the engine writes a journal (`<paths.live_dir>/journal.jsonl`, fsynced on every write) **ahead** of every action: one grammar for every signal — `sending entry <account> <ticker> <order_id>`, `sending exit <account> <ticker> <event_id>`, `sending breakeven <account> <ticker> <event_id>`, each followed by `ack|error_sent|not_sent <same fields>` (`error_sent` = the request went out and failed, so an entry becomes "unknown"; `not_sent` = it never left) — plus every state change (pending, maybe filled, unknown, filled, paused, killed, flat) and every cap update. On restart, an unacknowledged `sending entry` marks that account "unknown" (as does any uncleared entry, below) (an unacknowledged exit is simply re-sent, since exits may retry; an unacknowledged breakeven is re-sent after the rebuild only if price is still at least 1 tick on its favourable side, else skipped and logged), and the set of sent order ids is rebuilt from the journal so no id is ever sent twice. On any restart it rebuilds in order: (1) the pre-open state, (2) today's data via Databento Live intraday replay from 18:00 ET, (3) today's Skylit boards: those logged live today, plus `/v1/historical/range` only for spans that weren't logged (§9 Supervision), with entries and `breakeven` disabled (protective exits due meanwhile — news flatten, flat-by, `!flat`, kill — are still sent); breakeven resumes when the rebuild ends (not gated by `!confirm`), and for any assumed-filled leg whose breakeven trigger fired meanwhile, `breakeven` is sent at once if price is still at least 1 tick on the favourable side of the breakeven stop, otherwise it is skipped and logged; the engine first sends `exit` on the ticker of every uncleared entry leg (as the guard does after a crash) and each such account becomes "flat-unconfirmed" once those exits are acknowledged, or "unknown" if they aren't (§10.3 Account states), and entries stay blocked until the rebuild finishes and the owner confirms the state. Step 22 verifies intraday replay and that `/v1/historical/range` serves the current day.
- Must run through the **same code path** as the backtest (replay historical data through the live engine to test it).
- Everything logged (features, prediction, decision, outcome) → becomes new training data. Each evening after 17:00 ET, `nqbot run archive_live` moves the day's live events and paths and the engine and guard journals into `paths.data_dir/live_archive/<date>/`, writes the day's `state_<date>.parquet` with the backtest code from Databento Historical data for the full 18:00–17:00 session and the day's Skylit RTH boards pulled from `/v1/historical/range` at archive time (cached, so the monthly top-up reuses them: no net extra credits) — never from what the engine received live; if either isn't complete yet, the checkpoint is marked provisional and the pre-open job rebuilds it, verifies the copy, and only then trims `paths.live_dir` to today's working files. If the engine was not running at flat-by and wrote no `carry_state` for that day, the next engine start first derives it from the engine and guard journals of the last session the engine ran (days with no engine journal are skipped) (in `live_archive/<date>/` once archived): every account with an entry not shown cleared, a position not shown closed, an unknown or maybe-filled state, or no acknowledged flat-by exit starts blocked until `!resolve`. After the last flat-by retry (`flat_by` + `flat_retry_min`) the engine writes `carry_state` into `account_state.json` (any account still unknown, maybe filled or holding a position, and any pause not yet followed by `!resume`), closes the journal and exits. The guard exits after it (unless an account is "unknown" after a failed flat-by exit: then the guard keeps retrying that account's `exit` and alerts every `unknown_alert_min` (start 5) minutes until it is acknowledged or the owner sends `!resolve`; an acknowledgement after `carry_state` was written is applied at the next start), or, if the engine isn't running, at `flat_by` + `flat_retry_min` + 1 min once its own flat-by backstop exits are acknowledged or the account is journaled unknown. The pre-open job also kills any engine or guard left from an earlier session before `archive_live`. `archive_live` runs from a Task Scheduler entry at `archive_time` (17:05 ET), never writes `account_state.json`, and waits (retrying) while any engine or guard process is alive. `account_state.json` is never trimmed, and the next session starts with any carried account blocked until `!resolve`; before anything else, the pre-open job (or engine start) sends `exit` to every carried account's tickers, including any carried in the previous contract (`carry_state` stores each leg's ticker, quantity and direction, and the direction lock uses it until `!resolve`). `!resolve <account> position` is refused for a carried account unless test 4 showed attached brackets survive past the session.
- **Databento Live gaps:** on any disconnect the engine resubscribes with intraday replay from the last processed `ts_event` inclusive (trades and `mbp-1`), drops records already processed (same instrument, `ts_event` and sequence number) and processes the gap in sequence — bars, profiles, VWAP, CVD, fill inference — before marking the feed healthy. If replay is unavailable, the session is flagged `live_gap`, entries stay blocked for the rest of the day and the owner is alerted. The step-23 replay test includes a simulated 30-second drop.
- **Clock:** feature, bar and event logic use Databento `ts_event`. Every wall-clock deadline — flat-by, `entry_end`, time-to-live expiry, blackout start, exit retries, the heartbeat — runs on the local clock, so a stalled feed can't freeze them. A wall-clock jump of more than `sleep_detect_sec` (start 30) between loop iterations, or the Windows resume event, triggers the reconnect gap fills (Databento intraday replay from the last `ts_event`, the Skylit gap pull, §3.1) and fires every deadline that passed meanwhile (time-to-live, blackout start, flat-by), with entries blocked until the fills finish and `feed_recovery_sec` has passed (the full restart rebuild runs only when the process itself restarted); shorter stalls count as processing lag. At startup and every `clock_check_min` (start 10) minutes the engine measures the local clock's offset against an NTP time server (an SNTP query; not against `ts_event`, which includes feed latency); if |offset| > `clock_max_offset_ms` (start 250), entries are blocked and the owner alerted; while blocked it re-checks every minute and entries resume after one passing check + `feed_recovery_sec`. An unreachable `ntp_server` counts as a failed check (entries blocked, checklist "clock unverified"); step 22 confirms it answers on each network the owner trades from.
- Notifications and remote control: Discord (§9c); at the desk, the Control Panel's KILL button. If the bot or computer itself is down, flatten in Tradovate web/mobile (§10.1).
- **News blackouts (owner, 2026-10, all accounts):** `config/calendar.yaml` holds **dated** events `{date, time_et, name, blackout: true|false}` for the whole backfill window and as far ahead as the official schedules are published (usually to the end of next year). **Only the owner's high-impact list has `blackout: true`** (his ForexFactory high-impact list, the same for backtest and live): Non-Farm Employment Change (with the Unemployment Rate and Average Hourly Earnings), the FOMC rate decision and statement, the FOMC press conference, FOMC minutes, scheduled Fed Chair testimony and speeches (plus, owner 2026-10-05, the Fed Chair nominee's confirmation hearing and the Senate confirmation vote, listed as `Fed Chair` events), CPI, Core PCE, PPI, Advance GDP, ISM Manufacturing PMI, ISM Services PMI and Retail Sales; other releases are listed with `blackout: false` (calendar features only; owner, 2026-10-04: only the extra lines of the listed releases — Unemployment Rate, Average Hourly Earnings, Core CPI, Core PPI, Core Retail Sales, PCE Price Index, Personal Income — no other releases). The list ignores ForexFactory's impact rating (e.g. the feed rated ISM Services PMI Medium for 2026-10-05) and includes every scheduled Fed Chair testimony and speech. **Live-only red-folder extra (owner, 2026-10-04):** any **other** US event that ForexFactory marks High (red folder) in the feed as known at the `preopen_start` (08:30) refresh also gets a blackout window, live only: it is written to `calendar.yaml → live_extra` (date, time, ForexFactory title, time seen), posted on Discord with its window, and logged so the step-24/25 reviews can count how often it happens. Past red ratings aren't available, so the backtest, labels and both simulators use the listed releases only: live is deliberately a little stricter than the backtest (it can only remove trades, never add them). Comparisons of live with the simulator (the step-23 replay, steps 24–25 and 27–28) run the simulator with the same `live_extra` windows the live run had, so those trades aren't counted as mismatches. Claude Code builds past dates from each agency's release archive (BLS, BEA, Census, ISM, and the Fed's calendar for FOMC and the Chair's testimony and speeches; the October–November 2025 federal shutdown postponed or cancelled several BLS and Census releases inside development months 1–2), each release at the time it was scheduled at 08:30 ET that day (a postponement or cancellation counts only if announced before then), so the backtest never knows a schedule change before live would. **Daily refresh:** the pre-open job (below) downloads ForexFactory's free weekly calendar feed (`calendar_feed_url`; it covers the current week), matches US events to the list **by title** through `news_title_map` (settings.yaml, approved by the owner at step 6, 2026-10-05; ForexFactory's impact rating is ignored, so a re-rated release can't drop out), compares them with `calendar.yaml` from today through the last day the feed covers, writes any added event and, for a moved one, adds the new window while keeping the old one until `!calendar remove` (logged with the time it was seen), and posts a Discord alert listing the change; a non-clock time ("Tentative", "All Day") never moves an event, and same-name events on one day are matched one-to-one by nearest time, keeping any unmatched one.
