A listed event missing from the feed is **never removed automatically**: the bot alerts and keeps its blackout until the owner confirms the cancellation (`!calendar remove <date> <name>`). A US event ForexFactory rates high-impact that isn't on the list becomes a live-only blackout as above (from the 08:30 refresh only; a rating seen later in the day only alerts). If the feed can't be reached or doesn't parse, the job keeps the file as it is and alerts (the official schedules are already in it months ahead). Feed (checked at step 4a, 2026-10-04): `https://nfs.faireconomy.media/ff_calendar_thisweek.json`, a JSON list of `{title, country, date, impact, forecast, previous}` with `date` in ET carrying its UTC offset (e.g. `2026-10-05T10:00:00-04:00`); it covers the current week only, Sunday to Saturday (there is no next-week feed); the XML twin gives GMT times and is not used. The server answers 403 to a client without a browser-like `User-Agent` (Python's default is refused; curl works), so the refresh sends one. Live and the backtest use the same cut-off: the calendar as known at `preopen_start` (08:30 ET); a change seen later in the day only alerts: the engine re-fetches the feed every `calendar_poll_min` (start 15) minutes during the session and posts any added or moved listed event, and any US event newly rated High, with its window, suggesting `!pause` / `!flat`; blackouts are never changed automatically mid-day. Unscheduled events, and testimony that runs long, are the owner's call: he watches the news and uses `!pause` / `!flat` (§9c). Gameplan blackouts are not used (owner, 2026-10: this rule replaces them). **News flatten (owner):** at release − `blackout_before_min` (30) min − `news_flatten_lead_sec` (start 30 s; must exceed `order_latency_sec` + `webhook_timeout_sec` + 1 s) new entries are blocked and the bot sends `exit` (`event_id` `news-<UTC ts>`) to every account and ticker, then once more `order_latency_sec` + 2 s later, so nothing is open and no order (entry, stop or target) rests from 30 minutes before the release; no new entry is sent until `blackout_after_min` (60) minutes after it (owner's rule, a hard filter, not tuned). Blackout window = [release − `blackout_before_min` − `news_flatten_lead_sec`, release + `blackout_after_min`), used identically by the tradeable filter (§1.2), labels, both simulators and live (live also applies the live-only red-folder windows above). Each listed event has its own window, and overlapping windows merge (an FOMC day: decision 14:00 and press conference 14:30 → no trades 13:30–15:30). This covers MFFU's Tier-1 rule (FOMC meetings, FOMC minutes, the Employment Report and CPI: no positions or orders from 2 minutes before to 2 minutes after). News-flatten exits retry and fail like flat-by exits (still unacknowledged when retries end → "unknown"). In session the rule matters for 10:00 releases (ISM: no trades 09:30–11:00) and 14:00 Fed events (08:30 releases end at 09:30, before the first entry, so they block nothing). Labels, the policy simulator and the prop simulator close any trade still open when that exit takes effect (flatten decision + `order_latency_sec`) at market (a timeout), so the backtest never counts a trade the owner wouldn't hold. The same file lists exchange holidays and early closes (§1.2).
- Runs on the owner's laptop during sessions (one machine, §11; the owner must be watching anyway — MyFundedFutures requires active oversight). On trading days from `preopen_start` (08:30) to 16:15 the laptop stays plugged in, with sleep and the lid-close action disabled, on one network, and the external SSD connected to a USB-C port (§11 Storage). Windows active hours are 08:00–17:00 and updates are installed on weekends (pause them for the maximum and resume on a weekend); the pre-open job warns on Discord if a restart is pending. Before moving it, send `!pause` (blocks new entries and removes pending unfilled ones; open brackets stay at the broker); a network change goes through the reconnect path, a sleep or restart through the journal rebuild. `nqbot live` runs the pre-open job (including `archive_live`) to completion first if it hasn't run, and only then starts the engine and guard; if started after 09:00, entries stay blocked until that job finishes. **External SSD during the session:** all live files are on the SSD (§11). The engine and the guard each hold an exclusive OS lock file in `paths.live_dir` for their lifetime and exit if it is taken (a failed port bind is fatal too), so two engines can never trade at once; `nqbot live` refuses while either is alive, and `nqbot live --restart` kills a hung engine (by its PID) and restarts it as a crash. `nqbot live` refuses to start unless the SSD is mounted (marker file `.nqbot_drive`) with ≥ `min_free_gb_live` (start 20) free. If the SSD disappears or a journal write fails mid-session, the engine at once blocks new entries, keeps its in-memory state, alerts on Discord, and keeps sending protective signals without journaling them (pending-entry removal, `breakeven`, the after-trade `exit`, the news flatten, flat-by, `!flat`, kill; sending an `exit` twice is harmless). Open brackets stay at the broker. The guard does not restart a crashed engine while the SSD is missing; it alerts and still serves KILL / `!flat`. When the SSD is back (marker file found on any drive), the engine, which kept its in-memory state, appends that state and every signal and acknowledgement sent meanwhile to the journal, backfills the board log and resumes entries (no rebuild; if the engine itself restarted meanwhile, the normal restart rebuild runs).
- **Supervision:** `nqbot live` starts two processes: the engine and a small guard (`nqbot guard`). The guard is the only Discord and KILL listener: it forwards every command to the engine over a localhost-only channel (127.0.0.1), checks every `guard_check_sec` that the engine process is alive (if the engine's heartbeat stops while the process is alive, the guard only alerts on Discord), restarts a crashed engine through the journal rebuild at most `max_engine_restarts` (start 3) times per session (after that it stays down, alerts, and still serves KILL / `!flat` and the flat-by backstop; a rebuild reuses the boards logged live today and pulls from `/v1/historical/range` only spans that weren't logged), immediately sends `exit` on the ticker of every **uncleared** entry leg (the journal shows `sending entry` followed by `ack`, `error_sent` or no reply, with no later removal, time-to-live or after-trade `exit` acknowledged and no assumed fill) (acknowledged → "flat-unconfirmed", otherwise "unknown", §10.3 Account states), posts that it did, and sends `exit` to every account and ticker itself on KILL / `!flat` / `!kill` (shared `clients/traderspost.py`), so a hung engine can't block a flatten. The engine owns the paused/killed state; the guard posts the heartbeat (marked "engine down") while the engine is not running. The engine checks the guard every `guard_check_sec` (start 10); if it is gone, the engine alerts through the Discord webhook and restarts it. On KILL / `!flat` the guard first journals the block, forwards it to the engine (which re-checks the guard's block state right before every **entry** POST and sends no entry if it can't confirm; `exit` and `breakeven` are never held back by this check), sends `exit` itself, and sends it once more `order_latency_sec` + 2 s later. If the UI can't reach the guard within 2 s, it sends `exit` to every account and ticker itself through `clients/traderspost.py`, shows that it did, and sends the KILL / Flatten block to the engine on `engine_port`; if the engine doesn't answer either, it shows that the block must be repeated (`!kill` / `!flat`) once the engine or guard is back (the UI writes no journal). The guard also sends `exit` to every account and ticker at each account's `flat_by` + `guard_flat_grace_sec` (start 30) always (sending an `exit` twice is harmless, so it never skips an account because the journal shows another account's exits acknowledged), and likewise at every news-flatten moment + `guard_flat_grace_sec`, whatever the engine's state (from `calendar.yaml` as of the pre-open job), so a hung engine can't skip a news flatten. If a guard journal write fails or the journal can't be read (SSD missing), it sends its exits anyway, unjournaled, treats every journal check as "not acknowledged" and alerts. While it runs, the engine is the only writer of `account_state.json`, Discord-made `accounts.yaml` changes and the journal; the guard listens on `guard_port` and the engine on `engine_port` (both 127.0.0.1); the UI sends KILL and Flatten to the guard (with the same 2-s self-send fallback) and other edits and commands to the engine; settings and account edits are made only while the engine is running or before it starts, and the guard journals its own exits and every KILL / `!flat` block (fsynced, before sending) in `<paths.live_dir>/guard_journal.jsonl`. Every engine start and every rebuild restores the paused/killed state from the engine and guard journals. Optionally the guard pings a dead-man URL (`deadman_url`, empty by default; the ping carries no data) every minute, so the owner's phone is alerted if the laptop dies.
- **Daily checklist (Live Status shows it, green/red):** charger in, SSD mounted, Wi-Fi up, clock offset OK, pre-open job done, news calendar refreshed (today's blackout windows listed), Skylit credits OK, balance and floor typed, gameplan confirmed or "none today", heartbeat seen, TradersPost subscription settings unchanged (§3.5), and after flat-by: every account flat with no working order in Tradovate, followers in TradeSyncer included (§10.1). Backstop if the laptop dies mid-trade: the brackets at the broker protect the position, the heartbeat stops (§9c), and the owner flattens in Tradovate web/mobile; step 27 records, on the first prop account, whether the firm auto-flattens at its session close.

### 9b. Control Panel UI (owner-facing)
One local **Streamlit** app (`nqbot ui`, opens in the browser at `localhost`; nothing exposed to the internet) with five pages. It binds only to 127.0.0.1: `nqbot ui` launches Streamlit with `--server.address 127.0.0.1 --server.headless true --server.port <ui_port>` on the command line (whatever folder it is started from) and checks `server.address` at startup, refusing to start otherwise, so nobody else on a campus network can open it.

**Look and feel (owner's references: `docs/design/ui_reference.png` and `docs/design/ui_reference_2.png`; Claude Code opens both at step 14 and matches their style, not their content, name or logo):** a dark "trading desk" theme.
- **Colours:** near-black navy background, panels one shade lighter with thin 1-px borders and small rounded corners; text off-white, secondary text muted grey-blue; one muted steel-blue accent for the active tab, primary button and slider; green only for healthy status ("LIVE", "feed OK"), amber for warnings, red only for KILL and breaches.
- **Type:** a clean sans-serif for text; a monospace font for every clock, price, timestamp and small uppercase label (letter-spaced eyebrow labels such as "LIVE STATUS · MFFUR1").
- **Layout:** a left sidebar with the page list (thin line icons, the current page highlighted by a lighter panel), today's date and the engine state at its foot; a slim top bar with the page name, a status dot ("ENGINE LIVE" / "PAUSED" / "KILLED") and the ET clock; each page opens with a small eyebrow label, a large title and a one-line subtitle, then a row of KPI cards (big number, small caption, small icon), then an underlined tab strip and the page body. From the second reference: the sidebar groups pages under small collapsible uppercase headings (e.g. "TRADING" for Live Status and Gameplan, "SETUP" for Accounts and Settings, "RESEARCH" for Review and reports), the breadcrumb eyebrow ("NQBOT / LIVE STATUS"), a two-line page title whose second line is in the light accent blue, and chart legends as small coloured dots with labels. Its network graph is a style cue only, not a required chart. Small outlined tags at the right of the tab strip show context (e.g. "NQZ6 · 1m | Eastern time | read only"); pill badges show state ("SAVED", "DEMO · SIM FILLS").
- **Where it applies:** Live Status = one KPI-card row per account (est. P&L vs daily cap, drawdown left, lives, distance to payout) with tabs per account; Review = the reference's replay pattern (Restart / Play / Minute → / Next card, a replay clock and slider that reveal the chart minute by minute up to the decision, and an "at this point" side panel with the card's facts); Accounts and Settings = forms in bordered panels; Gameplan = the parsed table in a panel next to its chart.
- **Streamlit:** a dark theme in `.streamlit/config.toml` plus a small CSS file injected once (fonts, borders, card and badge styles); built-in widgets (tabs, metrics, buttons, select boxes, slider) are styled, not replaced. Step 14 shows the owner a screenshot of each page next to the reference before the review loop starts.
1. **Accounts** — add/edit/delete accounts with plain form fields, grouped:
   - **Account:** name (lowercase letters and digits), firm, phase (evaluation / funded), active yes/no, demo yes/no, TradersPost webhook variable name, sim fills (Tradovate sim).
   - **Targets & caps:** a **preset** picker (§6.7: MFFU Rapid EOD 25k / Builder 25k × evaluation / funded / after first payout; Pro 50k × evaluation / funded) that fills every field; profit target; stop at target (evaluations); funded buffer, payout min/max, profit needed since the last payout, payout wait (calendar days before each payout, counted from the first funded trade, then from the previous payout; both dates editable), profit split, payout cap group and its cumulative cap (shows the group's payouts so far and what is left), copied accounts (how many accounts copy this one through TradeSyncer, 0 = none, changeable any time), hold while the payout clock runs (off / pause / protect, default off) and the after-first-payout cap; **daily profit limit**: *Consistency rule* (enter consistency %, the bot computes target × % — e.g. MFFU Rapid EOD eval, Builder funded) or *None*; payout target and auto-protect distance (funded).
   - **Drawdown & losses:** start balance, max drawdown ($) and type (static / EOD trailing), drawdown lock level and when it locks (EOD trailing: when the floor reaches it / at the first payout); **daily loss limit on/off** — when on: $ (a breach ends the day); bot daily loss cap (optional; blank = the firm's DLL, or none when the DLL is off); max losses per day (optional).
   - **Firm rules:** minimum trading days, max inactive days, payout minimum days, commissions per NQ and MNQ round trip.
   - **Position limits:** max trades per day, max minis, max micros, how the firm counts contracts (*mini-equivalent*: micro-to-mini ratio, or *total contracts*: one limit for minis + micros counted alike, `max_contracts_total`), micros allowed, mixing allowed, and an optional **scaling plan** table (profit level → max size) for firms whose limit grows with the account.
   - **Sizing & trade rules:** risk per trade (pre-filled with drawdown ÷ 3), flat-by time, breakeven on/off, lives mode, last-life cutoff (auto or %), max three-loss rate.
   - Shows live as you type: daily profit limit, risk per trade, lives (from drawdown), the **smallest stop that still gets full risk** before the size cap binds, and the **largest stop that still fits 1 micro**. Validates (risk > drawdown ÷ 3, missing fields, two active accounts whose `traderspost_webhook_env` resolves to the same URL) and blocks saving until fixed; a bot daily loss cap above the firm's DLL only shows a warning. Saves to `config/accounts.yaml`.
2. **Strategy settings** — per account: **risk profile selector** (Aggressive / Normal / Conservative / Protect / Custom, §6.8) showing each profile's backtested trades per week, win rate, average R and losing-day odds; a **risk $ slider** (capped at drawdown ÷ 3) and threshold sliders when Custom is selected; auto-protect distance for funded accounts; lives mode on/off. Global: `gates.gate_pass_rate_min`; session and setup windows and the news blackout (`blackout_before_min` / `blackout_after_min`, owner's 30 / 60) shown read-only (owner's fixed rules: they set the labels, the tradeable filter and `minutes_to_horizon_end`, so a change goes through Claude Code — rebuild events and labels, re-run the walk-forward and the production fit, mark every account's sweep stale; neither value may go below 2, MFFU's Tier-1 rule). Per account: λ (`lambda_risk_penalty`, default 0) and a **Re-run sweep** button (§6.8).
3. **Live status** — one panel per active account (each TradersPost connection) (inferred values are labelled: "assumed filled" / "maybe filled", "est. P&L since the morning balance", "exit sent" until the next import confirms them): today's est. P&L vs daily cap, drawdown remaining, lives left, distance to payout, **current risk profile with a one-click switcher**, trades taken, open position with stop/target, last alerts; plus a big **KILL** button (same action as the Discord `!kill`), **Flatten** (same as `!flat`), **Pause / Resume**, **Resolve** (account + flat, or position with its legs; same as `!resolve`), **Confirm state** (same as `!confirm`) , **Payout taken** and **New evaluation** (demo) buttons, the balance and floor entry (each morning, and any time the account is flat), a P&L adjustment field, **Followers flat** (§10.1) and the Orders/Fills report upload (steps 24 and 27). If the balance and floor aren't entered by 09:40, the bot posts a Discord reminder. Discord commands `!profile <account> protect` etc. do the same from the phone.
4. **Gameplan** — drag-and-drop today's gameplan file (.md, .pdf or .docx; a .docx is converted to Markdown first, §3.4). The parser (§3.4) extracts it in a few seconds and shows the result as an editable table (zones, side, stops, targets, ES gates, HVL, scenarios) next to a small NQ chart of today's pre-open with the zones drawn on it (the chart appears from step 22, when the pre-open data path exists). Flagged fields are highlighted; the owner fixes anything wrong and clicks **Confirm**, and only confirmed gameplans reach the live engine. Only a gameplan confirmed before 09:30 is used for that day's live trading; a later upload or parse correction is saved for training but doesn't change today's trading. Status line: "Today: confirmed 09:12" or "No gameplan today — bot runs without it."
5. **Review** — the setup-card review loop (§8).
- Every `accounts.yaml` write goes through the engine while it runs (the UI sends edits over 127.0.0.1); when no engine is running for the session (before `nqbot live`, or after the engine's end-of-day exit), the UI writes only `accounts.yaml` / `settings.yaml`, under a lock file; while a session's engine is down mid-session it refuses edits (KILL and Flatten still work); every writer re-checks the file hash before saving and reloads on a mismatch.
- Settings changes apply **between trades only** (the engine reloads config when flat), never mid-trade; an open trade keeps the size and exits it was entered with. Config files are written atomically (temp file in the same folder, then rename) and validated with pydantic on reload; an invalid file keeps the last good config and alerts.
- Every change is logged with a timestamp so backtest-vs-live comparisons know which settings were active.

### 9c. Discord (phone remote, optional but recommended)
The Control Panel is the main interface at the desk. Discord is the **phone remote** for a bot that executes on its own: notifications of what it did, P&L checks, and commands to pause, flatten or kill. Free, private server, one channel.

**What it sends (via the webhook):**
- **Entry notice** (sent as the order goes out) — zone (what's stacked there; golden-zone flag), entry style, gamma regime, trigger, entry / stop / target with order type, size per account, P, EV in R, and the account's lives left.
- **Trade events** — assumed filled / target hit / stopped / breakeven sent / exit sent, with estimated P&L (confirmed by the daily import).
- **Calendar** — the pre-open job's news-calendar changes and today's blackout windows (§9).
- **Account events** — daily cap reached, daily loss stop hit, last life reached, auto-protect switched on, flat-by exits sent.
- **Health problems** — data feed dropped or lagging, Skylit stream stale or credits low, SSD missing, clock offset, engine restarted, webhook rejected or timed out (broker rejections are not visible), bot paused; plus a heartbeat every `heartbeat_min` (start 15) so silence means trouble. These matter most: they are how the owner learns something broke while away.
- **End-of-day summary** — trades, R, P&L per account.

**What it listens for (via a small Discord bot, `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN`):**
| Command | Action |
|---|---|
| `!status` | Reply with each active account's est. P&L, lives left, profile, and open position as the bot believes it |
| `!pause` / `!resume` | Stop / restart new entries; `!pause` also removes pending unfilled entries (`exit` on tickers with no filled leg); open trades untouched |
| `!flat` | Send `exit` to every account and ticker (this also removes pending entries), and pause new entries until `!resume` |
| `!kill` | Send `exit` to every account and ticker, then block entries until the next session. The process keeps running (exit retries, heartbeat reads "KILLED", commands still work) |
| `!balance <account> <balance> <floor>` | Enter the balance and drawdown floor from the phone (same as Live Status): each morning, and any time the account is flat |
| `!profile <account> <profile>` | Switch risk profile (§6.8) |
| `!calendar remove <date> <name>` | Confirm that a listed news event was cancelled, so its blackout is dropped (§9 News blackouts); the bot alerts first and never drops one by itself |
| `!resolve <account> flat` or `!resolve <account> position <ticker> <qty> [<ticker> <qty>]` | Clear an "unknown" or "maybe filled" state after checking Tradovate (§10.3); `flat` first sends `exit` to both tickers of that account (so no resting entry survives) and clears only when both are acknowledged; with `position`, the bot tracks only the stated legs and quantities, with their sent brackets, and first sends `exit` to every ticker of that account not listed (clearing once those are acknowledged) |
| `!confirm [<account>]` | Show the account's state (all accounts if none named), then confirm it after a restart rebuild or a webhook error; clears a "flat-unconfirmed" account; refused for an account that is unknown or maybe filled (use `!resolve`) |
| `!payout <account> <amount>` | Same as the "Payout taken" button: records the payout (`payouts_taken`, date, amount) and subtracts the amount from the confirmed balance (the payout that reaches a payout cap group's cap sets it to `payout_baseline_usd` instead, §6.7), leaving today's P&L, DLL and cap tallies untouched; on an account with a payout cap group it also asks how many accounts the payout was taken on (default 1 + `copied_accounts`); refused while the account has an open, pending or maybe-filled leg |
| `!followers <leader> flat` | Confirm that a TradeSyncer leader's followers are flat (§10.1), releasing the leader's direction from the lock |

- **Security:** the bot obeys only the owner's Discord user ID (`DISCORD_OWNER_ID`) in that one private channel; everything else is ignored.
- **Always auto-execution** (owner): the bot executes; Discord reports and is the remote control. There is no alert-only or confirm mode; validation runs as auto-execution on a Tradovate demo account (step 24).
- Without Discord the bot still works fully from the Control Panel; you just lose phone notifications and remote control.

---

## 10. Execution and risk (Phases F–G)

### 10.1 Chosen path: Python bot → TradersPost → Tradovate (MyFundedFutures)
Why: works with the owner's firm (MyFundedFutures); TradersPost officially supports webhooks from custom code; supports brackets (stop + target on entry), a built-in **breakeven** action, cancel and exit; no TradingView needed.

Firm rules the code must respect. MyFundedFutures confirmed (2026-10) that a TradersPost bot is allowed if it is supervised; that check covered MFFU firm-wide, not each account type, so re-read the Pro terms on automation before going live on a Pro account (step 26). Re-check the terms before going live, since they change:
- **MyFundedFutures:** semi-automated trading with tools like TradersPost and custom scripts is allowed on evaluation, sim-funded and live, **but only with active trader oversight** — fully autonomous set-and-forget bots are prohibited; no HFT (200+ trades/day); **no hedging across accounts** (§10.3 direction lock); sim-funded accounts follow the Tier-1 news rule (§6.7, §9).
- Therefore the bot runs **supervised**: the owner is at the screen during trading hours, and the kill switch (Control Panel KILL button or Discord `!kill`) is one tap away.

Order flow per trade:
1. Entry signal with `stopLoss` and `takeProfit` attached (bracket). Never a naked entry.
2. If the breakeven rule fires (§6.4): one `breakeven` signal.
3. v1 has **no model-driven early exits**: a trade ends at its stop, its target, flat-by, the news flatten (§9 News blackouts) or an owner `!flat` / kill (one `exit` signal). Labels and the simulator assume exactly this.
That is 1–3 webhook calls per leg — far below the 60/min limit.

Several accounts (owner's setup, 2026-10): each account with its own rules or stage (a Rapid EOD eval, a Builder eval, a funded account) gets its own TradersPost connected account (~$10/month each beyond the plan's first; TradersPost counts every Tradovate account separately even under one login, and a connection can be re-pointed to the next account when one passes or fails). The bot computes each decision once and then applies **each account's own** thresholds, profile, lives and caps, so the same setup can trade on one account and be skipped on another (e.g. an account on its last life with a stricter cutoff). Accounts with the same preset **and** stage may instead share one connection through TradeSyncer: TradeSyncer copies the leader's trades **without** the bot's per-account checks, so only identical accounts may follow (the owner sets TradeSyncer's size multipliers, daily loss limits and max position caps there). `!flat` / `!kill` exit every connected account and TradeSyncer copies the exits. An `exit` sent while a leader is already flat copies nothing, so a follower that drifted out of sync (failed copy, rejection) is not flattened by the bot: at flat-by the bot posts "check followers flat in TradeSyncer", the owner confirms it on Live Status (checklist), and flattens any out-of-sync follower in TradeSyncer or Tradovate. After every leader trade end or removal the bot posts "check followers flat", and the leader's last direction stays in the direction lock until the owner taps **Followers flat** on Live Status (or `!followers <leader> flat`). Live Status and `!status` show every connected account. When an account passes, fails or is replaced, the owner marks it inactive (`active: false`), re-points that TradersPost connection to the new account and enters it on the Accounts page; the bot blocks entries on it until its state is confirmed. The UI refuses to save two active accounts whose `traderspost_webhook_env` resolves to the same URL, and flat-by, `!flat` and kill exits go once per distinct webhook URL. If the bot or computer is down, flatten the leader in Tradovate web/mobile (TradeSyncer copies the exit), then `!resolve <account> flat`.

Bridge risks and mitigations:
- Extra hop (TradersPost cloud): sub-second, but it matters for seconds-scale order-flow entries, so labels and simulators delay every order, `exit` and `breakeven` by `order_latency_sec` (§5.5b).
- **Broker rejections:** a webhook reply only shows TradersPost received the signal, not that Tradovate accepted the order (max position, account locked, failed account). Unless step 21 shows rejections are visible, Live Status and Discord show entries as "sent – broker acceptance unconfirmed"; the owner checks TradersPost's signal log for the first 10 demo trades; every assumed fill is flagged for reconciliation and overridden by the next orders/fills import (step 24).
- Bridge or Tradovate outage: bracket orders already sit at the broker, so open trades stay protected; the bot stops sending new entries if a webhook returns an error or times out, and alerts the owner on Discord.
- No fill feedback: the bot logs what it sent; the owner reconciles with Tradovate; if TradersPost offers a trade log/API, poll it.

### 10.2 Not chosen (don't propose these)
PickMyTrade (TradersPost covers Tradovate), Topstep/ProjectX (owner declined), Rithmic direct API (needs firm permission and conformance testing), Quantower/NinjaTrader strategies (C#; would duplicate the Python feature code).

### 10.3 Hard limits in code
- Per-trade dollar risk from `config/accounts.yaml`; skip if the stop doesn't fit (§6.1). Size never exceeds the account's max minis/micros or current scaling tier.
- Daily loss cap where the account has one (§6.7; tracked by the bot from its own fills estimate, plus the firm's own enforcement); max trades/day; max position; flat-by time; news/data blackouts; kill switch (Discord `!kill` + Control Panel KILL button) that sends `exit` and stops new entries. At flat-by, on `!flat` and on kill, `exit` is sent to **every** connected account and ticker, whatever the bot believes its state is (a harmless no-op when flat).
- If the data feed or the bridge fails: no new entries; existing brackets stay at the broker; Discord alert.
- **Direction lock across accounts (MFFU bans hedging across accounts; owner):** at any moment every account's open positions and pending entries (NQ and MNQ together) point the same way. An entry opposite to any position, pending entry, or "maybe filled" / "flat-unconfirmed" / "unknown" state (which counts as the direction of that account's last sent entry, kept in `carry_state` for a carried account) on any account is **skipped for that account, never queued** (a removed pending entry keeps its direction until its removal `exit`, sent after the entry's own reply, is acknowledged + `order_latency_sec`, plus `tradesyncer_copy_lag_sec` (measured by test 9 (h)) for a TradeSyncer leader); the account can take a later decision. Decisions in the same second are processed in one fixed order (highest calibrated EV first, then account name), and the first direction sent locks the rest. `demo: true` accounts are outside the lock. Threshold sweeps, preset-stage sweeps, per-fold choices and gates 16/20 run per single account without the lock; the policy and prop simulators then replay the active account set together with the lock for the per-account reports (§6.7 Backtest, §6.8), showing the trades the lock removed per account.
- **One trade per account at a time.** A trade is one NQ leg and/or one MNQ leg, sent together with identical entry, stop and target prices; breakeven and exit go to every filled leg; a leg whose entry is still pending is removed with `exit` on its own ticker. After the trade's **last** open leg has ended (stop or target inferred on each filled leg's own instrument) and after every time-to-live expiry with no leg filled, the bot also sends `exit` to both tickers of that account (a no-op when flat, test 3), so no stale resting entry or leg can fill into the next trade. Live fill inference reads each leg's own instrument (Databento Live subscribes to MNQ trades for the contract month resolved from `NQ.v.0`, same dataset). If only one leg fills before the time-to-live, the other is removed with `exit` on its own ticker and the trade runs smaller (less risk, never more). No new entry is sent while a position is open, an entry order is pending, or any `exit` / `breakeven` for that account is still unacknowledged (being retried); an acknowledged one never blocks; `!resume` after `!flat` waits only for `!flat` exits still being retried (one whose retries ended has already made its account "unknown", which `!resolve` handles). Exits never flip into a reverse position (`exit`, never `reverse`).
- **Retries and data health** (keys in settings.yaml): `exit` / `breakeven` retry with backoff (1, 2, 4… s) until acknowledged or `exit_retry_window_sec` (start 120) has passed (before every `breakeven` send or retry, price must still be at least 1 tick + `exit_slippage_ticks` on its favourable side; otherwise retries stop, it is logged and the original bracket stays); flat-by exits keep retrying for `flat_retry_min` (start 11) minutes after that day's flat-by, kill and `!flat` exits for `flat_retry_min` after the command, and news-flatten exits for `flat_retry_min` after the flatten moment, and alert every `flat_alert_sec` (start 30). `breakeven` goes only to legs assumed filled (never "maybe filled"); a "no open position" reply ends its retries, marks the leg for fill re-inference and is not a webhook error; `breakevenOffset` is omitted when the converted offset is 0. Webhook calls time out after `webhook_timeout_sec` (start 5). Any `exit` for an account (either ticker) still unacknowledged when its retries end puts the account in "unknown" until `!resolve`. Each live schema (NQ/ES trades, NQ `mbp-1`, MNQ trades) has its own staleness check and a processing-lag check (local receive time − `ts_event` > `feed_lag_max_ms`, start 500); if any schema an enabled model or fill inference uses (MNQ trades whenever micros are allowed) is stale or lagging, new entries are blocked (inputs are never passed to a model as missing). New entries are also blocked while an NQ/ES connection is stale (no record of any kind, data or Databento heartbeat (heartbeat interval as low as Databento allows), for > `feed_stale_sec` (start 5); a quiet market is not stale), or while the Skylit connection has been silent (no event or ping) for > `skylit_stale_sec` (start 45 s, i.e. three missed pings), and on any webhook error; they resume after `feed_recovery_sec` (start 60) of healthy data plus a fresh Skylit board (after a Skylit gap, also the gap filled from history, §3.1), or (webhook errors) when the owner confirms. A data outage, the start of a news blackout or `!pause` removes pending entries, and the news flatten (§9) also exits open positions, (`exit`, §3.5). The simulator does the same for outages and blackouts.
- **Account states (one table; §3.5, §9, §9c and CLAUDE.md rule 8 follow it):** *flat*; *pending* (entry sent, unfilled); *filled* (assumed, §3.5); *maybe filled* (§3.5); *flat-unconfirmed* — an `exit` sent after the entry's own reply was acknowledged on every uncertain leg's ticker: the worse case is booked, the account takes no new entry and its last direction stays in the lock until the owner checks Tradovate and sends `!confirm <account>` (or Confirm on Live Status); *unknown* — an `exit` still unacknowledged when its retries end, or an entry whose fate can't be told: cleared only by `!resolve`. An `exit` sent while that leg's entry POST is still unanswered doesn't count for any of this: it is re-sent as soon as the entry's reply arrives. Step 21 records what a webhook reply shows when the broker rejects an `exit`.
- **Never auto-retry an entry.** If an entry webhook times out or errors after the request was sent, the bot does not resend it (a resend could double the position if the first one actually went through); it marks the account "unknown state", pauses new entries, alerts the owner to check Tradovate, and at once sends `exit` (`cancel: true`) on that leg's ticker, again after `webhook_timeout_sec` + `order_latency_sec` (in case the entry was still in flight); once acknowledged, the leg is "flat-unconfirmed" (worse case booked; `!confirm <account>` clears it after the owner checks Tradovate). An account still "unknown" (an exit unacknowledged after its retries) leaves that state only when the owner resolves it (Live Status **Resolve** or `!resolve <account> flat|position`); "maybe filled" also clears by itself per §3.5. An entry that failed before anything was sent (DNS or connection error) is logged as "not sent" and is not retried either, but does not set "unknown". Exits may retry, since sending `exit` twice is harmless.
- Every entry leg carries a unique `extras.order_id` (`<event_id>-<entry style><n>-<account>-<leg>`, §5.4) plus its `extras.event_id`; the bot refuses to send the same `order_id` twice. `exit` and `breakeven` signals carry the `event_id` and are exempt; blanket exits carry `event_id` = `flatby-<UTC ts>` (flat-by), `flat-<UTC ts>` (`!flat`), `news-<UTC ts>` (news flatten) or `kill-<UTC ts>` (kill). The journal logs every exit and breakeven too (`sending exit <account> <ticker> <event_id>`, `sending breakeven <account> <ticker> <event_id>`, §9).
- Start: one account, normal sizing (§6.1), owner watching.

---

## 11. Repo layout and coding rules

```
nq-signal-bot/
  CLAUDE.md             # working rules, read every session
  .env                  # SKYLIT_API_KEY, DATABENTO_API_KEY, ANTHROPIC_API_KEY, DISCORD_WEBHOOK_URL, DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN, DISCORD_OWNER_ID, DISCORD_CHANNEL_ID, one TP_WEBHOOK_<ACCOUNT> per TradersPost connection (never commit)
  .gitignore            # .env, config/local.yaml, data/, models/, reports/ (a safety net only: these folders never exist inside the repo)
  pyproject.toml
  config/
    settings.yaml       # sessions, anchors, symbols, thresholds
    features.yaml       # every tunable detector parameter, zone widths
    accounts.yaml       # per-account risk, max minis/micros, scaling tiers, caps, thresholds
    calendar.yaml       # release times (past dates: as scheduled that morning; refreshed by the pre-open job), holidays and early closes, OPEX, quarter-end, owner-entered event dates
  docs/
    SPEC.md             # this spec
    skylit/             # academy docs (owner provides)
    feature_spec.md     # approved definitions
    owner_notes.md
  data/                 # under paths.root on the external SSD, not in the repo
    raw/{skylit,databento,gameplans}/
    processed/          # parquet: bars, profiles, mapped nodes, events
    reviews/
    live/               # paths.live_dir: journal, account_state.json, today's state
    live_archive/
  src/nqbot/
    clients/  skylit.py  databento_client.py  discord.py  traderspost.py
    gameplan/ parser.py
    features/ vwap.py  profile.py  tpo.py  volctx.py  opening_range.py  session_levels.py  nodes.py  mapping.py  confluence.py  zones.py  darkpool.py  regime.py  orderflow.py  daytype.py
    detectors/ rug.py  pika.py  trinity.py  audit.py  absorption.py  exhaustion.py
    events/   builder.py  labeler.py  paths.py
    models/   structure.py  trigger.py  daytype.py  pair_scorer.py  calibrate.py  compare.py
    backtest/ walkforward.py  report.py  exit_sim.py  prop_sim.py
    live/     engine.py  guard.py  risk.py  sizing.py  execution.py  archive.py
    ui/       app.py  pages/{accounts,settings,live_status,gameplan,review}.py
  tests/
  reports/              # paths.reports_dir, on the SSD
  models/               # paths.models_dir, on the SSD
```
- **Path convention:** every `data/…`, `models/…`, `reports/…` path in this spec means a folder under `paths.root` on the SSD: `paths.data_dir` = `<root>/data`, `paths.raw_dir` = `<root>/data/raw`, `paths.live_dir` = `<root>/data/live`, `paths.models_dir` = `<root>/models`, `paths.reports_dir` = `<root>/reports`, `paths.log_dir` = `<root>/logs`, `paths.tmp_dir` = `<root>/tmp`. Code always builds paths from `paths.*`, never repo-relative.
- Python 3.12 (pinned in `pyproject.toml` and `.python-version`; `uv` manages it, `uv.lock` is committed, every command runs as `uv run nqbot …`, step 4a), pandas/polars, pyarrow, lightgbm, scikit-learn, shap, streamlit + plotly, httpx + httpx-sse, databento, anthropic, python-docx (gameplan .docx → Markdown), discord.py, pydantic, pytest, tzdata (Windows has no time-zone database of its own).
- **Where settings live:**
  - **Per account → `accounts.yaml`** (§6.7 fields, plus `lambda_risk_penalty` §6.3, `max_three_loss_rate`, `lives_mode`, `last_life_top_pct` (`auto` or a number), `sim_fills`, `custom_risk_usd`, `stop_at_target`, `funded_buffer_usd`, `payout_min_usd`, `payout_max_usd`, `payout_profit_since_last_usd`, `payouts_taken`, `after_first_payout`, `active` and the Custom profile's `min_ev_r` / `min_target_prob` §6.5b). Daily account state lives in `<paths.live_dir>/account_state.json`, not here (§6.7).
  - **Global trading/execution → `settings.yaml`:** `session.flat_by`, `min_minutes_before_flat`, the month ledger `months` (§7 rule 2b), `paths.root` (settings.yaml stores the folder relative to the drive, e.g. `nqbot/`; at startup the drive is found by scanning mounted drives for the marker file `.nqbot_drive`, never by drive letter) with `paths.data_dir` = `<paths.root>/data`, `paths.raw_dir`, `paths.models_dir`, `paths.reports_dir`, `paths.log_dir`, `paths.tmp_dir`, `paths.live_dir` under it, `min_free_gb` (50), `min_free_gb_live` (20), `max_fold_mem_gb` (10), `ops.job_lock_window` (08:30–16:15) with `ops.job_lock_window_enforced` (false until step 22 sets it true), `preopen_start` (08:30), `max_workers`, `order_latency_sec`, `default_risk_usd`, `exit_retry_window_sec`, `flat_retry_min`, `flat_alert_sec`, `unknown_alert_min`, `webhook_timeout_sec`, `guard_check_sec`, `guard_flat_grace_sec`, `guard_port`, `engine_port`, `ui_port`, `ntp_server`, `archive_time` (17:05), `threshold_grid`, `retrain_every_months`, `inactivity_alert_days`, `news_flatten_lead_sec`, `last_life_grid`, `profile_ev_quantiles`, `min_month1_sessions`, `feed_lag_max_ms`, `max_engine_restarts`, `sleep_detect_sec`, `swing_lookback_sec`, `style_set_grid`, `lgbm_grid`, `ablation_groups`, `default_max_three_loss_rate`, `gate_account`, `bootstrap_paths`, `clock_max_offset_ms`, `clock_check_min`, `skylit_credit_warn`, `skylit_reach_margin_min`, `skylit_pack_margin`, `skylit_credit_packs` (§3.1 Backfill), `deadman_url`, `tuning_start`, `max_entry_slip_ticks`, `exit_slippage_ticks`, `default_commission_per_mini_rt` / `default_commission_per_micro_rt` (labeling defaults), `profile_risk_multiplier` (§6.8), `recency_halflife_months`, `lives_dust_usd`, `pair_build_min_rr`, `heartbeat_min`, `map_latency_sec`, `skylit_symbols` (map name → API root, SPX → SPXW, §3.1), `skylit_metric`, `skylit_max_expirations`, `skylit_max_strikes`, `strike_edge_margin`, `gates` (`gates.gate_calib_max`, `gates.gate_pass_rate_min`), `min_new_sealed_months`, `enabled_entry_styles`, `style_tie_ev_r`, `max_target_candidates`, `feed_stale_sec`, `skylit_stale_sec`, `feed_recovery_sec`, breakeven rules `be_trigger` / `be_offset_ticks`, `blackout_before_min` / `blackout_after_min` (30 / 60, owner), `calendar_feed_url` (ForexFactory's weekly feed), `calendar_poll_min`, `news_title_map`, `gameplan_min_days`, `gameplan_model`, `gameplan_effort`, `gameplan_max_tokens`, `gameplan_usd_per_mtok`, `gameplan_max_usd_per_file` (§3.4), `grid_sample_frac`, and the start values of `entry_ttl_sec`, `stop_buffer` (with `stop_buffer_floor`, §5.1) and `entry_order_type` (per entry style).
  - **Detector, mapping and structure parameters → `features.yaml`:** structure: `zone_proximity`, `es_zone_proximity`, `confluence_tol`, `max_zone_width`, `rearm_dist`, `entry_reprice_tol`, `chart_level_half_width` / `chart_level_half_width_cap` (per level type), `node_band`, `min_node_pct_of_king`, `node_drop_pct`, `lean_window`, `velocity_lookback_sec`, `regime_band`, `flip_margin`, `flip_bar_minutes`, `ib_fib_start_minute`, `ib_lock_minute`, sweeps (`sweep_max_depth`, `sweep_reclaim_bars`), `retest_tol_ticks` / `retest_lookback_sessions`, `atr_period`, `rotation_min_pts`, `rv_lookback_sessions`;
