entries and order flow: `abs_volume`, `abs_ticks`, `abs_seconds`, `abs_refills`, `abs_push_ticks`, `abs_stop_buffer`, `big_trade_agg_ms`, `big_trade_min_contracts`, `big_trade_lookback_sessions`, `imb_ratio`, `imb_window_sec`, `imb_min_volume`, `imb_stack_min`, `exh_lookback_sec`, `exh_decay_ratio`, `div_lookback_sec`, `div_min_pts`, `cvd_slope_sec`, `tob_window_sec`, `trig_min_gap_sec`, `area_rearm_sec`, `abs_stop_window_sec`; stops: `min_stop_pts` / `max_stop_pts` (null, owner), `stop_merge_ticks`; labels: `hold_reaction_pts`, `react_pts` / `react_minutes`, `react_width_q`, `hold_window_min`, `trig_fav_ticks` / `trig_adv_ticks` / `trig_window_sec`; day type: `daytype_snapshot_min`, `daytype_max_features`, `daytype_inputs`, `trend_*`; pre-declared grids: `tune_grids_13`, `tune_grids_19`. **Frozen execution parameters** (`entry_ttl_sec`, `stop_buffer`, `abs_stop_buffer`, `abs_stop_window_sec`, order type per entry style) are written here too, marked `frozen_from: months 1-4` (§7.1); `settings.yaml` holds only their start values.
- All times stored in UTC; session logic in America/New_York.
- **One machine (owner):** everything — build, backtests, training and live trading — runs on the owner's laptop (Dell 16 Plus, 16 GB RAM). Its internal drive holds only the OS, the repo and tools; all data is on the external SSD (Storage, below). **Optional training machine (owner):** his stronger home PC, used occasionally when he is home (e.g. fall break) for long `nqbot run` jobs — never for live trading, which stays on the laptop. Setup: the code lives in a **private GitHub repo** (`git pull` before working on a machine, `git push` after; `data/`, `models/` and `.env` are never committed), the data travels on the SSD (found by its marker file, so the drive letter doesn't matter), and `.env` is recreated on the PC from the owner's password manager. Both machines install the same Python and package versions from a committed lock file (`uv.lock` or `requirements.lock`, created at step 4a), so models trained on the PC load and behave identically on the laptop; LightGBM runs with fixed seeds and `deterministic: true`. Machine-specific resource settings (`max_workers`, `max_fold_mem_gb`, the low-memory switch `low_memory`; no other key is accepted) go in an uncommitted `config/local.yaml` (format: committed `config/local.example.yaml`) that overrides `settings.yaml` on that machine only; nothing that changes results may go there. A job interrupted by unplugging the SSD resumes on the other machine from its last chunk (`nqbot run` is resumable), and only one machine runs jobs on the SSD at a time (the job lock files are on the SSD; **two locks, owner 2026-10-05:** the Skylit lock (step-7 checks and Skylit pull jobs; the short step-7 checks that run alongside the step-10 backfill don't take it, owner 2026-10-06, §12 step 7) and the Databento lock (`databento plan` and Databento pull jobs), so the step-10 and step-8 backfills run in parallel on the laptop; every other job holds both; each lock records its machine, so a second machine is still refused; a lock left by a run whose SSD was unplugged can't remove itself, so `nqbot unlock [--skylit | --databento]` shows the holders and removes them with `--yes` after the owner confirms that job has stopped (never a lock whose holder is still running on this machine); `nqbot status` shows both locks; a dead process's lock on the same machine is reclaimed automatically. The two backfills fit the 16 GB laptop side by side: neither uses worker processes (`nqbot status` shows each job's measured peak memory), and the `min_free_gb` check reads the drive's free space before every chunk, so it counts both).
- **Overnight jobs (owner):** every long job (data pulls, feature/event rebuilds, detector grids, walk-forward folds, sweeps, monthly retrains) runs through `nqbot run <job> --until HH:MM` (default `--until 07:30`). Jobs are split into resumable chunks (one session, one month or one fold at a time) that save their progress; a chunk starts only if its measured 90th-percentile duration fits before `--until`, work is checked against the clock between work units, and the next `nqbot run <job>` resumes where it left off. Folds are split into sub-chunks (one lower model or one grid point each). Every output is written to a temp file, fsynced and renamed, so a killed chunk leaves nothing partial and loses at most that chunk. A rename that Windows refuses (an antivirus scan holding a fresh file) is retried with backoff for about 30 s (presd_fixes D5). `nqbot stop <job>` asks a running job to stop after the call or window it is in, its answer saved (status `stopped`; unlike Ctrl-C, which can throw a paid answer away; owner 2026-10-06); `nqbot run <job>` resumes it. Overnight runs need the laptop plugged in with sleep and the lid-close action off; `nqbot run` also asks Windows to keep the system awake while a job runs (`SetThreadExecutionState`, owner 2026-10-05; this doesn't stop lid-close sleep, so the lid action on AC stays "Do nothing") and warns (changing nothing; `nqbot setup-ssd` too) if `powercfg /qh` shows AC sleep or hibernate after a timeout, a lid action on AC other than "Do nothing", or USB selective suspend on AC, or can't read one of them (read by position, so a non-English Windows works too). The owner's laptop uses Modern Standby (S0 low-power idle, no S3): before the first night, check once that the keep-awake request holds there: start `nqbot run dummy`, let the screen turn off, and see `python.exe` listed under SYSTEM in `powercfg /requests`; the next morning `powercfg /sleepstudy` should show no standby during the run. From step 22, the pre-open job kills any `nqbot run` still running when it starts (`preopen_start`, 08:30). `nqbot run` refuses any pull or job that would leave less than `min_free_gb` free on the drive it writes to, and checks the SSD is mounted before every chunk (it stops cleanly, and resumes later, if the SSD is unplugged). Jobs use at most `max_workers` cores (start: all but 2) so the laptop stays usable, and, once live trading has started (step 22 on), **never run 08:30–16:15 ET on trading days** (the pre-open job and live trading own the machine then; `nqbot run` refuses to start or stops in that window, except `archive_live` when the pre-open job (`nqbot preopen`) calls it). Before that, jobs may run any time, and the time-critical Phase C pulls run as soon as they are approved. `nqbot status` shows each job's progress and estimated nights left.
- **Low memory (16 GB RAM, the default):** never hold all pair rows in memory. Each fold's LightGBM Dataset is built with `lgb.Sequence` readers that stream parquet row groups (event features joined per batch), `free_raw_data=True`, then saved with `save_binary` for reuse; features are float32; grid searches use all pair rows by default (`grid_sample_frac: 1.0`, owner prefers quality over speed; lowering it only shortens the runs and is the one 16 GB shortcut that could change results slightly); SHAP runs on ≤ 50k sampled rows; `mbp-1` is streamed per session. A fold whose estimated peak memory is over `max_fold_mem_gb` is refused and re-chunked. `max_workers` (setting, default all cores but 2) is capped at run time at floor((free RAM − 4 GB) ÷ the measured peak per session). Live trading needs only a few GB.
- **Storage:** convert every Skylit range response to zstd parquet as it is pulled — a per-strike table (`asOf`, symbol, strike, value as float32) and a per-board table (`asOf`, symbol, `spot`, `previousClose`, `meta.resolution`; `priceChange` = `spot` − `previousClose` when needed), since mapping needs each board's spot and aged-out history can't be pulled again (a step-7 unit test rebuilds R from the stored sample). Layout (`clients/skylit_store.py`): `raw/skylit/range/<date>/<HHMM ET>_{boards,strikes}.parquet` per 15-minute window, with the request parameters in the parquet metadata (a window is reused only with identical parameters, never silently re-pulled); `fullchain/` (step-7 strike-window calls), `historical/`, `darkpool/`, `vol/`, `stream/`; the CLAUDE.md rule-2 re-pull exceptions go to `repull/<reason>/…`, never over the original; `ledger.jsonl` logs every call the client makes (paid or failed) with its credits (each step-7 check that runs alongside the backfill without the lock writes its own `ledger_<check>.jsonl`; readers merge them all); a charged answer that can't be decoded or converted is kept in `failed/` and never retried. A window interrupted between its two file writes has its orphan file moved to `repull/partial_write/` and is pulled again (25 credits, rare; a CLAUDE.md rule-2 exception, owner 2026-10-05). Dark-pool dates pulled fewer than 5 sessions after their trade date are marked `provisional` in the file metadata (first received live, so the later top-ups may re-pull them); a recent trading day with no prints yet is never cached as empty. The month ledger (`settings.yaml → months`) maps each month number to one `YYYY-MM` or a list of them (the §12 step-4b month-1 merge) (≈ 15–25 GB per year instead of ~150 GB of JSON); Databento `trades` for NQ, ES and MNQ plus NQ `mbp-1` are ≈ 50–100 GB, no depth data is stored. Databento layout (`clients/databento_store.py`, step 8): `raw/databento/<schema>/<raw contract>/<CME trading day>.dbn.zst`, one request per file as delivered (zstd DBN, lossless), reused only if the file's own DBN metadata (dataset, schema, symbol, start, end) matches the request; `raw/databento/symbology/` (the contract resolutions) and `ledger.jsonl` (every resolve, cost and download call); 1- and 5-minute bars in `processed/bars/<NQ|ES>/<day>_{1m,5m}.parquet`, roll spreads in `processed/rolls/<root>_<roll day>.json`. About 230–280 GB is used at the start, growing ≈ 15–25 GB per month with top-ups and live logs. **Retention:** keep only the current and previous features version of processed files and caches, and delete LightGBM binaries of superseded folds after each production fit; `nqbot status` shows SSD use and the months left at the current growth (about 2.5 years on 1 TB with this rule). **Where data lives (owner):** **all** project data — `data/` (raw history, processed files, caches, the live folder and its archive), `models/`, `reports/`, logs, LightGBM and SHAP temp files and downloads — sits on the owner's **1 TB external USB-C SSD** under `paths.root`, so the laptop's internal drive stays clean. Plug it into a USB-C port (the 10 Gbps USB-C or the Thunderbolt 4 port), never the 5 Gbps USB-A port. Format it NTFS once before use (journaled, so an unplug can't corrupt the file system) and turn off USB selective suspend while it is plugged in. Temp files are created in the destination folder (same drive), so the rename is atomic. Live trading needs the SSD (§9 says what happens if it disappears). **USB drops (owner 2026-10-11):** every atomic write and ledger append whose device vanishes waits up to `SSD_DROP_WAIT_SEC` (30 s) for the drive and is retried once (a ledger line already written is never appended twice; a Databento download is never re-sent, since a stream is billed for the data sent); every drop is logged to `%LOCALAPPDATA%\nqbot\ssd_events.log` on the internal drive and, once the SSD is back, to `<root>/logs/ssd_events.log`. Every `nqbot` command holds Windows awake while it runs (with the screen off, Modern Standby powered down the Type-C controller and the SSD reset 1–2 minutes later). `nqbot verify --since D` reads back every file written since D (parquet in full, `.dbn.zst` decompressed frame by frame and its size checked against the download ledger, json parsed) and lists anything damaged in `<reports_dir>/verify/verify.md`. **Recording fallback (owner 2026-10-11, the only exception to "all data on the SSD"):** `step7 stream-session --fallback-local` records on the internal drive (`%LOCALAPPDATA%\nqbot\fallback\<root>`, same layout) if the SSD isn't reachable or writable at the start, or from the first part it can't take mid-session; after the close it moves the recording to the SSD itself (copied, fsynced, checksum-checked, then deleted locally; ledgers merged line by line; a differing file stays put and is listed), or `nqbot fallback-move` does it later. Nothing else ever falls back. **Backup (once, when step 10 finishes; owner 2026-10-06, presd_fixes D12):** Claude Code copies `data/raw/skylit/` and the dark-pool prints (≈ 15–25 GB of Skylit parquet plus the prints; size measured at step 7) to the owner's Google Drive folder (owner, 2026-10; §13) with **rclone straight from the SSD** (not Drive for desktop; Google One 100 GB bought; owner 2026-10-05; the owner authorises the rclone Google Drive remote once with `rclone config`) and verifies checksums (`rclone check`) — a copy on the same SSD is not a backup, and that history can never be pulled again. Command: `uv run nqbot backup [--until HH:MM]` (= `nqbot run skylit_backup`; `backup.py`, session C): checks that rclone runs and that the remote `backup.rclone_remote` (`gdrive`) exists, then per folder of `raw/skylit/` (one chunk per month under `range/`) `rclone copy --checksum` to `<backup.drive_folder>/raw/skylit/` (`NQ bot backup`) and `rclone check --one-way` (MD5); atomic-write temp files are never copied; the `range/` months go first and the folders a running check may still write (the `ledger*.jsonl` files, `vol/`, `stream/`, `repull/`) last. Each chunk id carries a fingerprint of its folder's files, so a re-run copies and checks only folders that changed; a failed check stops the job loudly (`backup_failed`). It holds the Skylit job lock (never while the Skylit backfill writes; the Databento backfill may run alongside); report `<reports_dir>/backup/backup.md`, rclone logs in `logs/`. The Drive copy never deletes: a file later moved aside on the SSD (a `repull/partial_write/` orphan, a superseded sample-day window) stays in its old Drive folder too, so a restore uses the SSD layout's rules (a window counts only with both files and matching parameters).
- **Expected run time on the laptop (rough):** a full 12-month feature/event rebuild ≈ 1 night; the step-13 detector grid ≈ 1–3 nights; the first full walk-forward (step 15) ≈ 4–8 nights with full-data searches, re-runs with cached searches ≈ 1–2 nights; step 19 ≈ 3–6 weeks of nights (F16.10: ≈ 16–26 nights before any cut, each cut adds a round of arms) (the order-flow detector grid, a rebuild, the walk-forward and the full-pipeline arms: seven ablation arms in the first backward-elimination round, one fewer in each later round, plus structure-only and the logistic baseline once each; arms reuse each scorer's cached grid point and half-life, which are re-searched only when features change, not on a relabel); the production fit ≈ 2–4 nights; a monthly retrain ≈ 1–3 nights (weekends are used when needed, since nothing runs 08:30–16:15 on trading days). Backtest replay runs in two passes: pass 1 replays session by session the full zone and structure-label engine (with the Skylit boards, no feature or pair output), since freshness depends on whole zones, to carry the multi-day state (freshness counts, unfilled single prints, weekly profile, yesterday's day type, dark-pool lists, rolling statistics) and writes `state_<date>.parquet`; pass 2 replays sessions in parallel, each starting from its checkpoint, and asserts that its end state equals the next checkpoint. Live writes the same checkpoint each evening (§9), including the rolling statistics (ATR, realized vol, big-trade threshold, opening-range averages) so warm-up never needs more than the sessions since the last checkpoint. Mapped-level and node-type timelines are cached per features version, and grid values that don't change them reuse the cache.
- Money and pulls: CLAUDE.md rule 2.
- Small, tested modules. Each detector has a unit test with a hand-built example.

---

## 12. Build steps (in order)

**Legend:** 🛠 setup · 💳 buy · ✅ GATE (must pass before continuing)

### Phase A — Setup (no new cost; steps 1–4 should be done within days, see Phase C)
1. 🛠 Install Python 3.11+, git, VS Code, and Claude Code. Create a **private** GitHub repo `nq-signal-bot`, clone it, put `CLAUDE.md` at the root and `SPEC.md` in `docs/`.
2. 🛠 Create `.env` with `SKYLIT_API_KEY` (existing). Sign up for **Databento** (the $125 signup credit covers cost estimates; the Standard subscription starts at step 8) → add `DATABENTO_API_KEY`. Create a Discord server + channel → Integrations → Webhooks → add `DISCORD_WEBHOOK_URL`; also create a Discord bot (Developer Portal) with message permissions and the **Message Content Intent** enabled (needed for `!` commands) → `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN`, and add your own Discord user ID → `DISCORD_OWNER_ID` and the private channel's ID → `DISCORD_CHANNEL_ID`.
3. 🛠 Format the external SSD as NTFS and plug it into a USB-C port (§11 Storage). Put the Skylit academy docs in `docs/skylit/`, the owner's notes in `docs/owner_notes.md` (done 2026-10), the archived gameplans (Google Drive folder "Jeron gameplans"; all `.md`, owner 2026-10-05) ready to download from the Drive website into `data/raw/gameplans/` once the SSD is set up (step 4b). Account rules (§6.1, §6.7: profit target, consistency %, drawdown size/type, daily loss limit, max minis/micros, scaling plan, risk per trade, commissions) are needed by step 14, when they are entered on the Control Panel's Accounts page (§6.7, §9b); flat-by is 15:59 on every account (owner). Zone widths and stops need no owner input (§5.4b, §6.2).
4. **4a (can be done before the SSD arrives).** Claude Code: scaffold the repo (layout in §11), `pyproject.toml` with a committed lock file, configs (plus an uncommitted `config/local.yaml` for machine resources), logging, pytest, `.gitignore`, a private GitHub repo, and the overnight job runner (`nqbot run <job> --until`, `nqbot status`, §11) that every later long job uses. Build `config/calendar.yaml` from 14 months before today through the coming months, listing the sources: past release times as scheduled at 08:30 ET on each day (from the agencies' release archives and schedule announcements; a postponement or cancellation counts only if announced before 08:30 that day; list each change with its announcement date, §9), published schedules for future dates (FOMC, BLS, BEA, Census, ISM, the Fed's calendar for the Chair's testimony and speeches), and CME equity-index holidays and early closes (§1.2); the owner confirms it at step 6; Claude Code refreshes it at the start of every month from then on and checks it at steps 18, 20 and 22. Write `session.flat_by` (15:59, owner) into `settings.yaml`.
   **4b (on the day the SSD is set up, just before step 7).** Set `paths.root` on the SSD and write its marker file `.nqbot_drive` with `nqbot setup-ssd` (built 2026-10-05, `docs/presd_fixes.md` issue 6): it uses the drive already carrying the marker, else the one named with `--drive E:` (a first setup always names it, since an internal second partition looks like any other drive letter; without it the drives are listed with their sizes); refuses a drive that isn't NTFS or has less than `min_free_gb` free; prints the power warnings (§11 Overnight jobs); computes the window and the month-1 merge below; prints the `settings.yaml` diff (only the `paths.root` and `months` lines change, comments kept, checked by loading it back) and writes nothing without `--yes` (then: the marker at the drive root, the `paths.root` folder, `settings.yaml`, which is committed). It never changes an already-set `paths.root`, and changes an already-set ledger only with `--rewrite-ledger`. Every read of the ledger checks it strictly (month numbers 1–12, quoted `"YYYY-MM"` strings, a list only for month 1, calendar months consecutive). Fix the 12-month window as the 12 complete calendar months ending with the last full month before the step-7 pull starts, and write the month ledger (§7 rule 2b: development months 1–6, walk-forward test months 7–9, sealed months 10–12) into `settings.yaml`; every later step uses it. If fewer than `min_month1_sessions` (start 10) sessions of the first month are still inside Skylit's 365-day reach when step 10 starts, that remainder is merged into month 1 together with the next calendar month (the window then ends one month later; that new month 12 is pulled with the step-18 top-up once complete, and the ledger in `settings.yaml` is rewritten when step 10 starts, before any data is looked at). A session counts as inside the reach if its 09:30 window still is. `nqbot setup-ssd` applies the merge on SSD day if it already applies; `nqbot setup-ssd --rewrite-ledger [--yes]` re-checks it when step 10 starts (it keeps month 1's calendar month, prints where each calendar month and the step-7 sample days move, and refuses once the step-10 Skylit backfill has a plan), and `nqbot skylit plan` / `nqbot databento plan` refuse while step 10 has no plan yet, the merge applies and the ledger isn't merged; once step 10 has a plan the ledger is fixed (a Databento plan made earlier is simply run again after a rewrite: its window follows the ledger). A ledger is merged once at most; if even the merged month 1 has fewer than `min_month1_sessions` sessions in reach (step 10 starting more than about a month after the SSD day), the command refuses: the whole window must move, a hand edit done with Claude Code. With the merge, the new month 12 is not complete when the plans are written, so they include only its days up to yesterday (sealed, never looked at); the rest of it comes with the step-18 top-up.

### Phase B — Spec
5. Claude Code: read `docs/skylit/`, `docs/owner_notes.md` + §5 and write `docs/feature_spec.md` — every concept, its measurable definition, tunable parameters with defaults, and a list of open questions. Also propose a start value for every backticked parameter anywhere in this spec that doesn't have one yet (e.g. `zone_proximity`, `confluence_tol`, `entry_ttl_sec`, `hold_reaction_pts`, label windows, `lean_window`, the rolling parameters, `regime_band`, `abs_*`, `exh_*`, `div_*`, `imb_ratio`, `abs_push_ticks`, `be_trigger`, `recency_halflife_months`, and the day-type model's ≤ `daytype_max_features` inputs), and propose every pre-declared grid (`threshold_grid`, the ≤ 4 breakeven rules, `style_set_grid`, the step-13/19 detector and execution-parameter grids, the `recency_halflife_months` and LightGBM grids, and the §7 rule 5 ablation groups), listed in §13 for the owner to approve at step 6.
6. ✅ **GATE: owner reviews and approves the spec.** The owner answers the step-6 open questions in §13.

### Phase C — Data (Skylit credits + Databento Standard $199/month)
**Time-critical — runs right after step 4b (SSD day), before step 6 if step 5 isn't done yet.** Before the SSD arrives Claude Code may already do step 5 (no data needed; the owner's step-6 review too) and write and unit-test the step-7, 8 and 9 code against mocked APIs (no pulls, nothing written to `data/`). Skylit range history only reaches 365 days back and Databento's included L1 history is the last 12 months, so the window's oldest days drop out of reach (Skylit) or become paid (Databento) every day. Order: step 7 → owner OKs the parameter check and the backfill credits → step 10 (Skylit, oldest day first) and step 8 (Databento) start the same day → steps 5–6, 8b and 9 start while step 10 is still running (5–6 and 9 need no pulled data; 8b needs only the step-7 sample days and their step-8 trades) → step 11 starts once gates 6 and 8b have passed, steps 8 and 10 cover the warm-up plus months 1–6, and the step-7 live-session results (`map_latency_sec`, stream parity) and `tempest_lag_sessions` are in (the dark-pool late-print check may still be running: if it changes `dp_lag_sessions`, the dark-pool levels are rebuilt before step 12) (months 7–9 must be in before the step-13 full event build and step 15; sealed months may arrive later, at step 18 or 20). Nothing in these pulls depends on the spec definitions (symbols, `maxExpirations=1`, `maxStrikes`, gamma and RTH are decided), and no outcome is looked at.
7. Claude Code: Skylit client (auth, retries, rate limits, credit tracking from `X-Credits-Remaining`). Read the OpenAPI spec; call `/v1/symbols` and confirm QQQ, NDXP, SPY, SPX are served and how far back each goes, whether SPX includes the 0DTE (SPXW) expirations, and that NDXP's front expiry is each day's 0DTE; confirm one `/v1/historical/range` call covers them all. First set the strike window: one 15-minute `/v1/historical/range` call on a busy development day and one per sample day, each with the API's largest `maxStrikes` (25 credits per call); `skylit_max_strikes` = the smallest window in which every strike ≥ `node_drop_pct` × the full-chain King lies at least `strike_edge_margin` (start 5) strikes from either edge on every board. Then pull **5 days from development month 6** (≈ 3,250 credits, enough for the step-8b check; the owner looks up the same dates on Atlas replay — it reaches back far enough) and report actual credits used and payload size. Cache them so the step-10 backfill doesn't re-pull them. Check that `maxStrikes` = `skylit_max_strikes` covers each sample day's strongest nodes. During one live session (this doesn't hold up step 10), record `/v1/stream` (all 4 symbols: QQQ, NDXP, SPY, SPX; `maxExpirations=1`, `maxStrikes`) for 15 minutes: measure the gap between boards, the delay from `asOf` to arrival (`map_latency_sec`), one reconnect with `Last-Event-ID`, and credits used (`stream-record` refuses a start whose 15 minutes plus 60 s would run past the close or `early_close_et`, stops at the latest 60 s after its 15 minutes even if nothing arrives (nothing received: refused, nothing written), and allows at most 3 reconnects, the forced one included; an incomplete recording is kept and marked for a re-record); then, at least one session later, pull the same 15 minutes from `/v1/historical/range` and compare board by board (the §3.1 Ingest parity and revision check), and confirm the 15-second `: ping` (`skylit_stale_sec` = 45 s). Read the Flowseeker dark-pool reference (Skylit docs; not in `/v1/openapi.json`), pull the 45 calendar days of QQQ/SPY prints before one development-month date (cached for step 10), confirm coverage, history depth (ideally ≥ 45 days before month 1; otherwise `dp_partial`, §3.1) and credits per call, and check that the bot's top-3 list matches `/v1/dark-pool/top-prints` for that date and a few later development-month dates. The same check pulls the last ≤ 31 days of those dates once more as one `date_start`/`date_end` call and compares it print by print with the one-date answers; step 10 uses 31-day calls only if they match (§3.1 Dark pools). **Late-print check:** on ≥ 5 mornings save the pre-open pull of the previous session's QQQ/SPY prints and the computed top-3, re-pull those dates ≥ 5 trading days later and compare print sets and top-3 lists before any events are built; if late prints change a list, `dp_lag_sessions` (features.yaml, start 1) makes backtest and live both use prints through session T−2. Check `/v1/vol/symbols` and `/v1/vol/history` (does it serve VIX, how far back); on two or more mornings record when the previous session's row (and VIX) first appears and whether re-pulled rows change, and set `tempest_lag_sessions` (if in doubt, 2; §5.2) once the finality check has compared the pre-open copies with a later re-pull (owner 2026-10-09: `step7 tempest-morning` stores a row from the pre-open of a later day, `step7 tempest-finality` re-pulls and compares). Pull 40 `/v1/historical` instants (5 credits each, ≈ 200 credits) spread over development months 1–6 to compare against the bot's node-type classifier once step 11 builds it (no outcomes are used). **A full-chain call that times out (owner 2026-10-06)** is replaced by consecutive shorter calls that together cover the same full 15 minutes (3 × 5 minutes; a 5-minute piece that still times out, 5 × 1 minute; each priced and printed first), so the proposal rests on exactly the same boards; a manifest records the pieces, and the strike window is never measured on less than the full 15 minutes (a piece that fails otherwise stops the check with no report). Once the strike-window check passes, `step7 oldest-window` pulls one 15-minute window (25 credits) of the oldest day still in reach (at least `skylit_reach_margin_min` + 1 h inside it) and reports whether its answer converts (the backfill starts with those days, which may be 1-minute data); the window is kept for the backfill (presd_fixes D1). Show the owner the parameter check (symbols, SPX and NDXP 0DTE, strikes window, resolution, the oldest-window test; it says NOT READY while the strike-window check fails) and the backfill estimate, with the credit shortfall and the pack that covers it (§3.1); on his OK (and the purchase), step 10 starts the same day. **Owner 2026-10-05:** this OK comes as soon as the strike window and the sample days check out; the stream test, parity, the late-print mornings and the Tempest mornings run alongside and never hold up step 10. **Owner 2026-10-06 (decision 2):** these short checks don't take the Skylit lock: each writes only its own files (`stream/`, `repull/<reason>/`, a new `vol/` file, its own ledger file; the late-print check keeps lookback dates the main cache lacks in `repull/late_print/lookback/`, which the backfill copies for free instead of paying again), and the backfill leaves `skylit_alongside_reserve_credits` for them.
8. 💳 Owner subscribes to **Databento Standard ($199/month)**. Claude Code: Databento puller → NQ + ES `ohlcv-1s` and `trades` for the 12 backfill months, NQ `mbp-1` (RTH 09:25–16:05 only; no ES `mbp-1`: ES features use trades only) for the same months, plus the warm-up (NQ and ES `trades` + `ohlcv-1s` for `warmup_sessions` sessions before the first backfill day, §3.2; any session lookback approved or tuned later is capped at the warm-up bought here, or the gap is bought after asking), and MNQ `trades` for the window (only to tell whether MNQ orders filled; no feature uses MNQ — every feature comes from NQ and ES); confirm the plan's live data covers NQ, ES and MNQ trades and NQ `mbp-1`. Estimate with `metadata.get_cost` first; the pay-as-you-go items are the warm-up and any Skylit-pulled day that has left Databento's included window: print the amount and ask the owner before buying. Resolve `NQ.v.0` / `ES.v.0` to one raw contract per session (§3.2). Build 1-minute and 5-minute bars from the trades. If the build later stalls for months, the owner may cancel the subscription and resubscribe before the step-18/20 top-ups and step 22 (newer months stay inside the included window).
8b. ✅ **GATE: level-mapping precision** (§5.1): using the step-7 Skylit day(s) and the step-8 NQ/ES trades, each chain meets the §5.1 pass conditions (owner 2026-10-08: (a) bias, (b) the chosen estimator within 10% of the best possible R, the alignment check in place of spot lag; the F1.6 step change and spot lag are diagnostics, and spot lag on a 1-minute day still decides the §3.1 delay), and the owner's Atlas replay check matches (the automated Atlas match is a regression check only, §13 Q8b-5). Must pass before step 11. (The backfill is already pulled for time reasons; a mapping problem changes only how the raw strikes are converted, never the pull.) The step-8 Databento job pulls the step-7 sample days first (their trades only: 8b needs no warm-up, since its realized vol uses the 15 minutes before each board from the same CME day), so this runs after the first night: `nqbot step8b precision` (per chain: median / p95 / p99 / max, by 30-minute time-of-day bucket and by tercile of trailing 15-minute realized vol of 1-minute futures log returns (owner 2026-10-05), the F1.2 rejection rate and `ratio_shift_day`s, and the stop floors per level source, `stop_buffer_floor`), `nqbot step8b spot-lag` (a diagnostic; on a 1-minute day it gives the §3.1 recommendation), the gate conditions `nqbot step8b bias`, `estimator` and `alignment` `--dates D1,…` (QQQ, SPY, SPX on development days; each day's crossings kept on disk; NDXP pending its method), `nqbot step8b recorded-day D [--with-prev]` after each owner-approved session recording (refuses until Databento has published the CME day, prints the price and pulls that day's NQ/ES trades at $0 only, fetches the free NDX/QQQ closes, fits every map's live content offset and alerts past floor(`asOf`) + 1 s, and scores both NDXP methods against the recording's real NDX at every board, plus (report only, owner 2026-10-11) at NDXP's relevant-node crossings; `--with-prev` also pulls the previous session's NQ trades for the basis method's settlement, after the owner's OK; owner OK for 2026-10-10: 10-08's NQ trades), then `nqbot step8b ndxp-select` (the leave-one-day-out NDXP choice, §13 Q8b-1), `nqbot step8b atlas --date D --minutes HH:MM,…` (the owner's Atlas table: relevant QQQ/SPY strikes with % of King, mapped level and band, and each time spot crossed the strike in the next 30 minutes, the futures price then next to the mapped level from the R in force), then `nqbot step8b report` → `<reports_dir>/step8b/mapping_gate.md`; the owner's Atlas check is the last condition.
9. Owner adds `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` to `.env`. Claude Code: gameplan parser (file → Claude API → JSON per §5.6, §3.4). Validate on every archived gameplan; flag anything it can't parse. Report gameplan days per month. When gameplan zones and inputs are used: §5.6. 
10. Skylit backfill (runs right after step 7, §12 Phase C), RTH only on every weekday of the window; on a `calendar.yaml` holiday one 15-minute probe call is made and the day is pulled only if it returns boards, and early-close days are pulled 09:30 to `early_close_et` plus one probe window (days with no data are logged; holidays and early closes filter sessions after the pull), `maxExpirations=1`, `maxStrikes` = `skylit_max_strikes`, gamma only: all 12 months, **oldest day first** (≈ 163k credits: the plan's balance plus a top-up pack bought on SSD day, so it runs straight through, §3.1). Report any day already beyond the 365-day limit (wholly, or partly: its lost windows) and the share of sessions served at 1-minute resolution (`<reports_dir>/step10/coverage.md` and `.json`, rewritten after every chunk: counts per month, and dates listed only for development months and the days before month 1 (§7 rule 2b); the run commands and credit stop are in §3.1 Backfill); back it up as in §11 Storage. Also pull QQQ/SPY dark-pool prints for the same months plus `dp_lookback_days` before month 1 (oldest first; 31-day `date_start`/`date_end` calls once the step-7 darkpool check has matched them print by print, else, and always within a day of the 365-day reach, one date per call in case the same 365-day limit applies (§3.1 Dark pools); a date the API refuses or answers empty on a trading day is reported, never cached as an empty day, and the affected sessions are flagged `dp_partial`, §3.1), and Tempest daily vol history (`/v1/vol/history`, QQQ and SPY, plus NDXP/SPX and VIX if step 7 found them served, from `warmup_sessions` + 2 sessions before month 1 (covers either Tempest lag)).

### Phase D — Structure engine and backtest (1-second data, structure-only entry styles (c)/(d))
11. Claude Code: features — VWAP/bands, daily (18:00 ET) and weekly developing profiles (POC and LVNs as levels, VAH/VAL premium/discount context, §5.3), TPO single prints carried across sessions until filled, IB high/low and highs/lows with sweep/cross/flip and retested extremes, freshness, Tempest daily vol context and the expected move (§5.2, §5.4), adaptive opening range + fib golden zone (§5.8), overnight range, mapping, node tracker (King, % of King and node types computed from the per-strike values, §5.2; touch counts; velocity rebuilt from snapshots), regime, rug, pika, Trinity, rolling ceilings/floors, dark-pool levels, zone widths (§5.4b), confluence clustering, day type. With unit tests and a no-lookahead test.
12. ✅ **GATE: visual check.** Plot 5 sample days from development months 1–6, one of them a roll-week day (§3.2 Roll days); owner confirms levels match Skylit and Deepcharts, shows the NDXP levels, and reviews the node-type agreement report (the bot's types vs Skylit's `nodeType` on the step-7 sample).
13. Claude Code: first run the reaction-distance report (§5.4b) on development months 1–4 to freeze the chart-level zone widths and the NDXP band (owner confirms). Then the event builder. Before labeling, measure `exit_slippage_ticks` on months 1–4: for every stop candidate (§6.2) that price later traded through, a stop order of the default size (NQ-equivalent contracts, §5.5b) fills against the best bid/ask size from `mbp-1` at the first touch and any remainder at the prices of the following trades in sequence until filled (no depth data); slippage = fill − stop in ticks, against the trade; take the 95th percentile. Then the labeler (every approach and touch → features → labels for entry styles (c) and (d) (order-flow styles join at step 19) × every structural stop candidate × every target candidate with net RR ≥ `pair_build_min_rr` (start 0.8) at default costs, plus the fixed 1:2.5 baseline; each account's true 1:1 floor is applied in selection, the simulators and live). Store the post-entry path data from §6.5 as per-candidate summaries (first-hit times for every stop/target/breakeven price, MAE, MFE, overshoot past stop, stopped-then-target, extension past target); the simulators read the shared per-session `ohlcv-1s`/trades arrays instead of a copy of the path per candidate. Build months 1–4 only first (the reaction report, slippage measurement and tuning need nothing else); output `data/processed/events.parquet`, `pairs.parquet` (one row per event × entry style × firing n × order type × stop × target) and `paths.parquet`. Then tune the detector parameters and freeze the execution parameters (§7.1 Tuning detector parameters) with a provisional structure model cross-validated inside months 1–4 (the §7.1 structure inputs minus the day-type input, LightGBM at its default grid point, day-blocked CV; one single-style policy per style, decided at decision time — (d) on every touch, (c) on every completed audit or sweep reclaim — each with its start order type, `entry_ttl_sec` and `stop_buffer`; each style's execution parameters are frozen on its own policy and detector values are scored on the sum of both, run through the sequential rule of §7 rule 3b (one trade at a time, each touch group once, setup windows, blackouts, time-to-live), so settings that merely create more events aren't rewarded; detector parameters first, then the execution parameters on the frozen events), and then build events, labels and paths for the full window once, with the winners.
14. Claude Code: Control Panel UI (§9b) — Accounts, Settings and Gameplan pages first (the prop simulator in step 15 reads the accounts), plus the Review page. Owner enters his accounts (profile stats on the Settings page show "n/a" until step 15), then reviews **50–100 cards** from development months 1–6 (Take/Skip + reason). Claude Code groups reasons → proposes rule fixes and new features → owner approves → re-run the affected step-13 tuning (reaction report, detector and execution grids) on months 1–4 → rebuild events. Repeat once if needed.
15. Claude Code: structure and day-type models, the zone-width report (§5.4b; no gameplan report before step 20: months 1–9 have no gameplan days), then the pair scorer over every stop × target candidate (§7.1); the fold schedule of §7 rule 2 (cross-fitted lower-model predictions for months 1–4, folds 7–9); LightGBM with logistic baseline; breakeven re-simulation (§6.4); purged walk-forward; calibration; prop simulator with bootstrap (§6.7); threshold sweep (§6.5b); report (including stopped-then-target and extension-past-target tables). The sealed months and top-up months are **not** touched here (§7 rule 2b).
16. ✅ **GATE (numbers in `settings.yaml` → `gates`):** on the walk-forward test months, the one-sided day-block bootstrap 90% lower bound (10th percentile) of realized R per trade after costs is > 0 (the report also prints the smallest R per trade that this trade count could detect); total R per month of the policy the §6.3 Proof keeps beats the fixed 1:2.5 baseline policy (§6.3 Proof; paired day-block bootstrap: point estimate of the difference > 0, its one-sided 90% lower bound reported); expected calibration error over 5 equal-count bins, computed on every filled decision-point candidate in the test months, has a point estimate ≤ `gates.gate_calib_max` (start 0.05) and a one-sided day-block bootstrap 90% upper bound (90th percentile) ≤ 2 × `gates.gate_calib_max` (10 bins once their weights sum to ≥ 500), and the same ECE on the traded pairs is reported; the three-loss rate ≤ `default_max_three_loss_rate` (at `default_risk_usd`, §6.5b); the prop simulator's pass rate (the bootstrapped evaluation pass rate of the preset stage planned for step 27, single account, no lock, that stage's per-fold thresholds) is reported, and gates only once the owner sets `gates.gate_pass_rate_min` (null until he decides). The sealed months are **not** scored here: they are scored once, at step 20, by the model that will go live. If not passing: improve features/definitions (hand-made work on development months 1–6 only) — do not buy anything else yet.

### Phase E — Order flow (L1 only: trades and `mbp-1`, already pulled at step 8)
17. Claude Code: build the order-flow features from the step-8 `trades` and `mbp-1` (big trades, delta, CVD, footprint imbalance, absorption, exhaustion, delta divergence, top-of-book features, §5.7; nothing new to buy) and the trigger-firing rule (§5.7), with unit tests on hand-built examples and the no-lookahead test. Real-data checks and plots of the detectors use development months 1–6 only.
18. Claude Code: **top up** history for every complete month after the backfill window (it is repeated at step 20 for months completed since): Skylit (≈ 650 credits/session, skipping days already cached by the evening checkpoint pull, §9; ask first if over 5,000 credits), QQQ/SPY dark-pool prints, Tempest daily vol, and Databento NQ + ES `ohlcv-1s` and `trades`, NQ `mbp-1` (RTH) and MNQ `trades` (included). These months join the sealed set (§7 rule 2b): they are built into events and labels but appear in no report until step 20.
19. Claude Code: trigger model; add entry styles (a) and (b), VAH/VAL area events (§5.4) and the absorption stop candidate (§6.2 item 5) to the events and labels (features version bump, §5.0); compare all four entry styles (a)–(d); retrain the pair scorer with trigger inputs. Order-flow detector parameters and the order type for styles (a)/(b) are tuned and frozen on months 1–4 first (§7.1); the trigger model follows the §7 rule 2 schedule (cross-fitted for months 1–4). Then re-run the full step-15 pipeline (breakeven choice and relabel, calibration, ablation, prop simulator, threshold sweep → `models/profiles.yaml`). Entry styles, breakeven and thresholds are chosen inside each fold (§7 rule 2); the ablation is reported on months 7–9 and the entry-style comparison on months 1–6; the sealed and top-up months are not scored here. This applies to whichever model goes live, with or without order flow.
20. ✅ **GATE: order flow adds lift over structure alone.** Ablation = full system (per-fold style choice from (a)–(d), plus order-flow inputs) vs structure-only = exactly the fallback system (per-fold style choice from (c)–(d), no area events, no stop candidate 5, no trigger model or order-flow inputs). Pass if the one-sided 90% lower bound of the **total-R-per-month** difference (paired day-block bootstrap: both systems resampled on the same days) is > 0, and the three-loss rate stays ≤ `default_max_three_loss_rate` (at `default_risk_usd`, §6.5b). If not, set `enabled_entry_styles: [c, d]` and drop area events, stop candidate 5, the trigger model and the order-flow inputs, keep Databento Live for NQ/ES trades, NQ `mbp-1` (fill simulation and step-24 scoring) and MNQ trades (fill inference) only (no order-flow features); the NQ/ES trades feed the structure features, which the live engine needs. Either way, every step-16 criterion must also hold on months 7–9 for the model that will go live. **Then top up any month completed since step 18 and, once, score the sealed set** (months 10–12 plus all top-up months, together, with fold 10 of that model, §7 rule 2; report the total and each block; fold 10 also scores the structure-only and fixed 1:2.5 arms, and their sealed differences are reported). Pass rule: §7 rule 2b. If it fails, go back and apply the re-seal rule. After a pass, run the gameplan check (§5.6) on the now-scored months; then the production fit runs (with the gameplan inputs if the check passed and ≥ `gameplan_min_days` exist).

### Phase F — Live auto-execution on a Tradovate demo account
21. 💳 Owner subscribes to **TradersPost Starter (~$49/month)**, connects a **Tradovate demo (sim) account as a broker connection** (TradersPost's own paper mode can't use Tradovate data, so use a real Tradovate demo login), creates a strategy + webhook (strategy subscription: **Allow signal override** on, adding off, no default stop-loss or take-profit, no order-type override), and adds its URL to `.env` as `TP_WEBHOOK_<ACCOUNT>`. Claude Code builds `clients/traderspost.py` (build and POST signals: entry bracket, breakeven, exit; retries for `exit` and `breakeven` only — entries are never resent (§10.3); an `extras.order_id` already sent (journal, §9) is refused; `exit` / `breakeven` are exempt; error handling), `live/sizing.py` (§6.1), `live/execution.py` (send entry, watch price for breakeven, log everything), the hard limits from §10.3 and the kill switch, with unit tests on a mocked webhook. Then it runs the nine verification tests in §3.5 (the owner also sets up a second demo account as a TradeSyncer follower for test 9), measures end-to-end latency (§3.5) and sets `order_latency_sec` to its 95th percentile. If it is above the 1.0 s placeholder, the **execution-change re-run** follows: labels are rebuilt, the execution parameters are re-frozen on months 1–4, per-fold steps (1)–(4) are re-run for folds 7–9, which must still meet the step-16 criteria and the step-20 full-vs-structure-only test (else the fallback system; if the step-16 criteria fail, stop before step 24 or 27 and ask the owner), and the production fit re-runs (not re-scored; steps 24–25 validate it, §7 rule 2); the same applies if step 22 has to raise `tempest_lag_sessions` (§5.2). It also records whether the demo fills a resting limit on a touch: if so, for sim accounts (per account: `accounts.yaml → sim_fills`, default true; all of the owner's accounts are Tradovate sim) the live fill inference treats a touch as filled (no "maybe filled"), and the simulator used for the step-25/28 comparisons does the same (training labels keep trade-through). The owner enters the demo account with the same rules, caps, commissions and flat-by as the prop account planned for step 27. On a `demo: true` account, reaching the profit target or the floor starts a new simulated evaluation at the next session: the bot posts it, and the owner clicks **New evaluation** on Live Status (it records the current balance and new floor as the evaluation start in `account_state.json`; not a sweep-relevant edit). Demo trades keep counting toward step 24, and the step-25 simulator interval uses the same restarts. ✅ **GATE: all nine §3.5 tests pass before step 24, and the SSD passes a sustained-load test with zero disconnects before any live trading (owner 2026-10-11):** at least 2 hours of continuous mixed reads and writes on the SSD on the live machine, on AC and with the screen off, through the live port and cable; pass = no `disk` 51, no `Ntfs` 140, no new mount of the SSD's volume (`Ntfs` 98) in the System log and no line in `ssd_events.log` for the whole run (§11 Storage; the 2026-10-08..11 drops, §13).
22. Claude Code: live engine (asyncio), with the pre-open warm-up and restart journal of §9: Skylit `/v1/stream` (QQQ, NDXP, SPY, SPX; hourly reconnect with `Last-Event-ID`) + Databento Live (NQ and ES trades, NQ `mbp-1`, MNQ trades; intraday-replay reconnect, §9) → same feature code → models → chosen entry/stop/target/size → orders through TradersPost (auto). Risk module, logging, reconnect handling, session schedule, the guard process, the evening archive job and the Task Scheduler pre-open entry (§9). Also record when Tempest's previous-session row first appears (§5.2). Live Status page in the Control Panel; Discord notifications, health warnings, end-of-day summary and all §9c commands. Runs on the owner's own computer.
23. ✅ **GATE: replay test.** Claude Code — feed a historical day through the live engine (orders to a mocked webhook); it must produce the same event ids and decisions as the backtest, with features equal within 1e-9; a second run drops the Databento feed for 30 seconds and a third for 10 minutes mid-session (intraday replay mocked from the cached files; each drop run is compared with the policy simulator replayed with the same outage injected — blocked from drop start + `feed_stale_sec` to recovery + `feed_recovery_sec` — and features, events and decisions must be identical, with no entries inside the blocked span); two more runs mock a Skylit silence longer than `skylit_stale_sec` filled from `/v1/historical/range` (§3.1) and an engine killed mid-session and rebuilt from the journal (§9): features must be identical to the backtest from fill/rebuild + `feed_recovery_sec`, with no entries before that; the engine's clock is injectable, so in replay local time = `ts_event` + the measured receive lag and every wall-clock deadline (time-to-live, `entry_end`, flat-by, blackout starts) matches the backtest; a fourth replays the busiest development-month session (e.g. a CPI day, 09:30–10:30) at real-time speed on the laptop with the UI open, and the 99th-percentile processing lag must stay below `feed_lag_max_ms` (else the order-flow detectors move to their own process).
24. Run auto-execution on the demo account during the session until 100+ live events are scored and 30+ demo trades are done (typically 4–8 weeks). Every decision-point candidate, traded or not, is scored on live ticks with the training-label (trade-through) fill rules whatever `sim_fills` says; only the demo-trade comparisons use `sim_fills`. Owner does review round 2 on the live trades. Fill and order reconciliation is **automatic where possible**: step 21 checks whether TradersPost offers a trade log/API; if not, the owner uploads Tradovate's daily **Orders** report (all orders, including cancelled) and **Fills** report on Live Status, only during validation (steps 24 and 27; no automated web download, no Tradovate login stored); afterwards it is optional, since the morning balance confirmation already corrects the bot's estimates. Claude Code also compares the live pre-open dark-pool top-3 lists with the backtest's reconstruction for the same days (§3.1) and, once those days are pulled at the step-27 top-up, the Skylit boards logged live with that history. Claude Code builds the importer: it checks that every entry leg had a stop and a target created with it, measures slippage and send-to-fill latency (send log vs order/fill time), and imported results override the bot's inferred fills, P&L and caps. No prop account trades before gate 25 passes (owner: demo first).
25. ✅ **GATE: over those live events and demo trades, calibration-in-the-large holds (the mean predicted P of the scored best pairs lies inside the two-sided day-block bootstrap 90% interval (5th–95th percentile) of their realized hit rate; the full step-16 test once ≥ 500 live events exist); the mean R of the demo trades stays inside the policy simulator's two-sided 90% interval for that trade count (built from the out-of-fold trades of months 7–9 and the sealed set); no missing brackets; entry slippage ≤ `max_entry_slip_ticks`; stop slippage: if the measured 95th percentile (from the step-24 imported fills; needs ≥ 20 stop fills, else stop fills plus market exits — flat-by, news, owner — never target fills) exceeds `exit_slippage_ticks`, set it to the measured value, run the step-21 execution-change re-run at the new cost (net RR, `R_be`; an execution-only fix: steps 24–25 don't repeat); daily caps never breached (no entry sent after a daily stop and none sized above the remaining allowance at the configured slippage; overshoot from slippage beyond that is reported, not a failure); `order_latency_sec` and `map_latency_sec` re-checked against live (§5.5b, §3.1).** If it fails: execution-only fixes → repeat steps 24–25 with no refit; fixes to features or model code → the step-27 re-check, then the production fit, then repeat steps 24–25.

### Phase G — Prop accounts
26. 🛠 Done 2026-10: MyFundedFutures confirmed a TradersPost bot is allowed if supervised. That check covered MFFU firm-wide; the Pro 50k type (added 2026-10-08) was not checked on its own, so re-read the Pro terms on automation before going live on a Pro account. Before going live, re-read MyFundedFutures' current automation rules for the exact account types.
27. First pull every complete month since step 20 (as in step 18). If round-2 fixes were applied and not already re-checked after step 25, re-check first: the fixed model's walk-forward over every scored month from 7 onward (excluding live months used for the round-2 fixes) must meet the step-16 criteria, and the first complete month after the fixes are frozen (the demo keeps running meanwhile) is scored once by fold j, where j is that month, with the §7 rule 2b pass rule. A round-2 fix to features or model code repeats steps 24–25 on demo first (as on the gate-25 fail path), and any feature or engine code change after step 23 — here or at a later retrain — re-passes the gate-23 replay before it trades. Then connect **one** prop account; auto-execution with the normal sizing (§6.1: NQ and/or MNQ, whatever fits the stop and risk best), owner supervising, for 2+ weeks; record whether the firm auto-flattens at its session close. Daily orders/fills reconciliation as in step 24, and every limit the bot counted as filled on a touch (`sim_fills`) is checked against the fills import: gate 28 requires them to have filled, else that account gets `sim_fills: false` and its sweep is re-run; compare fills, slippage and R against the demo and the backtest.
28. ✅ **GATE: prop execution matches the demo results (no missing brackets, the 95th percentile of prop stop fills ≤ the configured `exit_slippage_ticks` (with fewer than 20 stop fills, gate 25's fallback set: stop fills plus market exits, never target fills; judged once ≥ 20 such fills exist, and scaling waits until then), every entry fill ≤ `max_entry_slip_ticks`, daily caps never breached as defined at step 25).** Then scale to more accounts (before the first prop TradeSyncer follower copies live trades, repeat test 9 on it): TradeSyncer followers are sized by their TradeSyncer multiplier; only extra TradersPost connections are sized by their own risk.

### Phase H — Ongoing
29. Retrain every `retrain_every_months` (§7.1) on the new months rebuilt from pulled history (live logs feed the fill, slippage and drift checks), judged as in §7.1 Retraining; refresh `config/calendar.yaml`; review rounds as needed; re-run breakeven simulation; pull each new complete month as in step 18 (Skylit, dark-pool prints, Tempest, Databento NQ + ES `ohlcv-1s`/`trades`, NQ `mbp-1`, MNQ `trades`). The home PC remains the only fallback if the laptop proves too slow (§11).

## 13. Open questions

**Checked by Claude Code during the build (no owner input needed):**
- **SSD-day dry run (2026-10-05):** 17 issues found, owner decisions recorded; all fixed before the SSD in sessions A, B and C: `docs/presd_fixes.md`.
- **Pre-SSD session C (2026-10-05), Claude Code's choices:** the step-8b report's time-of-day buckets are 30 minutes and the Atlas table lists strike crossings in the 30 minutes after each picked minute (both kept by the owner, 2026-10-06); a crossing's moment is interpolated between the two boards whose `spot` straddles the strike, and the futures price is read then (only crossings between 1-second boards count toward the tolerance; 1-minute ones are shown for information); `stream-record` allows 3 reconnects and stops 60 s after its 15 minutes at the latest. Owner answers the same day: 8b volatility split = trailing 15-minute realized vol of 1-minute futures returns; dark pools by 31-day calls once step 7 proves them; the backup covers `raw/skylit/` only (remote `gdrive`, folder `NQ bot backup`).
- **Step 8b Atlas table (fixed 2026-10-07):** a spot landing exactly on a strike was counted as two crossings (arrival and departure, same second); the departure row used the R of the board whose own spot was the strike, so it always showed 0.00. A touch now counts once, on its arrival; the gate report lists every sample day's table (a missing day is pending) (`docs/step8b_findings.md` §6).
- **Answered 2026-10-05 (step-7 code review): yes**, a 15-minute window whose two files were not both written (crash or unplug mid-write; the orphan is kept) may be pulled again: added to the CLAUDE.md rule-2 re-pull exceptions.

A row that exists and changes before the close is never used for that session (`tempest_lag_sessions` ≥ 1 uses the previous session's row). **Results (2026-10-08/09):** poll #2 (started 15:19 ET 10-08) saw no row before the close; the row appeared at 16:06:56. The morning re-pulls of 10-09 08:48 ET (`tempest-compare`) changed 121 cells of the 10-07 copy pulled at 16:08 and 119 cells of the 10-08 copy pulled at 16:07 (most fields, all 5 symbols): the post-close row is a draft. **Owner 2026-10-09:** only a copy pulled at the pre-open of a later day is stored and used (§3.1 Tempest, §9 pre-open job); the 16:07 copies of 10-07 and 10-08 were moved to `repull/tempest_draft/` and replaced in `vol/` by the 08:48 re-pulls (0 credits); `tempest_lag_sessions` stays at its start value until the finality check below.
- **SSD drops (2026-10-08..11, owner 2026-10-11):** the Fanxiang PS1008 (JMicron JMS583 USB-NVMe bridge, UASP driver `UASPStor`, on the Intel Type-C/Thunderbolt xHCI 8086:A831 root hub port 3, running as USB 3) was surprise-removed and re-mounted 51 times (HarddiskVolume8 → 110, disk device DR1 → DR51) with `disk` 51 and `Ntfs` 140; chkdsk clean. Pattern: every drop came 1–2 minutes after a Modern Standby entry (Kernel-Power 506) while a command that didn't hold Windows awake (step 8b, databento condition) was using the SSD; none during the `nqbot run` backfill and backup nights (no standby entry then), and more on battery (shorter screen timeout, USB selective suspend on). Power plan: Balanced only, no plan change logged (Razer Cortex didn't swap plans; the AC selective-suspend setting hadn't reverted, the battery one was never set); USB 3 link power management and PCIe link-state power management at maximum savings on AC and battery. Fixed in code: every command holds Windows awake; writes retry once after a drop; drops logged; recording fallback (§11 Storage). Owner: power settings (USB 3 LPM off, selective suspend off on battery, PCIe ASPM off), Razer Cortex removed, cable/port/heat; the step-21 soak test is the gate. Confirmed the same evening: `nqbot verify` read all 66.9 GB written since 10-04 with no drop and found nothing damaged (three old task logs were UTF-16, now read as such); then at 22:26:08 the laptop entered Modern Standby with no nqbot command running, a test run started inside it at 22:27:41, and the SSD dropped at 22:29:23. A command that holds Windows awake from its start keeps standby away, but one started after standby began doesn't end it, so long jobs also need the display-off timeout on AC set to never (or the screen kept on) while they run.

- **Q8-degraded Databento non-available days (added 2026-10-09). ✅ Answered by the owner 2026-10-09: approved as proposed, the five values unchanged; written into §3.2 (Dataset condition).** `get_dataset_condition` over every UTC date of the step-8 window (warm-up included) lists 10 `degraded` dates, the rest `available`: 2025-09-17, 2025-09-24, 2025-11-28, 2026-01-31, 2026-03-15, 2026-03-16, 2026-03-21, 2026-04-10, 2026-05-24, 2026-08-29 (four are Saturdays and touch no CME session). The measured damage per session is in `<reports_dir>/step8/step8_report.md` (completeness only, for every month: no prices, levels or outcomes, §7 rule 2b). **Proposed rule, fixed 2026-10-09 from completeness only, before any outcome on those days was looked at** (written before the damage numbers were read; parameters in `settings.yaml`, nothing reads them until approved):
  1. A UTC date that is not `available` maps to the parts of the CME days it overlaps: ETH (18:00 ET → 09:30, and the RTH close → 17:00) and RTH (09:30 → 16:00 or the early close). A date touching no CME day (a weekend) needs nothing.
  2. Each part is measured per series (NQ `trades`, ES `trades`, NQ `mbp-1` in RTH) against the same part on the `databento_damage_baseline_sessions` (20) nearest sessions whose UTC dates are all available. A part is **damaged** if its empty minutes exceed the normal maximum by more than `databento_damage_excess_minutes` (5), or a gap is longer than both the normal maximum gap and `databento_damage_gap_sec` (120 s). Its **damaged minutes** are the minutes inside such gaps plus its excess empty minutes. A degraded date whose parts are all undamaged is kept unchanged (listed in the report).
  3. **RTH, small damage** (damaged minutes, union over the three series, ≤ `databento_damage_session_minutes` (30)): drop only the events (training rows, labels, backtest and simulator trades) whose span, from the touch to the end of the label horizon, comes within `databento_damage_window_pad_min` (15) of a damaged minute; the rest of the session stays. Cumulative session features after the damage (VWAP, session profile, CVD) use the stored data as it is.
  4. **RTH, large damage** (> 30 damaged minutes): drop the whole session (no events, labels, scoring or simulator days). The levels it would hand to the next session (prior-day high/low, value area, POC) are left empty there, the way any missing value is.
  5. **ETH only:** RTH events are kept. If the ETH part has > 30 damaged minutes, the overnight-derived levels and features of that session (ON high/low, overnight profile) are left empty; otherwise used as stored.
  6. The rule is applied by code to every month alike; sealed and walk-forward test months are handled without anyone looking at their outcomes (§7 rule 2b), and the report shows completeness only.
  **Measured 2026-10-09 (after the rule above was written; completeness only):** every RTH part of every degraded date is within the normal range (NQ/ES `trades` 0 empty minutes, longest gap ≤ 27 s; NQ `mbp-1` 0 empty, ≤ 1.2 s; 2025-11-28's early-close RTH has one same-length normal session to compare with). The damage is overnight: **2025-11-28 (month 2) ETH is missing 21:44 ET 11-27 → 08:30 ET 11-28** in NQ and ES `trades` alike (647 / 645 of 870 minutes empty; the stored `ohlcv-1s` has the same hole), so the rule leaves that session's overnight levels and features empty and keeps its RTH; small ETH damage, used as stored under the rule: 2025-09-17 (warm-up) NQ 24 empty minutes vs a normal max of 2, longest gap 193 s; CME day 2026-03-16 NQ one 121 s gap; CME day 2026-03-17 (18:00–20:00 ET on 03-16) NQ 4 empty minutes, 132 s gap. Undamaged: 2025-09-24, 2026-03-15, 2026-04-10, 2026-05-24 (Memorial Day Globex). No session is dropped. The Saturdays 2026-01-31, 03-21, 08-29 touch no session.
  Question as asked: approve the rule and its five values as proposed (or change them)?
- **Q8-degraded-2 Re-pulling an upgraded day (added 2026-10-09). ✅ Answered by the owner 2026-10-09: approved; written into §3.2 and CLAUDE.md rule 2.** If Databento later marks a stored day `available`, the recorded condition change goes to `raw/databento/condition/upgrades.jsonl` and every pull report flags it; nothing is re-pulled (it is not one of CLAUDE.md rule 2's re-pull exceptions). Proposed new exception: an upgraded day may be re-pulled once, only after the owner's OK for that day; `metadata.get_cost` printed first (inside the included window it should print $0; a priced one needs `--approve-usd` like any paid pull); the old files are moved to `repull/degraded/` (kept, never deleted); the affected events are rebuilt (features version, §5.0) and Q8-degraded's rule is re-applied with the new condition. For a sealed or test month the swap is mechanical (completeness only, no outcome looked at). Question as asked: approve this exception?

  - *For information (no answer needed):* 2025-11-28 (Globex outage, early close) is kept (owner, 2026-10-04). The 2027 BLS, BEA, Census and ISM schedules aren't published yet, so the monthly refresh fills them in. 2027 FOMC minutes dates are derived and 2027 press conferences assumed. Funding runs to 2026-12-11, so a lapse could move the Dec-2026 releases. Optional: Bash deny rules for `.env` in `.claude/settings.json`.
- **Step 9, gameplan parser v2 (2026-10-07). ✅ All five answered by the owner 2026-10-07** (written into feature_spec F8, F5.9, F5.10, F11.2 and §5.6; new setting `gameplan_ny_am_end` 12:00):
  1. **"Settled bar"** with no timeframe: = the §5.5 settled bar (1-minute); if the gameplan names a timeframe, that one.
  2. **Scenario set:** the set matching the event's time: `ny_am` during the NY AM window (09:30 to `gameplan_ny_am_end`), `session` otherwise; if the gameplan has only one set (`unspecified` included), that one.
  3. **ES gate wording:** "holding" / "reclaiming" X = ES above X; "losing" / "rejecting" / "failing" X = ES below X; anything else stays unclear, and an unclear gate gives an empty feature, never a guess. (Claude Code's reading, in F8: a gate given as a range holds only when ES is beyond the whole range, and is empty inside it.)
  4. **HVL and HVL0** are two separate features (distance to each), empty when not given; never pick one. A target range is a band like any other level: reaching its near edge counts as reaching it; order uses the middle.
  5. **Gameplan invalidations are features only, never trading rules** (rule 6): "invalidation as written has been met", per timeframe: acceptance = a bar of the stated minutes (15 if not given) closing beyond; settled_bar = answer 1; not_stated = a settled 1-minute close (the §5.2 chart-level default); other = empty feature. A gameplan zone is crossed when price trades past its far edge.
- **Step 9, parser v3 (2026-10-07). ✅ Answered by the owner 2026-10-07: an entry with an empty zone uses a single-price zone at `trigger.nq_price`, like other single-price zones in F8** (written into feature_spec F8). The question was: F8 treats a gameplan zone as a container [`zone`[0], `zone`[1]]; a conditional entry has `zone` null and its level in `trigger.nq_price`. Use it as a single-price zone [p, p] at `trigger.nq_price` (its touch by `zone_proximity`, its own stop and targets as candidates), or only as a feature of the zone it breaks out of? Rec.: the single-price zone, since F8 already keeps single-price zones.
- **Step 8b (✅ gate), first run 2026-10-07: every condition fails** (F1.6 p95 QQQ 4.63 NQ, NDXP 5.73 NQ, SPY 1.04 ES, SPX 0.68 ES; spot lag −1 s on 03-06/03-11, NDXP −46 s on 03-18; Atlas crossings 40–63% within tolerance). Owner 2026-10-07: find the causes read-only and change no gate limit or definition until he decides. Evidence, method and the before/after table: `docs/step8b_findings.md`. Until these are answered, nothing below changes and step 11 waits:
  - **Q8b-1 NDXP spot.** History's NDXP `spot` is an NQ futures last price, not NDX (it equals the NQ trade 1 s after `asOf` and switches between H6/M6/U6; U6 confirmed against NQU6 1-second bars). The live stream's NDXP spot is the index. **Owner 2026-10-07/08: keep NDXP and fix it; choose the method with the lower error.** Two methods, both known before the open (findings R6, R9): (1) basis + carry (previous NQ settlement ÷ NDX official close, per contract, decayed to expiry; day-ahead p95 15.1 NQ, bias +0.2); (2) QQQ × k (NDX ≈ QQQ spot × k, k = NDX close ÷ QQQ official close over the previous N sessions, dividend-adjusted across ex-dates; N = 5 day-ahead p95 13.8, bias +2.2). Sources are free: FRED `NASDAQ100`, Nasdaq's quote API for QQQ closes and ex-dividend dates. Live 2026-10-07 against the stream's real NDX: basis + carry p95 14.4 / 10.0, QQQ × k 2.9–10.8 depending on N. **Pending:** the session recordings decide, chosen leave-one-day-out across the three recordings (owner 2026-10-08): candidates basis + carry and QQQ × k with N ∈ {5, 10, 20}; hold one day out, pick the candidate with the lowest p95 on the other two, score it on the held-out day, rotate; each candidate's held-out p95 and median are reported, the method and N with the best held-out p95 win (every-board scoring stays the main method, owner 2026-10-11); report only, the same candidates' p95 at relevant-node crossings (F2.4 on the NDXP board in force, the gate's crossing rule) is shown next to it, and the proposed NDXP `stop_buffer_floor` is the larger of the winner's every-board held-out p95 and its crossing p95, so stops are never tighter than crossings show; the owner decides on it (owner 2026-10-11; `nqbot step8b ndxp-select`), (owner 2026-10-08: record `--maps NDXP,QQQ,SPY` on all three sessions, ≈ 1,185 credits each (≤ 1,254 with 12 reconnects); `step7 stream-session`, Windows task 09:27 ET 10-09, 10-12, 10-13; $0 Databento NQ and ES trades for each recorded day, priced first). NDXP keeps the ±20 band until the step-13 reaction-distance report. The Skylit question stays open (findings §8).
  - **Q8b-2 Board effective time (ingest rule). Answered (owner 2026-10-08), written into §3.1 Ingest, §5.1, F0.1 and F1.1 (`board_content_offset_ms`, `skylit_stamp_change_date`; SPY from 03-17 and NDXP pending the recordings):** apply a board at max(arrival, floor(`asOf`) + 1 s + `map_latency_sec`) in every era and live (history: no arrival), and read the futures at the board's measured content time (`board_content_offset_ms` per era and map; medians in findings R4/R10). Measured content times never pass floor(`asOf`) + 1 s (NDXP exactly +1 s). An alignment check (`step8b alignment`, to be built) measures the offset on every recording against the futures and alerts if a content time falls after floor(`asOf`) + 1 s. SPY's live offset (+0.3/+0.5 s on 10-07 vs −0.06 s in March) is re-measured on the next recording that includes SPY. **Owner 2026-10-10: QQQ's live offset joins SPY's in Wednesday's decision (after the third recording):** fitted live QQQ −80 / −140 ms (10-07) and −320 ms (10-09, −220 to −320 by hour) vs the configured −60; SPY +520 / +320 (10-07) and +560 ms (10-09, +440 to +620 by hour). Neither puts a live board's content after floor(`asOf`) + 1 s (the correlation peak's upper edge is at most +620 ms), so the alignment check does not alert; no setting changed (findings R14). Content offsets compare Skylit `asOf` with Databento `ts_event`, so the laptop clock does not enter them.
  - **Q8b-3 R estimator. Answered (owner 2026-10-08), written into §5.1 and F1.7 (`ratio_window_sec`, `ratio_gap_reset_sec`; NDXP pending):** QQQ trailing 60-s median; SPY trailing 60-s median (the lowest event-moment level error of per board / 15 / 30 / 60 s: 0.11 ES vs 0.16, 0.26 and 0.89; findings R8); SPX per board (aligned) with F1.2; NDXP per Q8b-1. Trailing only, over applied boards by content time; reset at the session's first applied board (after `ratio_open_skip_sec` for SPX/NDXP), at a contract change (only ever between sessions) and after a board gap > `ratio_gap_reset_sec` (10 s); warm-up: after a reset the median uses the applied boards since it, the first board alone at first, no minimum count; one function for backtest, rebuilds and live.
  - **Q21-resume Hourly reconnect with `gapCovered=False` (for step 21, the live tests; 2026-10-10; open).** Observed on the 2026-10-09 recording (`docs/step8b_findings.md` R14): Skylit ends every stream connection after an hour (`reconnect`, reason `max_duration`, at 10:27, 11:27, 12:27, 13:27, 15:09 ET) plus occasional `server_shutdown` reconnects (14:09, 15:26); every resume took 0.2–0.4 s. 3 of 7 resumes said `gapCovered=False` (10:27 NDXP, 12:27 QQQ, 15:09 NDXP), each with that symbol `advanced` from `lastAsOf` to a `currentAsOf` exactly one second later, and the recording holds every second's board around all three: nothing was missed. §3.1 already says a resume sends only the latest board and that a gap is pulled from `/v1/historical/range` only after `skylit_stale_sec` or a `lagged` event. Proposal: on every resume, the engine reads `gapCovered` and each symbol's `lastAsOf`/`currentAsOf`; if a symbol advanced by more than one board step (currentAsOf − lastAsOf > 1 s), it pulls the missed seconds for that symbol from `/v1/historical/range` (the §3.1 gap fill, rule-2 exception; a few credits) and applies them by the ingest rule before the current board, logging the gap; a one-second advance needs nothing. Entries stay blocked only by the existing staleness rule. Step 21 tests: a resume with `gapCovered=False` advanced by 1 s (no pull, no block), by 30 s (pull, boards applied in order, no lookahead), and `gapCovered=True` (nothing). The step-8b scores leave out a window around each uncovered resume anyway (findings R14). Separate observation: the Windows Time service was not running on 2026-10-09 (the laptop clock was at least ≈ 0.6 s behind: boards 'arrived' before their content; arrival − asOf drifted ≈ −0.1 s more over the session); the owner started it and resynced on 2026-10-10. §9 Clock needs it synced before live, and `map_latency_sec` is re-measured on the 10-12 and 10-13 recordings. **Which way the clock biases `map_latency_sec` (owner 2026-10-10):** the delay is arrival (laptop clock) − `asOf` (Skylit's clock). A laptop clock running δ behind stamps every arrival δ too early, so the measured delay is the true delay − δ: a clock behind makes `map_latency_sec` **too small**, never too large. 0.59 was measured on 10-07 (14:59 recording, p99.9 0.585 s), not 10-09; that day's bound says the clock was at least ≈ 0.07 s behind, with no upper limit, so the true 10-07 delay was at least ≈ 0.66 s and possibly more. (10-09's clock, ≥ 0.6 s behind, would have understated it by ≥ 0.6 s.) Too small is the risky direction: the backtest would apply a board up to δ before live could have it (a small lookahead). **Decision (owner 2026-10-10):** the 10-12 and 10-13 recordings, with the clock synced, re-measure the delay (p99.9 over both, and the arrival − content bound to confirm the clock); Wednesday sets `map_latency_sec` from them before the gate re-run, and if the value changes, the gate (bias, estimator), the Atlas tables and the volatility numbers are re-run with it. Content offsets, alignment and the gate's `asOf`-based timing don't use the laptop clock and are unaffected. (findings R14)
  - **Q11-instab Mapping-instability feature (for step 11, feature_spec; owner 2026-10-09; proposed, not built).** Add per chain (QQQ, SPY, SPX; NDXP once its method is chosen) the trailing dispersion and the largest change of the per-board R over the last 30–120 s (trailing only: boards applied by t, no lookahead), so the model can learn that ETF-mapped levels are unreliable in dislocations like 2026-03-31 12:43 (R moved ≈ 0.5% in two minutes, findings R12). Questions for step 11: the window (30, 60 or 120 s, or all three), dispersion as the MAD or the range of per-board R in bps, and whether the largest change is measured board to board or as max − min over the window. Evidence on how often such dislocations happen: the dislocation count (findings R13).

The fixed `stop_buffer_floor` (QQQ 4.0 NQ) covers 99.4% of months 5–6 crossings in the quietest quartile but 88.6% in the busiest. Proposed: floor = max(fixed minimum, k × trailing volatility), fitted on months 1–4 and checked on months 5–6: QQQ max(2.79, 0.195 × vol15) NQ (months 5–6 coverage 96.5% overall, 95.9–97.3% per quartile; mean floor 2.79 quiet → 6.73 busy vs 4.0), SPY max(0.53, 0.20 × vol15) ES (96.6%, 94.7–98.0%; 0.56 → 1.34 vs 0.78); SPX max(0.52, 0.165 × vol15) ES generalizes worse (93.7%; SPX creates no NQ stops). Versus the 0 s run: QQQ minimum 2.77 → 2.79, SPY 0.52 → 0.53, SPX 0.45 → 0.52; slopes unchanged. The tail (2026-03-31 12:40–12:47, an ETF-vs-futures dislocation of ≈ 55 NQ) is covered by neither. Question for step 13: replace the fixed floor with the scaled one (re-fitted then on months 1–4), keep the fixed floor, or offer both to the stop tuning? Nothing changes until then.
