Answered (owner 2026-10-08): option (1)** — the automated Atlas match is information only and the owner's replay check is the Atlas gate condition, since (a) and (b) already gate the crossing error; kept as a regression check for later re-runs with the tolerances frozen at today's crossing p95 (`atlas_match_tol` QQQ 4.0 NQ, SPY 0.78 ES), alerting if fewer than `atlas_regression_min_share` (90%) of crossings fall within them, judged on the share pooled over all sample days (owner 2026-10-08; per-day shares information only; **baseline 2026-10-11** with `map_latency_sec` 0.59 and §5.2's relevance rule with the 20% hold applied to the listing and to each crossing (owner 2026-10-11, findings R11): **QQQ 94.6% of 519, SPY 94.6% of 574**; tolerances unchanged (the owner decides on any change: the chosen-estimator p95s moved to QQQ 4.015 NQ / SPY 0.792 ES). Before, with a plain ≥ 25% per board: QQQ 94.2% of 500, SPY 95.2% of 495 (0 s: 94.4% of 500 / 95.0% of 535; the 40 SPY crossings that drop out are all strike 651 on 2026-03-24 at 09:35: it was exactly 25.0% of King on the 09:34:59 board, in force at 0 s, and just under 25% on the 09:34:58 board, in force once the delay applies; timing, not a lost board). Written into §5.1 and §12 step 8b. Question as asked: With the approved rule, gate (a), (b) and the alignment check pass for QQQ, SPY and SPX on 22 development days (findings R11), but the Atlas tables' automated match within `atlas_match_tol` (1.0 NQ / 0.5 ES) cannot pass: it sits below the measured crossing floor (QQQ p95 4.0 NQ, SPY 0.78 ES; 37–69% of QQQ crossings and 83–94% of SPY crossings are within it, 90–100% / 93–97% within the crossing p95). Options: (1) the automated match becomes information only and the owner's replay check (same strikes, similar size) is the Atlas condition; (2) `atlas_match_tol` = the chain's crossing p95 (4.0 NQ / 0.78 ES), checked as a share (≥ 95% of crossings) rather than every crossing. Recommendation: (1), since the crossing error is already gated by (a) and (b). No change until the owner answers.
  - **Q8b-4 Gate (findings R7–R10). Answered (owner 2026-10-08), written into §5.1, §6.2, §12 step 8b, F1.6 and F7:** gate pass = (a) bias 95% interval contains 0 or is within one futures tick, (b) the chosen estimator's p95 within 10% of the best possible R, the alignment check in place of spot lag, plus the owner's Atlas check; F1.6 stays a diagnostic; stop floors per level source (`stop_buffer_floor` QQQ 4.0 NQ = the crossing p95, NDXP pending its method, chart levels 0; SPY/SPX no NQ stop floor; the `stop_buffer` grid no longer drops values, since the floor applies per stop). `step8b precision` prints the per-source floors; `step8b bias`, `estimator`, `alignment` and `recorded-day` built 2026-10-08 (results in `docs/step8b_findings.md` R11). Test A: the crossing floor is **not** just the 1-second resolution. With the futures completely still across the crossing's board interval, 81% / 80% / 68% of the floor remains (QQQ 3.3 NQ, SPY 0.62 ES, SPX 0.53 ES at p95). So, by the owner's rule (c), stop floors come from the crossing p95, not the level error. Proposed: (a) bias within its day-block interval or one futures tick (all pass); (b) the chosen estimator's crossing p95 within 10% of the best possible R (gate run 2026-10-09 with `map_latency_sec` 0.59, each crossing using the board in force by its apply time: QQQ 4.010 vs 4.040 NQ, SPY 0.775 vs 0.791 ES, SPX 0.779 vs 0.781 ES (was 0.731 on 10-08, before the latency entered the gate): all pass; bias QQQ +0.022 [−0.017, +0.058] NQ, SPY 0.000 [−0.005, +0.005] ES, SPX +0.001 [−0.008, +0.010] ES; findings R11); (c) a stop floor per level source (the zone member that sets the far edge): QQQ 4.0 NQ, NDXP ≈ 14–15 NQ pending its method, chart levels 0, SPY/SPX none (they create no NQ stops); each node band ≥ its chain's crossing p95 (all pass; NDXP ±20 vs ≈ 15 is tight); F1.6 a diagnostic; spot-lag becomes the alignment check; the owner's Atlas replay check stays.
